Same app round-trip keeps everything because the Library keeps the original file so private extensions layout and ids return from that saved copy. A different app keeps only what the shared model can express; if the target has no place for something it does not travel, and one app's private scripts are never copied into another as the safe choice. Example: converting a card with trigger scripts and regex to Agnai or legacy Backyard keeps name description and greetings but drops scripts; use Risu .charx to keep the full rule pack. Every export shows outcomes first via the editor Export honesty view listing what will and will not travel line by line before commit.
- **H2 Converting many at once** (`converting-many-at-once`)
  The Press converts a whole set in one go. Stage pieces via right-click Stage for the Press or left rail; the queue survives room switches. Pick one target, run once, every piece prints into a single zip. Each row reports printed with notes, skipped when the target has no format for that kind, or failed with error. A staged character travels as a bundle dragging linked lorebooks as riders; drop a rider from one run without unlinking. One target per run; for a second app run the press again with a different platform.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Prefer same-app round-trip to keep everything via the original file. Read drop notes keep note and dropped lines before export. Convert one piece from editor Export or a batch from the Press with the same honesty. If scripts matter pick a target that keeps them such as Risu .charx; plainer formats keep words not machinery. A lorebook whose entries have no keywords will never fire; Press flags that in red so fix keywords first.

### Edit a piece

- id: `guide/editing`
- path: `docs/guide/editing.md`
- User guide for editing pieces on the Workbench. A piece opens as its own tab filled from import or a blank template and stays a private draft until save. Characters get the deepest editor with Grid or Steps fill modes, platform lens preview of what a target keeps, and a scripts workshop; pack lorebook regex persona and preset use simpler editors with the same save mechanics. Short version: open from Library via Send to Workbench; choose Grid direct fields or Steps quiz style with Bento all-at-once or Playbill paged acts; change fields knowing platform tabs only change view not saved data; Save or ctrl+s with dirty-dot warning; close discards without confirm so save first. Editor panels cover Bunzai full card vs platform tabs with Lens count, Hide and Dim currently both hide fields, completion chips, Sealed Cargo read-only original, Variables macros, Fields/Workshop for scripts, and simpler pack lorebook binder regex persona preset forms. Save requires a name except packs fallback Untitled pack; whole draft write; close does not confirm. Tips cover dirty dot, platform tabs view-only, Sealed Cargo for lossless round-trip, toggles moving to Settings after tour, and Workshop only when scripts exist.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Open from Library by right-click Send to the Workbench or stage and send bar; each piece is its own tab and already-open pieces do not reopen. Pick Grid for every field or Steps as a quiz, with Bento all fields at once or Playbill acts. Change fields; platform tabs preview what a target keeps and only change what you see never what is saved. Save or ctrl+s; dirty tab dot means unsaved. Close never confirms so save first.
- **H2 The editor panels** (`the-editor-panels`)
  Character editor has platform tabs above fields; Bunzai is full card; selecting platforms hides fields that platform cannot carry with a Lens count of surviving fields. Hide and Dim currently both drop fields; dim half is not built yet. Header has name scale Grid Steps Bento Playbill Save Export; toggles move to Settings under Workbench after tour once. Six completion chips track Portrait Name Core Prompts Greeting Tags Lens Check. Body cards include portrait with sprite links, Sealed Cargo holding original platform file whole read-only for lossless round-trip, Variables counting macros, and Fields/Workshop switch for scripting that does not retire to Settings. Other kinds: pack is name brief sprite grid; lorebook binder one entry at a time with TOC keys timing placement; regex personas presets closer to single form; all save the same way.
- **H2 Saving, and what happens if you don't** (`saving-and-what-happens-if-you-dont`)
  Every editor requires a name before Save does anything; blank name makes Save quietly do nothing with status a name is required before saving. Packs fall back to Untitled pack instead of blocking. Save writes the whole draft not just changes so untouched fields round-trip byte-for-byte. Ctrl+s saves from anywhere. While save is in flight you can keep typing; if you outrun it status says newer changes not yet saved. In Steps last Next becomes Complete and saves. Closing a tab with X or Delete does not ask first and discards unsaved work; the dirty tab dot is the only warning.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Watch the dirty tab dot as the unsaved work signal. Platform tabs change what you see not what you save; click Bunzai to clear selection and see the whole card. Sealed Cargo is read-only on purpose for same-app lossless round-trip. After first-run tour, layout and mode toggles move to Settings under Workbench; right-click Replay tutorials to restore header toggles. A piece with no scripts never shows the Workshop toggle.

### Export a piece

- id: `guide/exporting`
- path: `docs/guide/exporting.md`
- User guide for exporting pieces from Vaudeville Studios. Two paths: character editor Export button for one piece with full honesty view of keep note and dropped lines and immediate single-file download, or staging for the Press for batches and for lorebook persona or preset alone. Short character path: open Workbench, Export, pick platform defaulting Risu or SillyTavern when available, read honesty lines, Export or Export anyway. Honesty checks whether the target carries Bunzai behavior shape for trigger scripts regex virtual script backdrop HTML and packaged modules; when it does scripts keep and Risu .charx re-packs modules; when not scripts drop and plain fields stay with pointer to Risu for rule packs. Example Agnai drops expression packs and maps lorebooks to MemoryBook with regex key warning. Press stages queue surviving room switches; linked lorebooks ride as bundles; one target; readiness not honesty showing keyword gaps red amber and filled field counts; run prints printed skipped failed then zip download. Tips: Press for non-character exports; Export anyway is soft warn; rename and flavor lock after result; fix zero-keyword lorebooks first.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  For characters open Workbench Export at top of editor; lorebook persona or preset skip to Press staging. Click Export, pick a platform from formats for this kind with Risu or SillyTavern chosen first if available. Read honesty lines that state facts or open with Note or Dropped. Click Export or Export anyway when anything needs a second look; one file downloads immediately no zip.
- **H2 Reading the honesty view** (`reading-the-honesty-view`)
  Every export checks whether the target carries Bunzai behavior shape: trigger scripts, regex, virtual script, backdrop HTML, and packaged module rows. When the target carries behavior, scripts keep; Risu .charx re-packs module scripts and lore whole while elsewhere the module blob may not survive intact. When it does not, scripts drop and plain fields stay with explicit dropped marks not silent loss; dialog points to Risu .charx if rules matter. Concrete case: export to Agnai drops expression pack and maps linked lorebook to MemoryBook with regex key survival warning; SillyTavern or Risu keep both. Media chips show portrait expression pack and named assets before platform pick.
- **H2 Staging for the Press** (`staging-for-the-press`)
  Press is the only way to export a lorebook persona or preset alone and the only multi-piece single run. Stage via right-click Stage for the Press or left rail; queue survives rooms. Staged characters auto-bring linked lorebooks as riders; drop a rider from this run without unlinking. One target for the whole queue. Press shows readiness not honesty: lorebooks check keywords immediately red if none amber if some missing; characters need target then show filled vs empty platform fields. Open in editor preserves queue spot. Run the press settles rows as printed skipped or failed; download the bundle is always a zip even for one piece once any row prints.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Export is character-only; stage other kinds for the Press even alone. Export becomes Export anyway on Note or Dropped as a soft warn. Press readiness is not honesty. Rename or pick txt/md flavor before run; fields lock after a result. Zero-keyword lorebooks read red and never fire; fix keywords first.

### Frequently asked questions

- id: `guide/faq`
- path: `docs/guide/faq.md`
- FAQ for privacy, convert survival, supported apps, and whether convert or edit can damage cards. Content is private: local loopback server 127.0.0.1, encrypted library.db in the studio folder default Documents/Bunzai Studio, no account cloud or telemetry; scripts import as text never run; deep access refused; fail-closed parse with zip-bomb caps. What converts: plain card fields and lorebook entries keys placement; private extras only on same-app round-trip; different app keeps shared model only and drops scripts when target cannot run them with Risu .charx keeping full rule pack; honesty view before export. Supported apps: SillyTavern RoleCall RisuAI Backyard/Faraday Agnai NovelAI Pygmalion Lumiverse Marinara and Chub with kind gaps; Chub is ST-shaped with extensions.chub; NovelAI lorebook only; Marinara regex and personas only; native JSON is studio storage not a convert target. Convert cannot damage cards: writes new files only, originals kept, import never overwrites, Workbench save is only overwrite path, whole-piece save preserves untouched fields, broken lorebooks heal on import, cross-app drops are named not corrupted.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Four short answers. Content is private via local server, an encrypted library file on disk, no account cloud or telemetry. Converts keep plain card and lorebook structure; private extras only on same-app round-trip. Supported apps are SillyTavern RoleCall RisuAI Backyard/Faraday Agnai NovelAI Pygmalion Lumiverse Marinara and Chub though not every app carries every kind. Convert cannot damage a card: only writes new files, original kept, export shows travel before commit.
- **H2 Is my content private?** (`is-my-content-private`)
  Yes. bunzai ui starts a local server bound to 127.0.0.1 loopback only. Pieces are stored encrypted in library.db in a studio folder default Documents/Bunzai Studio unless pointed elsewhere; this computer opens it by itself unless a password is set and nobody at Bunzai can read it. No sign-up or account; interface requests go only to that local server; no telemetry is collected. Import does not trust files: trigger scripts and regex are saved as text never run; deep access requests are refused with receipt notice. Uploads parse through fail-closed readers with caps against zip bombs so hostile files stop at we could not read this.
- **H2 What converts, and what stays behind?** (`what-converts-and-what-stays-behind`)
  Every convert goes through one shared middle model never direct app to app. Same-app round-trip is lossless via kept original including private extensions layout ids. Different app only shared part crosses: name description personality scenario greetings example messages for characters; lorebook entries keys placement. Target without a place drops trigger scripts regex virtual script and backdrop HTML on purpose never as inert baggage. Risu .charx keeps the full rule pack; plainer Agnai or legacy Backyard keep words not machinery. Export honesty view lists keep note drop before download; Press readiness shows filled target fields instead.
- **H2 Which apps are supported?** (`which-apps-are-supported`)
  Nine apps covering four kinds character lorebook persona and regex set; the support figure is ground truth not a wishlist. Chub has no own wire format and is SillyTavern CCv2/v3 with extensions.chub. NovelAI has no character-card concept only lorebook. Marinara ships regex and personas only. Bunzai native JSON is the shape every piece is kept in, stored encrypted in library.db, for every kind lossless by construction and is not a convert target in the table because pieces already are that format before converting elsewhere. Formats are drop-in folders so the list can grow without touching existing formats.
- **H2 Can a convert damage my card?** (`can-a-convert-damage-my-card`)
  No. Worst case is leaving something out and telling you before export. Original is never overwritten by convert or import; Library keeps whole file; re-import makes a second piece with -2 id not a refresh; only Workbench edit and save overwrites on purpose. Editor saves whole piece so untouched fields escrow behavior media stay byte-identical; save is explicit Save or Ctrl+S with dirty flag. Slightly broken lorebooks heal on import with receipt listing fixes. Cross-app conversion drops fields it does not corrupt them; honesty names lines before commit. Original file in Library is the safety net.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Prefer same-app round-trip for full keep via original file. Read honesty view or readiness line before export for exact keep note drop. The whole library is one encrypted file, library.db; export pieces to read them in an editor or keep copies elsewhere. If scripts matter pick Risu .charx; leaner formats keep words not machinery. Lorebook entries without keywords never fire; Press flags red before export so fix keywords first.

### Get started

- id: `guide/getting-started`
- path: `docs/guide/getting-started.md`
- Getting started covers release or source installation, the one-time first-run wizard, everyday Dock rooms, specialist catalog apps, and the Library's import and blank-character paths. Release builds are the shortest install; source use requires Bun 1.3+, clone, install, and bun run dev for the primary loopback server at 127.0.0.1:8321. Optional remote listeners stay off until enabled. The studio folder defaults to Documents/Bunzai Studio or a custom bunzai ui path. Wizard asks theme, one or more kinds to make, publish targets, and a palette or custom accent; Skip all uses defaults. Settings retains theme, the first selected kind as the initial Library deck, publish targets, and accent rather than the complete ordered make selection. Dock rooms are Workbench, Library, and Press with Settings pinned below; Docs and CSS Workshop are catalog tools. Import receipts write only checked pieces, while Start fresh creates, saves, and opens a blank character.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Download a platform build from GitHub Releases, or install Bun 1.3+, clone Bunzai, and run bun install for source use. Open the app or use bun run dev for the primary loopback address at 127.0.0.1:8321. Studio JSON defaults under Documents/Bunzai Studio or a custom path. Complete or skip the wizard, then import through the Library door or create a blank character through Start fresh.
- **H2 The first-run wizard** (`the-first-run-wizard`)
  Wizard appears once when the Studio finds no completed setup; after finish or skip it never returns on its own. Four questions: light or dark, default Dark; one or more kinds to make, where the first pick controls the first Library deck and an empty choice falls back to Characters; publish targets as a multi-select populated from known formats, with Not sure as default; and a house-palette or custom accent, default Rose. Skip all fills remaining defaults and jumps to the You're set recap. Theme, the initial Library deck, publish targets, and accent remain editable afterward; the complete ordered kind selection is not a Settings field.
- **H2 The Dock** (`the-dock`)
  Dock is the side column of everyday app tiles. Workbench edits character lorebook persona regex preset or pack; Library browses every deck's cards in rows or a grid, with a rail of live counts that shows one deck alone with its views, and the import door; Press ships only a staged queue to one selected target. Settings is pinned in the foot. The app catalog exposes included specialist tools such as Docs and CSS Workshop plus future apps such as Company; catalog-only tools are not ordinary Dock tiles. The configured home app controls normal launch, while a reload restores the active room when possible.
- **H2 Bringing in your first file** (`bringing-in-your-first-file`)
  Empty studio primary door accepts drop or click file picker; after pieces exist drop anywhere on Library for the same flow. Files are read before write. Headings are Pick what to keep when all clean or Here is what we read when some fail. Readable files show receipt with name, kind, and default-checked checkbox; unreadable files show a plain reason without a checkbox. Add more files appends the batch, Import N writes only checked pieces, and Not now writes nothing. The second empty-studio door creates and saves a blank character, then opens it in the Workbench.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Release builds are the shortest installation path; Bun 1.3+ is required only for a source checkout. Skip all fills defaults, and retained setup choices remain editable. The first selected make kind chooses the initial Library deck; later the Library remembers the last open deck. Attached character lorebooks arrive in one bundle receipt. An unreadable receipt means safe parsing failed because the file may be unsupported, malformed, or damaged; bunzai formats lists adapters. bunzai ui port path selects another studio folder.

### Import a file

- id: `guide/importing`
- path: `docs/guide/importing.md`
- User guide for importing cards lorebooks personas or regex sets into the Library. Nothing is written until confirmed; each file shows as a receipt first. Short flow: drop files on shelves or use Import to be guided to drop; read per-file receipt of name kind platform or failure reason without blocking others; uncheck unwanted then Import with live count; find piece on matching deck. Brand-new empty studio drop zone is also a clickable file browser, the only place outside active import that opens a browser; once pieces exist Import does not open a file browser. What gets kept: original file whole with unmapped private layout ids escrowed; slightly broken lorebooks heal with receipt listing fixes; scripts saved as text never run; deep access refused on receipt; personas not filed as characters; import never overwrites so re-import makes second piece with -2 id while Workbench save overwrites on purpose. Batch import reports each file alone; Add more files accumulates before one write; shelves route by kind not open deck. Tips: Import button only navigates to shelves; failed reads can retry after fix; uncheck before Import; read extras for embedded lorebook scripts deep access; update via Workbench not re-import.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Drop one or more files on Library shelves; from elsewhere click Import or right-click Import files to go to shelves and drop; once studio has pieces Import does not open a file browser. Each file returns a receipt with name kind and platform or failure reason without blocking others. Uncheck unwanted; click Import with live count; Select all resets batch; Add more files reads another round into same overlay. Saved pieces land on kind decks character lorebook persona or regex. Empty studio drop zone is also a file browser button, the only place that happens outside active import.
- **H2 What gets kept** (`what-gets-kept`)
  Import reads into the shared studio model so the receipt knows the piece before write. Original file is kept whole; private layout ids and unmapped fields stay alongside for same-app export later. Slightly broken lorebooks with missing name or wrong entry shape are healed not rejected; receipt lists fixes and entry counts. Scripts on cards are saved as text never executed; deep access requests are refused with plain receipt; personas are shelved as personas not characters. Import never overwrites: same file twice yields two pieces with -2 on the second id; update via Workbench save which overwrites on purpose.
- **H2 Importing many at once** (`importing-many-at-once`)
  Drop many files and each is read alone. Overlay is Pick what to keep when all clean or Here is what we read when some fail; failures never block successes. Add more files appends into the same overlay so you accumulate before one Import. Route is by kind: a lorebook dropped on Characters still lands on Lorebooks.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Corner Import only takes you to shelves to drop; it does not browse files. Failed read is not final: fix in the source app and retry or check unsupported format. Uncheck receipt rows to skip without blocking the rest. Read character receipt extras for embedded lorebook scripts or deep access lines. To update an existing piece edit and save on the Workbench; re-import always makes a second copy never a refresh.

### Browse the Library

- id: `guide/library`
- path: `docs/guide/library.md`
- Browse the Library covers the shelves once pieces are in the studio: every deck's cards in rows or a grid, each deck's own views, each card's own tools, and staging one piece or a batch to the Workbench. Import is covered separately. The short version: pick Carousel or Grid at the top of the rail, click a deck to show it alone and again for every deck, stage by click with shift-click ranges across decks, then send with the send bar or right-click one piece. Each non-empty deck gets a numbered section of RC cards: tall portraits for characters and personas, presets with carried samplers and prompt count, lorebooks on a book cover, regex sets with rules on and what they do; the art slider in the top bar sizes them, Carousel rows scrub, Grid allows drag-select. Cards carry their tools: on/off, Split, Copy, and drag-to-merge for lorebooks and regex sets, a slow flag, a persona's default badge and sections, and a New tile ending each row. Every section header switches its own deck among Cards, Tiles, Show, List, Shelf, Sets, and Profiles. The top search finds pieces by name, id, lorebook keywords, tags, or source across every deck. Staging never opens on tap. Tips cover tray clearing, collections, the narrow-window chip strip, view fallback, Shelf search, and import-only decks.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Pick a layout with the switch at the top of the rail: Carousel runs each deck in one row that scrolls sideways, Grid lets the cards wrap. Click a deck in the rail among Characters Lorebooks Personas Packs Presets Regex sets and Quick replies, each with a live count, to show that deck alone; click it again to see every deck. Stage by clicking a piece, or Stage for the Workbench in Show, for an accent ring and corner check; click again to drop; shift-click stages everything between two pieces, across decks while every deck shows; the tray stays full as you move around. Send with the send bar, Send it to the Workbench or Send all N, or right-click one piece to send it without staging or stage it for the Press.
- **H2 The shelves** (`the-shelves`)
  Each deck holding anything gets a numbered section of cards. In Carousel they run in one sideways row with a bar to drag or click; in Grid they wrap and a dragged box stages several. The top-bar art slider sizes every card, down to small tiles of art, name, switch, and menu. Cards say what the studio knows: tall portraits for characters and personas, presets with carried samplers and prompt count, lorebooks with entries on a book cover, regex sets with rules on and what they do, plus the import date and a ... menu. Cards carry their tools: lorebooks and regex sets switch on or off, Split, Copy, and merge when dragged onto another of their kind, and the ... menu always holds them; regex sets flag slow rules; personas show default, pronouns, and sections. A New tile ends each row. The search box finds a piece by name, id, lorebook keywords, tags, or source across every deck; All and Recent set the order.
- **H2 Each deck's views** (`each-decks-views`)
  Every rail deck shows even at count 0 so the full studio shape stays visible; the status line reports one deck's share or every deck, and an empty deck appears when picked in the rail, where its shelf offers a way to start. Characters, Lorebooks, Regex sets, Personas, Presets, and Quick replies offer New on their section and a New tile; Packs are import-only. Every section header has its own view switch and each deck remembers its choice: Cards, then Tiles Show and List on every deck; Shelf also runs everywhere with a name, key, or tag search, lorebook spines carrying on/off, Split, Copy, and drag-to-merge, and regex Sets adding rule counts, a computed description, and slow flags. An off book or set is skipped on export.
- **H2 Staging and sending** (`staging-and-sending`)
  Tapping a piece anywhere in the Library stages it and does not open it. A staged piece wears an accent ring; a piece already open on the Workbench wears a quiet on the workbench tag instead, and tapping that one only repeats the tag rather than toggling. The tray survives switching decks so you can pick a character from one chip and a lorebook from another before sending. The send bar appears only once something is staged; it carries the live count, the Send button, and Clear that empties the tray without sending. Sending opens every staged piece as its own tab and, depending on Workbench follow setting changeable in Settings, may take you there right away. You do not have to stage to move a single piece: right-click Send to the Workbench opens immediately, or Stage for the Press queues it for export on a separate queue from this tray, covered in Convert a card.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  A piece already open on the Workbench cannot be re-staged; tapping it only tells you it is already there. The staged tray persists across deck, layout, and view switches and empties only on send or Clear. Collections sit in the rail under the decks and filter the shelf like their groups-bar chip; click again to stop. On a narrow window the rail folds into deck chips above the shelf. A view the current deck does not support falls back to Cards. The Shelf view's search narrows what is on screen without changing the rail count or deck total. Personas get their own Profiles view beside the plain Shelf, showing brief, pronouns, sections, and the default. Packs have no New button; import is the only way in.

### Build a lorebook

- id: `guide/lorebooks`
- path: `docs/guide/lorebooks.md`
- Build a lorebook covers writing entries, keying them to fire, and placing them in the assembled prompt. Other apps call this world info, character book, or memory. The short version is create or open a lorebook, add a blank entry from the binder, give it Keys chips or switch Always on or By meaning, place it on the Where rail at World Character or a richer host stop, then Save or ctrl+s. Keywords and triggers fire one of three ways: Keywords default on recent chat, Always on every turn, By meaning by similarity only for Bunzai or SillyTavern; Advanced adds Only together with AND/NOT sets, regex chance, and min messages between activations; whole-word and case defaults inherit from Book rules with per-entry override; Keywords with no keys never fires and health flags it. Where it lands is after scan chance cooldown sticky delay and optional recursion budgets; World and Character are the portable floor; richer stops under Bunzai, subset on SillyTavern, World Character only on Chub Lumiverse Agnai, and Risu NovelAI force character floor; foreign positions stay marked and export to closest slot. Building the book covers TOC Always on and Entries with health pips, bulk Select, Book rules defaults, Writing for host lens that hides not deletes, and Knowledge rail Attach lorebook. Tips cover new entry defaults, Try a line, Wake map, Always on portability, and full check Fix all.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Create or open a lorebook from the Library with New lorebook on an empty shelf or open one you already have. Add an entry in the binder with + New entry in the table of contents; a blank entry starts with no keywords so it will not fire until you give it one. Open the Keys card and type a word then Enter or comma to add a chip; switch the entry to Always on or By meaning instead if you do not want keyword dependence. Place it via When and where then Where and pick a stop on the rail: World, Character, or a richer position your target host supports. Save with the Save button or press ctrl+s.
- **H2 Keywords and triggers** (`keywords-and-triggers`)
  Each entry Keys card holds primary keywords typed one at a time as chips. An entry fires one of three ways picked per entry: Keywords default when a key shows in recent chat; Always on skips matching and injects every turn; By meaning fires on similarity to chat rather than exact word and only survives writing for the Bunzai lens or SillyTavern since no other host on Writing for carries it. Switch Keys to Advanced for a second optional set under Only together with, combined against primary by AND any, AND all, NOT any, or NOT all; Advanced also exposes regex with flags, per-key chance, and a minimum messages wait between activations. Whole-word and case-sensitive matching default from Book rules and can be overridden per entry; each toggle cycles inherit, on, off. An entry set to Keywords with no keywords never fires; health check flags it; brand-new entries start that way so give at least one key or leave Keywords.
- **H2 Where it lands** (`where-it-lands`)
  A key clearing the scan window is only the first gate. Chance, cooldown, sticky, and delay still must pass, and if recursion is on one entry text can wake another entry keys under the same rules. If the book token or entry budget is full a fired entry can still be cut. What survives lands at the Where rail position, with depth and role when that position is Depth or Append which behave the same. World and Character are the portable floor every write host keeps. Richer stops Scene Depth Append Top Bottom Before example After example are only under Bunzai; SillyTavern keeps World Character Depth Before example After example; Chub Lumiverse Agnai keep World and Character only; Risu and NovelAI have no placement dial and every entry lands on the character floor. Pick a position your host does not carry and the rail keeps it marked foreign so export lands at the closest slot instead of losing it.
- **H2 Building the book** (`building-the-book`)
  The table of contents groups entries into Always on and Entries, is searchable, and reorders by dragging rows only while not searching or in Select mode. A colored pip shows health: green ready, amber worth a look, red problem, grey off. Click Select to pick several entries at once and turn them on or off, delete them, or move them into a new book. Book rules behind the header link hold book-level defaults every entry inherits: name description case sensitivity whole words recursion global scan depth token or entry budget and named categories. Writing for next to it switches which fields the binder shows to match the export host; it never deletes anything, only hides what that host cannot carry. A lorebook sits alone in the Library until you attach it to a character or persona on the Knowledge rail with Attach lorebook; reorder or detach there. A staged character brings attached lorebooks along automatically when you run the press.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  A new entry starts on World at 100 percent chance with no keywords; nothing is broken, it just will not do anything until you key it. Use Try a line on the binder right rail to paste a sample chat line and see exactly which entries fire and why using the same matcher real chat uses. Wake map on the same rail shows which entries can wake your focused entry through recursion and which it can wake in turn. Always on works on every host; By meaning does not, so check Writing for before you rely on it. Open the full check from the rail to see every finding across the book at once, with Fix all where a fix is safe to apply automatically.

### Create and manage personas

- id: `guide/personas`
- path: `docs/guide/personas.md`
- Create and manage personas covers building your side of the fiction, the identity a target app shows as user. Only name and text are required; tagline pronouns portrait colors linked lorebook and injection are optional. The short version is New persona on the Personas deck saves Untitled persona and opens Workbench; fill name tagline pronouns height age plus sections Appearance Body Personality Quirks History or flat Identity Text; Write for defaults Bunzai and can narrow the form without deleting; set Prompt Injection and Knowledge lorebook; Save; star as default and stage for Press to ship. What actually reaches the model treats Content as fallback: any filled section ignores Identity Text; Body folds into Appearance; Identity always leads; Brief never crosses; labeled colors compile as tags; Live Preview is the real compiler. Where it's injected, and what travels with it lists RoleCall or SillyTavern stops under Write for; platforms with no wire hide the card; foreign stops show dashed kept; Knowledge attaches lorebooks but export carries only the top reference as an id not entries, and staging a persona never pulls the book as a rider; default star is studio shelf only. Converting and exporting a persona has no editor Export button; stage and run Press as solo row; Agnai skips; SillyTavern backup keeps sealed siblings. Tips restate sections beat flat text, Brief shelf only, Write for hide not delete, first lorebook only, Press for files.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  On the Personas deck click New persona from the shelf tile or empty deck button; it saves immediately as Untitled persona and opens on the Workbench. Fill who you are with name tagline pronouns height and age, then write section fields Appearance Body Personality Quirks History or, if you have no use for sections, flat Identity Text. Narrow to one host if building for one: Write for in the header defaults to Bunzai the full card; pick a platform and the form drops to only what that wire carries without deleting. Set where it is injected and link a lorebook if needed in the right-hand Prompt Injection card and the Knowledge card on the left. Save with Ctrl+s or Save like any piece. Star it as default if you want the shelf to flag it, then stage for the Press when ready to ship as a file.
- **H2 What actually reaches the model** (`what-actually-reaches-the-model`)
  Content is the fallback not a second copy. Fill even one section and the compiler ignores Identity Text entirely; it only wakes once every section is empty. Body has its own card for physical specifics but folds into the Appearance tag when compiled and never gets a tag of its own. Identity always leads: pronouns tagline height and age are not a section; they compile first ahead of whatever section order you set every time. Brief never crosses: it is the library-card blurb on the Personas shelf only and never enters the injected block; voice lines belong in a section or Identity Text. Full sections leave old Identity Text inert not deleted. Switch Write for to Agnai and sections traits palette and injection leave the form; switch back and everything remains. Labeled colors compile one tag per color from the label. Live Preview always shows this exact output as the real compiler not a mock.
- **H2 Where it's injected, and what travels with it** (`where-its-injected-and-what-travels-with-it`)
  The Prompt Injection card lists stops your Write for lens carries: RoleCall four World Character Scene Depth or SillyTavern five In prompt Note top Note bottom In chat None; a platform with no injection wire such as Lumiverse or Marinara hides the card. Depth and In chat both ask for a depth number; In chat also asks role system user or assistant. A stop set under a different lens shows dashed amber disabled labeled kept not carried here so foreign choices stay visible. None of this makes Vaud chat; it decides where the target app places the block on export. Knowledge attaches reorders and detaches linked lorebooks like a character; SillyTavern RoleCall and Lumiverse each carry a single lorebook reference not a list so only the top one crosses as an id the target should already have; entries do not ride in the persona file and staging a persona for Press never pulls the book as a rider. The star Set as default persona only flags the gilt Default badge on the shelf; nothing else in Vaud reads it automatically.
- **H2 Converting and exporting a persona** (`converting-and-exporting-a-persona`)
  A persona editor carries no Export button unlike a character. To get a file out right-click Stage for the Press or click it on the Press left rail pick a target and run the press as in Convert a card. A staged persona always runs as its own solo row; it never bundles the way a character and linked lorebooks do. A target with no persona wire such as Agnai prints a skip row because Agnai has no persona format; its structured persona lives on a character card not as its own file. SillyTavern keeps every persona in a single Personas backup file not one file each. Import that backup and Vaud reads the default persona or the first if none marked as the editable piece; every other persona stays whole and sealed inside not shown as its own Library piece. Export back from that piece re-emits sealed ones untouched alongside your edit. There is currently no way to pull one sealed persona out on its own.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Sections beat Identity Text the moment any one of them has text; clear every section if you want old flat text gone not just hidden. Brief lives on the shelf only; keep the one-line blurb there and put real voice in a section or Identity Text. A Write for pick only narrows what you see; nothing is deleted; click Bunzai to see the whole card again. Only the first linked lorebook crosses on export; reorder the Knowledge card if a different book should travel. There is no Export button on a persona own editor; stage it and run it through the Press when you want a file out.

### User guide

- id: `guide/README`
- path: `docs/guide/README.md`
- User guide index for Bunzai, the forge for AI-roleplay content to convert edit and ship any format. Pages walk through using the app itself as opposed to formats or internals in the reference docs. New users start with setup and first import; other pages can be read in task order. The rooms section maps Dock rooms: Workbench for editing as home after first boot, Library for browsing six decks and import door, Press for shipping a staged queue to one target platform, CSS Workshop for token restyling, The Company not open yet as a dimmed tile, and Settings pinned in the dock foot for theme accent home app first deck publish targets and Workbench follow. The pages table links getting-started, importing, library browse, editing, personas, lorebooks, regex, converting, and exporting. Platform notes list per-platform guides for Agnai, Backyard, Chub, Lumiverse, Marinara, NovelAI, Pygmalion, RisuAI, RoleCall, and SillyTavern covering what is read kept and dropped; read Convert a card first for the general rule.

- **H2 The rooms** (`the-rooms`)
  Fast map of rooms behind one dock; getting-started covers the dock in full. Workbench is editing where every kind of piece opens as a tab and is home for every boot after the first or last location. Library is browsing: every deck's cards in rows or a grid, a rail of decks with live counts, one deck's views and tools a click away, and the import door. Press ships out only a staged queue: stage a piece, pick one target platform, run the press, download the bundle. CSS Workshop restyles the studio by repainting tokens. The Company is not open yet as a dimmed tile below the divider. Settings is pinned in the dock foot for theme, accent, home app, first deck, publish targets, and Workbench follow behavior.
- **H2 The pages** (`the-pages`)
  Index of task guide pages. Get started is the start-here page for install, four-question setup wizard, and first import. Import a file brings card lorebook persona or regex into the Library with a receipt before write. Browse the Library covers deck filter, Grid Show List, and staging for Workbench. Edit a piece covers Workbench Grid or Steps and saving draft. Personas covers identity sections or flat text and inject location. Lorebooks covers entries keywords and prompt placement. Regex covers find-replace rules order and check before save. Convert a card covers cross-format shipping and what crosses or stays. Export a piece covers editor Export or Press batch.
- **H2 Platform notes** (`platform-notes`)
  Every supported platform has its own page describing what Bunzai reads, what comes across cleanly, and what is dropped on purpose when converting to a different app. Read Convert a card first for the general rule, then platform pages for detail. Agnai covers character JSON and memory books. Backyard covers older flat JSON export and modern .byaf archive. Chub covers character cards and lorebooks. Lumiverse covers cards expression archives personas and regex. Marinara covers regex and personas. NovelAI covers lorebooks. Pygmalion covers classic flat character JSON. RisuAI covers .charx cards lorebooks and regex. RoleCall covers character cards and lorebooks. SillyTavern covers character cards and worldbooks.

### Edit a regex set

- id: `guide/regex`
- path: `docs/guide/regex.md`
- User guide for editing a regex set: a list of find-and-replace rules that run on chat text wherever you point them, one after another top to bottom. The short version: open or start a set from Library Regex sets chip Sets view or New set or drop file; add a rule via New rule with 37 starter recipes or blank; write find and replace in Guided Plain words or Pattern mode; pick Where it runs pipeline stages; Quick try alone then full test bench chain then Save. Three ways to write a rule share one find one replace and phases so mode switches never lose work: Guided asks catch become where from examples without showing pattern; Plain words Match these words or Match by example; Pattern is raw Find and flags with live In words reading. Order and how to check it: rules run listed order each seeing prior output so earlier wins on competing text; full Health check flags never-fires shadowing and one-click Fix for exact duplicates; Only run after sequences without reorder; no drag-to-reorder yet; Quick try vs full bench with Import preview for SillyTavern dialect and Escape a literal tool. Tips: start from recipe empty Replace removes match let full check find shadows use Only run after and Duplicate insert after original.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Open or start a set in the Library via the Regex sets chip then Sets view for rule count and one-line read of each set; open existing, click New set, or drop a file to start blank on the Workbench. Add a rule with New rule; pick a starter recipe among 37 each with real Before After or Start blank; either opens ready to edit. Write find and replace: pick Guided Plain words or Pattern mode; fill Find and Replace with; leave Replace empty to remove the match. Under Where it runs turn on pipeline stages this rule should act on such as user input model output display. Try then save: Quick try beside the rule runs it alone on a sample line; open the full test bench to watch the whole set as a chain; then Save or Ctrl Cmd S.
- **H2 Three ways to write a rule** (`three-ways-to-write-a-rule`)
  Every rule is one find pattern one replacement and a list of phases. The three modes view the same fields so switching never loses work. Guided never shows the pattern; it asks what to catch what it becomes and where it runs; examples build the pattern and show one honest miss that it only knows words you showed; escape link hands off to Pattern. Plain words offers Match these words with comma list and tune grid plus plain English pattern and hit chips, or Match by example with two to five phrases detecting shared structure. Pattern is raw Find and flags with live In words reading; undescribable forms say so instead of guessing. Empty Replace with removes the match. Where it runs uses phase pills from Write for; foreign phases show dashed not vanish. Fine print holds depth window re-run macros character filter first-match only draw-over and Only run after.
- **H2 Order, and how to check it** (`order-and-how-to-check-it`)
  Rules run top to bottom in list order; each sees text the prior left so earlier wins when two compete for the same text. Full Health check runs the real chain on execution-verified examples; never-fires shadowing is flagged in plain words; exact duplicates get one-click Fix off; shadowed rules stay for your reorder judgment. Only run after sequences two by fired or not without moving either. No drag-to-reorder yet; new and imported land at bottom; Duplicate inserts after original; to run earlier delete and re-add. Quick try runs one rule alone on a sample. Full test bench steps every rule with word diff capture chips or skip reason ending in What comes out. Import preview checks a rival file against your sample before import; SillyTavern dialect today others not wired. Bench Escape a literal works; Pattern-mode Find Escape button is still a placeholder.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Start from a recipe: New rule opens 37 starters in Cleanup Guardrails Formatting Style Compatibility Roleplay; every Before After is engine-computed not hand-written. Empty Replace with removes the match. Two rules can shadow without either being wrong; let the full check find it. Prefer Only run after before workarounds. No drag reorder yet; add in run order and Duplicate to insert right after the original.

### Security and privacy

- id: `guide/security`
- path: `docs/guide/security.md`
- User-facing security and privacy guide for a local-first Studio that opens untrusted cards, scripts, regex sets, PNGs, and archives. Your content stays under your control through a primary loopback listener, no accounts or telemetry, and an encrypted studio library, while the guide names three deliberate network boundaries: provider-backed Kit turns, user-opened update and version-switch information, and explicitly enabled remote access. A card cannot run itself because regex is validated and budgeted, macros and triggers are analyzed, and Lua runs only in the opt-in Risu Test Bench with worker, WebAssembly, resource, and separate-origin defenses whose packaged-host isolation is not yet proven. The sandbox origin serves only the Lua worker, regex worker, and glue.wasm. Other files stay bounded by body, archive, parsing, and path rules. Converting never copies one app's code into another because only portable canonical content crosses formats while private fields stay escrowed. About API keys describes optional BYOP Kit vault storage, hop-by-hop provider destination checks, the missing non-Windows passphrase setup flow, and Studio's ephemeral mutation token.

- **H2 Your content stays under your control** (`your-content-stays-under-your-control`)
  The Studio's primary listener binds only 127.0.0.1, and Bunzai has no hosted account, telemetry, analytics, background content upload, or inference proxy. Pieces live encrypted in one file, library.db, in the chosen studio directory, which this computer opens by itself through the system's protection: Windows, the macOS Keychain, a Linux keyring, or else a key file in the home folder that does not protect a whole stolen disk. Without a password anyone using the computer as the user can open it; a password set in Settings Security needs the twelve recovery words first and is asked at the stage door. Network activity is deliberate: Kit sends model context to the configured provider on a submitted turn, Updates reads fixed GitHub release endpoints, and remote access opens only its separately approved listener or tunnel. The private-mesh helper is downloaded only on request and checked against a built-in fingerprint before anything is written. Import, editing, conversion, and analysis make no provider request.
- **H2 A card cannot run itself** (`a-card-cannot-run-itself`)
  Downloaded content is parsed rather than automatically executed. Regex rules are budgeted find-and-replace data, never eval; structurally dangerous patterns are rejected before execution because a timeout cannot reliably interrupt one pathological native match. Macros and Risu-style triggers are analyzed without evaluation, including calc expressions handled by a small numeric parser. Lua executes only after the user opens the Risu Test Bench, never during import, preview, or background work. The bench uses real Lua in WebAssembly inside a killable worker with time and memory limits, served from a second loopback origin that exposes only the Lua worker, regex worker, and glue.wasm, with no session token or API. HTTP-layer tests prove that boundary, but packaged Windows-host isolation remains unmeasured, so the guide claims opt-in limitation rather than complete isolation.
- **H2 Other files stay bounded** (`other-files-stay-bounded`)
  Untrusted files are constrained before they can consume unbounded memory, corrupt state, or escape storage. Uploads and request bodies have hard size ceilings. ZIP and PNG readers enforce bounded extraction so compressed archive bombs stop at configured limits rather than expanding indefinitely. Parsers fail closed on malformed content instead of producing partially trusted entities. Kit's gated apply saves and re-reads its target to verify intended canonical content, while Studio editors send expected revisions so stale writes are rejected. Piece identifiers are validated and resolved strictly beneath the selected studio directory; crafted traversal names fail before filesystem access can escape that root.
- **H2 Converting never copies one app's code into another** (`converting-never-copies-one-apps-code-into-another`)
  Cross-format conversion carries only the portable canonical content understood by both sides. Source-specific extension blocks, layout data, triggers, and executable-looking payloads remain in the saved piece's escrow rather than being copied into another platform's output. That escrow allows same-format re-export to preserve source-owned information while preventing a conversion from smuggling one application's private code into a different application's file. Bunzai reports content that cannot cross instead of silently pretending it was portable. The rule is structural: the shared readable core converts, while platform-private material remains attached to its source format.
- **H2 About API keys** (`about-api-keys`)
  Provider-backed Kit is optional and uses bring-your-own-provider credentials; import, conversion, inspection, validation, editing, and deterministic checks continue without a key. Windows uses the operating-system credential backend. A portable passphrase-encrypted backend exists, but the current settings flow does not collect or supply the passphrase, so first-run setup and unlock remain incomplete on non-Windows systems. Credentials authenticate the configured provider and stay out of messages, tool observations, canonical pieces, exports, transcripts, and privacy accounting. Provider requests validate the configured host before every hop and refuse cross-host redirects, HTTPS downgrades, and chains beyond five redirects. Separately, Studio creates a fresh local session token at launch to authorize mutations and does not persist it.
- **H2 What we do not claim** (`what-we-do-not-claim`)
  The guide avoids reassuring but unsupported claims. Current desktop binaries are unsigned, so SmartScreen or Gatekeeper warnings are expected; users should verify the SHA-256 checksums shipped with releases rather than treating the warning alone as evidence of tampering. The separate Test Bench origin has automated HTTP-layer proof but has not been measured inside the packaged desktop host. Anyone using the computer as the user can open the library, and without a system keyring a stolen disk can too. Kit's portable vault still lacks a settings-driven passphrase setup and unlock flow. Security reports should go privately to chibiconeja@gmail.com, also listed in SECURITY.md.

### Troubleshoot common problems

- id: `guide/troubleshooting`
- path: `docs/guide/troubleshooting.md`
- Troubleshoot common problems using plain messages Bunzai already shows: failed Studio boot prints address and SSH command; failed import names a reason; export lists Note or Dropped per field; Press rows settle printed skipped or failed with why. The short version: read the message you have; ask the file with bunzai inspect and validate; check Workbench lens or Export honesty before re-export; check FORMAT-SUPPORT matrix; if unexplained run bunzai formats for known support. The studio says forbidden over SSH: bind is loopback 127.0.0.1:8321 so use printed ssh -L command leave tunnel open open exact localhost not server IP; second -L for sandbox test benches; recovery buttons Open Studio and Remote Access setup; Settings Remote access for everyday devices. A card would not import: receipt headings Pick what to keep or Here is what we read; unrecognized format not damage; import never overwrites -2; lorebook heal; scripts inert; deep access refused; Import no browser after pieces exist. A convert dropped something: shared canonical only; same app means exact format id; persona one lorebook ref; placement foreign closest; Press bundle lorebook once. An export looks wrong: lens honesty agree; Export anyway on Note Dropped; Press skipped vs failed; -2 name collision; editor one file Press zip; casting fields platform slots. Tips: message first CLI inspect exact format round-trip shared coverage unknown format issue sample.

- **H2 The short version** (`the-short-version`)
  Read the message you already have: a Studio boot error receipt line Note or Dropped line or Press row note states a fact and a next step. Ask the file what it is from a project terminal: bunzai inspect names kind and format or says it could not be read; bunzai validate exits 0 if the file opens at all. Check the lens or honesty view before you export again: pick target platform on Workbench and read dimmed fields or open Export Note and Dropped lines; both read the same coverage claims so they never disagree. Check the format matrix if one field is the question: docs FORMAT-SUPPORT.md is generated from live adapters what each platform reads and writes kind by kind. If none of that explains it the file may be a format Bunzai does not know yet not a broken one; bunzai formats lists everything currently supported.
- **H2 The Studio says "forbidden" over SSH** (`the-studio-says-forbidden-over-ssh`)
  Bunzai listens at 127.0.0.1:8321 by default deliberately local. Container or headless use needs you to carry loopback to your browser machine. Start Bunzai on the server; copy the printed full ssh -L command; replace user at server with your login; leave the tunnel open; open the exact localhost URL not server or container IP. Two -L entries appear when the Lua regex sandbox is up: Studio plus random sandbox port; copy both for test benches. If shell loads but Studio could not start and refused the address use Open Studio Open Remote Access setup and Copy SSH command recovery pointed at forwarded localhost. Restart reprints the current two-port command. This tunnel is owner loopback access; Settings Remote access is for approved phones and everyday devices. Test benches stay host-local unless the second port is forwarded too.
- **H2 A card would not import** (`a-card-would-not-import`)
  Every file is read before write under Pick what to keep when clean or Here is what we read when some fail; every row has checkbox and reason. Could not read means unrecognized format not damage for known shapes png json charx lorebook byaf; use bunzai inspect before calling it broken. Import never overwrites: second drop gets -2 id so re-drop cannot clobber edits; update via Workbench save instead. Broken lorebooks heal with receipt fix lines and entry counts. Scripts stay text inert until the test stage; Workshop toggle shows them when present. Deep access is refused on the receipt; rest still imports. After pieces exist Import only routes to shelves to drop; empty studio alone opens a file browser.
- **H2 A convert dropped something** (`a-convert-dropped-something`)
  Convert reads into one shared canonical model then writes the target shape; most drop bugs are that design working. Different-app loss of scripts regex modules is expected: only shared model crosses; private blocks never copy; full rule packs need a keeper like Risu charx. Same-app still different means you did not use the exact format id both ways; RoleCall in then plain SillyTavern JSON is cross-format; export through the import format for byte-honest copy. Persona wires carry one lorebook ref so only the first crosses; reorder Knowledge or stage the book alone. Rich placement exists only under Bunzai and some platforms; Risu NovelAI land every entry on the character floor with foreign closest slot. Staged characters bundle linked lorebooks once; Press drop from this run omits without unlink; ride again restores.
- **H2 An export looks wrong** (`an-export-looks-wrong`)
  Export and Press share Workbench lens coverage so they never disagree on carry. Dimmed or missing fields are the lens hiding unsupported slots; Bunzai tab restores the full card nothing deleted. Export becomes Export anyway when honesty shows Note or Dropped; Note is partial Dropped does not travel not a hard stop. Press skipped names reasons like no persona format before the run not a failure. Failed is a real row error with the actual message while the batch continues. Colliding names mint -2; rename or pick txt md before run then fields lock. Editor Export always one file; Press always a zip even for one piece. Casting full name title pronouns drop when the target has no slot; check the lens not the field.
- **H2 Tips** (`tips`)
  Read the message first; it was already the answer. Use bunzai inspect validate formats before clicking around. Same-app lossless needs the exact import format id out not a lookalike. Lens and honesty share one coverage table so dimmed means Dropped. Unsupported format is not a broken file; file an issue with a sample.

## guide/platforms

### Agnai

- id: `guide/platforms/agnai`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/agnai.md`
- Agnai also known as Agnaistic stores characters in its own JSON shape with a structured persona map, not SillyTavern shape. What Bunzai reads: character .json only no png option, and memory books as standalone or embedded .json; drop on Library for a plain-words receipt recognized by structured persona plus greeting shape. What comes across cleanly keeps the original whole and lifts name description version culture personality structured or plain scenario appearance greetings examples system post-history prefill one depth injection creator tags voice avatar or sprite recipe image-gen affixes structured response schema as data only and embedded memory book. What changes when you convert to another app: same-app round-trip keeps untouched fields byte for byte except persona always rebuilt from the editor so multi-block plain personality collapses to first; cross-app keeps shared character and drops sampler provider account layout private settings; no regex feature either way. Editing on the Workbench has dedicated controls for structured persona voice sprite image-gen and schema. Exporting back out stages through the Press as .json with embedded book riding; txt md available. Common questions cover round-trip exception no png structured persona location sprite voice and memory book pull-edit-put-back.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads the two file types Agnai gives you. Characters as a .json file; Agnai does not embed a card inside a picture the way SillyTavern does so there is no .png option to bring in. Memory books Agnai name for lorebook or worldbook as a .json file either standalone or embedded inside a character. Drag the file onto the Library and drop anywhere; Bunzai shows a plain-words receipt of what it read and kept. It recognizes an Agnai character by its own shape a structured persona plus a greeting not by the file name. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original card then lifts editable fields: name description version label; culture or language for default voice; personality as plain text or Agnai structured persona map via W++ boostyle square-bracket or attribute list; scenario and separate appearance for image generation; first message alternate greetings example messages; system prompt post-history instructions assistant prefill; one depth injection with more editable on Workbench but only first travels to Agnai; creator tags voice selection; avatar image or layered sprite recipe; image-generation prefix suffix negative template; structured response schema as data only never run; embedded memory book entries keywords placement. Card picture when present is the piece portrait. Listed fields ready to edit; the rest held safely in the background.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app round-trip keeps everything you did not touch byte for byte because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrites changed fields. One exception: Agnai persona is always rebuilt fresh from the editor so multi-block plain-text personality collapses to the first block even on straight Agnai round trip. Converting to another app drops Agnai-only extras on purpose. Core character name description personality scenario appearance greetings examples system post-history prefill creator tags come across as far as the target holds. Structured persona voice sprite image-gen affixes schema memory book and depth injection come across only if the target has matching slots. Sampler provider settings inside image config account id and saved layout stay on same-app and drop cross-app. Another app means SillyTavern RoleCall Risu Backyard Pygmalion and similar. Agnai has no regex-key feature so nothing carries either way.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece from the Library to the Workbench and it opens as a tab. Edit name description personality scenario first message and example messages the same as any character. Agnai extras get dedicated controls too: structured persona map voice sprite recipe image-generation affixes and response schema each have an editor so you are never stuck hand-editing raw JSON. When you save Bunzai writes the whole card back so parts you did not touch stay exactly as they were.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage a ready piece for the Press via right-click Stage for the Press or the Press rail. The Press works only staged pieces and each run exports to one target format you pick. Bunzai writes Agnai cards as .json the same shape Agnai exports. An embedded memory book rides along with the character automatically. You can also ask for plain .txt or .md when you just want to read the text. Choosing a different app as target applies the convert drop story above.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the card come back exactly as it was? Yes on export to Agnai except multi-entry plain-text persona collapses to first because persona is rebuilt from the editor. Agnai does not give a .png card; native export is JSON only so Bunzai never offers a picture for it. Structured persona still there: W++ boostyle or attribute map goes to structured persona editor while plain text lands in ordinary personality. Sprite voice and image-generation settings keep full on same-app round-trip and are editable; cross-app only if matching slots exist. Agnai has no regex scripts like SillyTavern so nothing carries either way. Embedded memory book is pulled for solo edit and put back on export to apps that support memory books or worldbooks.

### Backyard

- id: `guide/platforms/backyard`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/backyard.md`
- Backyard.ai formerly Faraday ships two character shapes and Bunzai reads and writes both. What Bunzai reads: older flat JSON with aiName aiPersona customDialogue keys, and modern .byaf zip of character scenarios and images; two separate adapters tell them apart by content not rename. What comes across cleanly keeps the original and lifts display name nickname description personality scenario system first message examples creator tags from flat JSON; from .byaf also alternate greetings portrait gallery background content rating as explicit or all-ages source dates; flat JSON rewrites single-brace placeholders to double braces while .byaf text is untouched. What changes when you convert to another app treats same-shape round-trip as full keep but moving between Backyard shapes counts as conversion like a different app so personality does not travel flat-to-byaf and byaf-only media rating dates drop on legacy; lore items not yet lifted to editable lorebook. Editing on the Workbench covers core fields with whole-card save. Exporting back out offers Backyard .byaf and Backyard legacy JSON as two Press targets. Common questions cover same-shape exact return, picture only on byaf, placeholder rewrite rules, and lore items stay escrowed.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads both shapes Backyard has shipped. The older flat JSON export from Faraday-era versions is a plain .json with keys like aiName aiPersona and customDialogue. The modern .byaf archive is a zip that bundles the character one or more scenarios and any images together. These are not two names for the same thing; each has its own vocabulary and two separate adapters. Drop either on the Library; Bunzai looks inside a .byaf by zip signature and manifest shape and flat JSON by Backyard-only keys so renaming does not confuse it. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original then lifts editable parts. From older flat JSON: display name and separate nickname when short char name differs; description and personality as two fields; scenario system prompt first message example messages creator tags and optional version tag. From a .byaf archive: display name nickname single description no separate personality; scenario from primary narrative; system first message alternate greetings examples; avatar-labeled or first image as portrait other images as gallery; scenario background; creator source URL created and updated dates; content rating as explicit or all-ages only because byaf stores a plain NSFW switch so mature never comes from Backyard import. Across both shapes Backyard placeholders are single braces; flat JSON rewrites to double braces on the way in while .byaf text comes across exactly as written without rewrite.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-shape round-trip keeps everything: flat JSON to flat JSON or .byaf to .byaf because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrites changed fields. Moving between the two Backyard shapes counts as conversion not same-app: only listed shared fields cross. Personality is the clearest case; flat JSON keeps it but .byaf has one persona field so personality does not travel on export to byaf. Alternate greetings portrait gallery background content rating source dates keep on byaf round-trip and drop on flat JSON which has none of them. Lore items sampler generation settings prompt template grammar chat transcript and other Backyard extras without a canonical slot stay on same-shape and drop elsewhere. Lore items stay on the twin but are not yet lifted into an editable lorebook so they do not cross into another app lore fields. Another app includes SillyTavern RoleCall Agnai Risu and the other Backyard shape.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece from the Library to the Workbench and it opens as a tab. Edit name nickname description personality scenario first message and any alternate greetings and example messages. When you save Bunzai writes the whole card back so parts you did not touch stay exactly as they were.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage a ready piece for the Press via right-click or the Press rail. Each run exports staged pieces to one target you pick. Backyard shows as two targets: Backyard for modern .byaf and Backyard legacy for flat JSON. Bunzai writes .byaf as a zip and legacy as .json. Pick the target that matches where the card is going; the other applies the drop story like a different app. Plain .txt or .md copies are available for reading text only.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the card come back exactly as it was? Yes only when exported to the exact import shape; switching shapes follows the same crossing rule as a different app. Imported .byaf returns as .byaf only if you pick Backyard; Backyard legacy writes flat JSON and drops alternate greetings images background and content rating. Pictures ride on .byaf round-trip including gallery; flat JSON has no image archive. Flat JSON single-brace placeholders convert to double braces on read and only re-convert if you edited the field; .byaf text is never touched that way. Archive lore items stay attached on .byaf round-trip but are not yet lifted into an editable lorebook so they will not cross into another app lore fields.

### Chub

- id: `guide/platforms/chub`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/chub.md`
- This guide covers Chub character cards and lorebooks, which Bunzai reads through their SillyTavern-shaped character-card fields and the `extensions.chub` block. Cards arrive as PNG or JSON. Lorebooks may be embedded or downloaded as a standalone `character_book` entry array. Bunzai retains parsed source data in escrow while exposing supported character fields and Chub extras for editing. A same-format card export preserves original field values and applies edits, but JSON formatting or the output container may change. Conversion to another app omits Chub-specific fields that have no target equivalent. Standalone Chub lorebooks are edited as ordinary lorebook entities and exported through a supported lorebook format; their download wrapper is not preserved as a Chub-specific output. The Workbench shows Chub-tagged fields, previews custom CSS in a sealed Chub-style surface, and narrows lorebook editing to Chub's supported field shape. The Press writes character cards as CCv3 JSON with the Chub extension when present; Chub is not a lorebook output target. Common questions cover card output, portrait retention, preserved Chub fields, and standalone lorebook exports.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads Chub character cards as .png with text inside the image or plain .json. It does not treat Chub as its own wire format: it recognizes the SillyTavern shape underneath and keeps the extensions.chub block that rides along. Lorebooks arrive as .json embedded in a card or standalone from Chub lorebook shelf; standalone downloads are a bare character_book entry array not SillyTavern keyed worldbook map, and Bunzai recognizes that shape too. Drop on the Library for a plain-words receipt calling origin Chub CharacterHub when extras give it away; recognition is by inside content not filename. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Bunzai retains parsed source card data in escrow and exposes supported fields for editing: character identity and description, greetings, examples, prompts, creator details, tags, embedded worldbook, and expression images when present. Chub extras include chat background, custom CSS, bound preset, and related-lorebook links; the Workbench shows supported extras as Chub-tagged cards. Chub id, full path, and Stages references remain in source data even though they are not editable fields. The card image is retained as the portrait. A standalone lorebook exposes entries, keywords, and placement for editing. Retaining parsed fields does not mean the exported file has identical bytes or formatting.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  A same-format card export preserves original field values and applies edits; JSON formatting or the output container may change. Conversion to another app omits Chub-specific fields without a target equivalent, including chat background, custom CSS, bound preset, related-lorebook links, Chub id, path, and Stages references. Shared character fields, embedded worldbooks, and expression images cross over only as far as the target supports them. A Chub card uses the SillyTavern-shaped card structure, so it can be exported as the corresponding SillyTavern card. A standalone lorebook has no Chub output target: Bunzai writes it using a supported lorebook format, such as a SillyTavern worldbook, and does not preserve the original download wrapper. Entries, keywords, and placement remain represented in the lorebook data.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece to the Workbench as a tab and edit core character fields the same as any card; save writes the whole card so untouched parts stay. Cards with a Chub bag show Chub-tagged cards for chat background bound preset and related-lorebook links. Custom CSS opens in the CSS Workshop sealed preview mocked to Chub chat-bubble classes never applied to Bunzai interface. Open a lorebook and Write for Chub narrows to character_book floor emphasizing priority secondary triggers and before-character after-character placement only; hiding never deletes fields.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press via right-click or rail; each run exports staged pieces to one target. Chub is a character target writing the same file SillyTavern would get: .json CCv3 with extensions.chub bag if present. Embedded worldbook rides automatically. Plain .txt or .md available for text. Chub is not a lorebook target; export standalone lorebooks as SillyTavern or another lorebook-capable app instead. Other app targets apply the drop story.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Does the card return exactly as it was? A same-format export preserves its original field values with edits applied, but formatting or container may differ. Imported PNG cards export as JSON, which Chub and SillyTavern can read. Bunzai retains the art as the portrait and stores source data in escrow. Chub background and custom CSS remain Chub fields and are not copied into unrelated formats. An embedded worldbook can be edited separately and re-embedded for a target that supports it. Standalone Chub lorebooks export through a supported lorebook format, such as a SillyTavern worldbook; the original download wrapper is not preserved.

### Lumiverse

- id: `guide/platforms/lumiverse`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/lumiverse.md`
- Lumiverse cards ride SillyTavern-shaped JSON or PNG plus Lumiverse-only expression images alternate faces text persona and regex export. What Bunzai reads: character .png or .json, .charx archive with expressions alternate faces and baked regex, persona .json dump, and standalone regex .json; detect by Lumiverse-only keys not filename. What comes across cleanly keeps the original and lifts core fields talkativeness linked world embedded worldbook portrait resolved expression images and alternate text or faces as variants; unresolved image refs stay plain text never guessed. What changes when you convert to another app: same-app JSON keeps fields unchanged and .charx may re-path files inside the archive; cross-app drops variants regex TTS LoRA databank ids layout while core and worldbook cross as far as target holds. Editing on the Workbench uses portrait variant strip Manage Sprites and Lumiverse-tagged extras with world-book ids read-only for later lore editor work. Exporting back out keeps container JSON or charx by default and refuses JSON when archive files would strand. Common questions cover round-trip paths expressions variants scripts and persona dump.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads four Lumiverse outputs. Character cards as .png or .json like SillyTavern. A Lumiverse archive as .charx packing the card expression images alternate faces and any regex scripts as separate files inside rather than inline. A persona as a .json dump of one account persona with name title description and pronouns. A set of regex scripts as standalone .json separate from any card. Drop on the Library for a plain-words receipt. It tells Lumiverse from plain SillyTavern by Lumiverse-only keys inside data not by filename so renaming does not confuse. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original card and for an archive every file inside then lifts name description personality scenario greetings examples system post-history creator notes tags talkativeness linked world name embedded worldbook portrait art and expression images the archive carried plus alternate description personality scenario or face as variants. An expression image becomes editable art only when bytes are found embedded or in the same archive; a missing file reference stays plain text and Bunzai never guesses. Card picture is the piece portrait. Listed fields ready to edit; the rest held safely in the background.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app JSON round-trip returns every field unchanged because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrote changed fields. A .charx round-trip keeps every image alternate face and script though paths inside the archive may be regenerated; Lumiverse reads its manifest so the card still opens. Plain JSON has no layout to shift so it comes back closer to original. Converting to another app drops Lumiverse private extras: alternate faces and text variants for now since no other write target reads variants yet; regex baked into archive; TTS voice LoRA hint attached world-book and databank ids alternate character name internal ids layout. Core character worldbook and resolved images come across as far as the target holds. Another app means SillyTavern Agnai Backyard RoleCall Risu. The drop is deliberate never blind-copy private scripts or settings.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece to the Workbench as a tab and edit core character fields like any card.
