The portrait card carries a variant strip with Base plus one tile per alternate version; click a tile to edit that variant text or art or add with plus. Manage Sprites opens the pack editor when expression images exist. Lumiverse-tagged fields sit alongside: alternate character name image-gen LoRA TTS voice attached world-book and databank ids read-only full lore editor is later work and a catch-all. Save writes the whole card so untouched parts stay.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press; each run exports staged pieces to one target. Export to Lumiverse keeps the container the card came in: .json stays .json and archive returns as .charx with edited images faces and scripts packed back. You can ask for the other container. Asking .json after an archive import refuses rather than silently drop stranded files; that check can still trip after removing fields that pointed at them. Plain .txt or .md is available for JSON output not for .charx. Other app targets apply the drop story.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the card come back exactly as it was? Yes on export to Lumiverse unedited: plain JSON fields unchanged; archive keeps image and script bytes though internal paths may regenerate which Lumiverse does not mind. Imported archive returns .charx by default; ask for .json and Bunzai refuses rather than drop archive-only files. Picture and resolved expression images come across as portrait and editable art. Alternate faces and text fold into variants on the portrait card and fold back on Lumiverse export. Regex baked into archive stay with the card and do not copy cross-app; standalone regex is its own piece. Persona .json dumps import name title description pronouns with everything else on the twin for round-trip.

### Marinara

- id: `guide/platforms/marinara`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/marinara.md`
- Marinara-Engine has no import export screen of its own; Bunzai takes server dumps of regex scripts and personas. Character cards are SillyTavern-shaped and go through the SillyTavern page with a Marinara tab not this page. What Bunzai reads: bare .json regex arrays matching GET regex-scripts and persona objects with sections theming and stats; detect by Marinara-only marks like targetCharacterIds order ai_output or nameColor personaStats. What comes across cleanly lifts full regex row fields with timestamps escrowed and persona name description sections comment avatar while theming stat bars crop tags scenario ride sealed. What changes when you convert to another app keeps same-app everything; regex crosses to apps with regex slots ST RoleCall Risu Lumiverse and skips Agnai Backyard; personas to ST RoleCall Lumiverse only with theming stats dropped. Editing on the Workbench narrows Writing for Marinara and flags Bunzai-only regex controls. Exporting back out writes .json only. Common questions cover exact return character path sealed theming Writing for hide and character targets.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads two bare .json file types from Marinara-Engine. Regex scripts as an array one row per script matching GET /regex-scripts. Personas as an object with sections comment avatar theming and stat bars. A Marinara character card is different: SillyTavern-shaped so it comes and goes through the SillyTavern page with Marinara-only fields like stat bars and tracker colors under a dedicated Marinara tab not here. Recognition looks inside not name: regex dumps need targetCharacterIds order or placement values like ai_output or user_input that SillyTavern regex never has; personas need id name description personality plus at least one theming or stat key like nameColor or personaStats. Drop on the Library. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. For a regex script Bunzai lifts pattern replacement flags trim strings where it runs input output or both prompt-only depth window character limits on/off and order; row created and updated timestamps ride in the background only. For a persona it lifts name description first-person voice comment as short blurb appearance personality backstory sections and avatar. Marinara persona extras per-persona chat theming name dialogue box colors stat bars avatar crop tags and scenario have no shared home so they ride sealed in the background and return exactly on export back to Marinara rather than being dropped.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app round-trip keeps everything for both kinds because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrites changed fields. Regex scripts cross as far as the target regex engine holds; timestamps drop cross-app. A regex only lands if the target has a regex slot: SillyTavern RoleCall Risu Lumiverse do; Agnai or Backyard do not so Press skips the piece for that run. Personas keep name description sections comment avatar on cross-app as far as the target holds; chat theming stat bars avatar crop tags scenario and Marinara persona id drop. Fewer apps take personas: only SillyTavern RoleCall Lumiverse; converting to Risu Agnai Backyard skips the piece. The drop is deliberate never blind-copy theming stats or scripts.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  A regex set opens as one rule per page in a table of contents with pattern replacement flags where it fires and fine print for the rest. Writing for Marinara narrows fine print to depth window and character targets and hides controls with no place like macro substitution and run-on-edit. Three Bunzai-only controls show under every lens flagged not portable: first match only overlay and conditional chaining. A persona opens with name portrait section tiles appearance personality History for backstory full first-person text and live injection preview. Writing for Marinara narrows to name sections avatar and hides color palette and injection-position picker because Marinara wire has no place for them; sealed theming and stats only reappear on export back to Marinara. Save writes the whole set or persona so untouched parts stay.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press via right-click or rail; each run exports staged pieces to one target. Bunzai writes both Marinara regex sets and personas as .json the only shape Marinara understands. Plain .txt or .md copies are available for reading text. Choosing a different app applies the drop story: regex to apps with regex slots personas to apps with persona slots each dropping what the target cannot hold.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will regex or persona come back exactly as it was? Yes on export to Marinara-Engine same app in same app out. Marinara character cards go through the SillyTavern page not this one including stat bars and tracker colors under a Marinara tab. Persona colors and stat bars still ride sealed in the background and return untouched on export to Marinara. Writing for strip narrows the editor to what the platform wire carries so you never fill fields thrown away on export; switch to Bunzai lens for every field. Character targets on regex are kept; empty list means applies to everyone same as the wire.

### NovelAI

- id: `guide/platforms/novelai`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/novelai.md`
- NovelAI has no character-card concept; the only portable file is a lorebook .lorebook plain JSON underneath. What Bunzai reads: .lorebook or same-shaped .json by version and entries list not filename; imports always land as standalone lorebook pieces never characters. What comes across cleanly keeps the original and lifts entry title passage keywords including inline /regex/ enabled always-on scan distance placement category book categories key-relative and non-story toggles context wrap budget settings and phrase bias groups; book settings folders ride in background. What changes when you convert to another app keeps same-app everything; cross-app keeps shared entry core and categories only where RoleCall Risu have them while NovelAI-only dials drop; scan distance number can cross into message units unchanged not a bug. Editing on the Workbench uses the lorebook binder and Writing for NovelAI surfaces private dials. Exporting back out writes .lorebook; reverse convert uses blank-entry defaults. Common questions cover exact return no character cards regex keywords unnamed books and private dials stay on NovelAI export.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads NovelAI native lorebook export as a .lorebook file or plain .json with the same shape. It recognizes by looking inside at version number and entries list not by file name. Because NovelAI has no character card dropping one in never produces a character; it lands in the Library as a standalone lorebook piece. Drag onto the Library for a plain-words receipt of what it read and kept. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original lorebook then lifts each entry title and passage; keywords including inline /regex/ patterns; enabled and always-on; scan distance and placement order; category assignment and the book list of categories; two NovelAI-only activation toggles key-relative match and non-story fire; context wrap and budget settings framing text token budget trim behavior; phrase bias groups nudging words while active. Book-level settings and folder details ride safely in the background with the original file so nothing is thrown away even without a field.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app round-trip keeps everything because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrites entries you changed. Converting to another app drops NovelAI private dials: key-relative and non-story toggles context wrap budget phrase bias book-level settings folders internal ids. Core entry title passage keywords enabled always-on scan distance placement order come across as far as the target holds. Category assignment comes across only if the target has categories or folders RoleCall Risu; dropped elsewhere. Another app means SillyTavern RoleCall Risu Agnai; plain apps without lorebook format like Backyard or Pygmalion are never offered as lorebook targets. Honest unit note: NovelAI scan distance is characters of story text; an app that counts messages reads the same number unchanged so 1000 characters can land as 1000 messages after convert, a unit mismatch not a bug.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a lorebook to the Workbench and it opens on its binder with a table of contents and the focused entry on the page. Edit title passage and keywords there. Save writes back only what changed so untouched entries stay exact. Pick Writing for NovelAI and NovelAI dials rise: activation toggles context wrap budget and phrase bias; fields other apps use fold away without deleting. Switch Writing for back to Bunzai and everything is still there.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press via right-click or rail. Since NovelAI has no character card a lorebook staged alone prints by itself; a lorebook already linked to a character rides with that character. Bunzai writes .lorebook. Choosing a different app applies the drop story. Reverse convert into NovelAI from another app builds each entry on NovelAI blank-entry defaults the same starting point NovelAI uses for a new entry so the file loads clean.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the lorebook come back exactly as it was? Yes on export to NovelAI with only touched entries rewritten. NovelAI has no character cards Bunzai can read; every import is a standalone lorebook. Inline /regex/ keywords are kept as regex not flattened. An imported book may show no name because NovelAI export carries no book-level name; name it in Library or Workbench and it sticks. Phrase bias and context wrap do not survive another app; they stay untouched on export back to NovelAI.

### Pygmalion

- id: `guide/platforms/pygmalion`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/pygmalion.md`
- Pygmalion is the classic five-field flat character format before Character Card V2: name persona scenario greeting example dialogue as .json or text inside .png. What Bunzai reads: either container by true Pygmalion shape so SillyTavern PNG cards are not mistaken. What comes across cleanly keeps the original and lifts the five fields with persona also shown as description; PNG portrait kept; tool bookkeeping version timestamp tool name rides in background. What changes when you convert to another app keeps same-app everything including folding edits into a fresh PNG when imported as PNG; cross-app has little private extras to drop beyond tool stamps. Editing on the Workbench: description mirrors persona but edit personality to change how the character reads on export. Exporting back out writes .json or folds into PNG only from a Pygmalion PNG you gave it; fold failure falls back to JSON. Common questions cover exact return PNG rules recognition thresholds and bookkeeping drop.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads Pygmalion classic cards in either container: a .json five-field document alone or a .png with the same text tucked inside the picture like SillyTavern cards. Drop on the Library for a plain-words receipt. Recognition looks inside not filename. A picture can hide several card shapes so Bunzai checks the tucked text is genuinely Pygmalion before claiming the file and never mistakes a real SillyTavern card for Pygmalion just because both hide text the same way. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original then lifts name; persona the one blob of who they are and how they act also shown as description so description-only views still have text; scenario; greeting opening line; example dialogue. If the card arrived as .png the picture is the piece portrait. Some exporters stamp version timestamp tool name bookkeeping with no field so it rides untouched in the background. After import the five fields are ready to edit and the rest is held safely.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app round-trip keeps everything because Bunzai kept the original and only rewrote changed fields. If the card came as .png exporting back to Pygmalion folds possibly edited fields into a fresh copy of that same picture. Converting to another app drops little: classic wire never carried extensions scripts tags or system prompt so there is no large private pile. Core five fields come across as far as the target holds. Tool bookkeeping drops cross-app. Picture comes across if the target has a slot. Another app means SillyTavern Agnai Backyard RoleCall Risu. The drop is deliberate though for Pygmalion there is rarely much private to drop.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece to the Workbench as a tab and edit name personality scenario first message and example messages. Description mirrors persona for display but on a Pygmalion piece is read-only in effect: editing description alone without personality will not reach the card on export so edit personality when you mean to change how the character reads. Save writes the whole card so tool bookkeeping and untouched parts stay.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press; each run exports staged pieces to one target. Bunzai writes Pygmalion as .json five fields plus whatever rode along. If the piece history includes an imported .png exporting to Pygmalion folds current fields into a fresh picture; if fold fails it falls back to plain .json rather than a broken file. Converting a piece that started elsewhere to Pygmalion writes plain .json with no picture; Bunzai only produces Pygmalion .png from a Pygmalion .png you actually gave it. Other app targets apply the drop story.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the card come back exactly as it was? Yes on export to Pygmalion. Imported .png returns as .png only when fold into a fresh picture succeeds; otherwise plain .json not a broken file; other targets write JSON fields without picture. Portrait is kept on import and returns on Pygmalion export; cross-app picture only if target has a slot; converting a card that never had a picture never invents one. Name and greeting alone without persona or scenario is not enough: Bunzai needs at least two of persona greeting and scenario or name plus persona specifically. Tool bookkeeping rides on round-trip and drops cross-app.

### Platforms

- id: `guide/platforms/README`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/README.md`
- The Platforms index maps ten source-platform guides to the Bunzai adapter kinds covered on each page. It explains what every guide covers: accepted file shapes, fields represented in Bunzai, same-format field preservation, changes during cross-format conversion, Workbench editing, and common questions. The table lists Agnai characters and lorebooks; Backyard characters; Chub characters and lorebooks; Lumiverse characters, personas, and regex sets; Marinara personas and regex sets; NovelAI lorebooks; Pygmalion characters; RisuAI characters, lorebooks, and regex sets; RoleCall and SillyTavern characters, lorebooks, personas, and regex sets. The Handles column is limited to this guide's coverage and does not claim to inventory upstream platform features. Backyard's flat JSON and .byaf shapes share one page; Marinara character cards use the SillyTavern-shaped path. For unlisted apps, inspect the export; Character Card v2 or v3 JSON is covered by the SillyTavern guide, while other shapes may need another adapter. The generated format reference lists current adapters. Conversion differences are described per page.

- **H2 The platforms** (`the-platforms`)
  The table maps each platform guide to the Bunzai deck kinds it covers: Agnai characters and lorebooks; Backyard characters; Chub characters and lorebooks; Lumiverse characters, personas, and regex sets; Marinara personas and regex sets; NovelAI lorebooks; Pygmalion characters; RisuAI characters, lorebooks, and regex sets; RoleCall and SillyTavern characters, lorebooks, personas, and regex sets. Handles describes this guide's adapter coverage only; an omitted kind says nothing about the upstream platform's capabilities. Backyard's older flat JSON and modern .byaf character shapes share one page. Marinara character cards use the SillyTavern-shaped path, while Marinara's page covers its persona and regex formats.
- **H2 If your platform isn't listed** (`if-your-platform-isnt-listed`)
  Bunzai detects supported files by their contents. If an app exports Character Card v2 or v3 JSON, the SillyTavern guide describes that supported shape. Other exports may need another adapter or may be unsupported, so inspect the file before selecting a target. For the exhaustive generated list of current Bunzai adapters, run bun run bunzai formats or read the format reference. That reference is built from the live adapter registry.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Why do some platforms list fewer kinds? The table describes only the adapters covered by these guides; it is not an inventory of upstream features. Will a conversion lose anything? Sometimes. Each platform page names the fields that remain or drop, and the converting guide explains the general rule. If an app is not listed, inspect its export or try importing it: Bunzai recognizes supported file shapes rather than relying on app names and reports files it cannot read.

### RisuAI

- id: `guide/platforms/risu`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/risu.md`
- RisuAI exports a character as .charx zip with card.json assets and sometimes module.risum of regex trigger and lorebook rows. What Bunzai reads: .charx cards lorebook .json or .lorebook and regex as .risum or bare JSON rows; detect by zip and card.json not filename. What comes across cleanly keeps the original and lifts core fields greetings group-only Risu additional text creator notes dates display settings bias words image-gen voice scripts as data never run privileged flag as warning only embedded lorebook; images ride as bytes and may not preview live because of internal refs. What changes when you convert to another app keeps same-app fields assets scripts though not guaranteed identical archive bytes; cross-app drops Risu behavior surface scripts display package ids. Editing on the Workbench has Workshop tab for script data; bundled .risum is look do not touch and edit blocks export. Exporting back out writes .charx with linked lorebook in character_book; media only survives same-app Risu round trip not foreign pictures yet. Common questions cover semantic exactness not byte identity preview scripts risum edit block and lorebook put-back.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads RisuAI cards as .charx a zip holding card.json plus assets folder of images and sometimes a bundled module.risum package. Lorebooks export as plain .json or .lorebook. Regex scripts arrive as a .risum module with rule rows or a bare .json list of rows. Drop on the Library for a plain-words receipt. Recognition uses zip signature and card.json entry not filename so renamed exports still import. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Importing loses nothing. Bunzai keeps a complete original then lifts name nickname description personality scenario first message alternate and group-only greetings examples system post-history Risu additional text creator notes including per-language source license tags dates display settings view screen large portrait inlay utility bot lore plus bias words image-gen hints voice config regex trigger virtual script background HTML/CSS default variables module toggles prebuilt-asset config privileged flag as warning marker never executed and embedded lorebook. Script rows never run; Bunzai parses edits and writes them as plain data. Every image rides as background bytes; Risu internal image refs mean live Workbench preview is not always available though bytes export back. Bundled .risum package is kept whole.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  Same-app round-trip keeps everything you can see and touch though not necessarily the same bytes: fields assets and script rows come back when exported to RisuAI. Converting to another app drops Risu private extras on purpose: display settings additional text and the whole behavior surface regex trigger virtual script background HTML/CSS default variables module toggles prebuilt-asset config privileged flag plus bundled .risum package internal ids bookkeeping. Core character and embedded lorebook come across as far as the target holds; bias image-gen voice only if matching slots. Another app means SillyTavern Agnai Backyard RoleCall Pygmalion. Never blind-copy scripts; they stay where they work. Fewer target slots drop on their side not yours.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Send a piece to the Workbench as a tab and edit name description personality scenario first message and example messages like any card. Save writes the whole card so untouched parts stay. Cards with Risu scripts get a second tab Workshop for regex rows trigger rows virtual script background HTML/CSS and default variables as data you read and edit never run. If a separate .risum package is bundled Workshop shows its contents as look do not touch for now: editing the package and exporting is currently blocked rather than risk a broken write-back. Leave it as imported and it exports whole.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage for the Press; each run exports staged pieces to one target. Bunzai writes RisuAI cards as .charx the same zip container Risu exports so it loads like any Risu export. A linked lorebook rides written into character_book the slot Risu reads on import. Plain .txt or .md available for reading text. Other app targets apply the drop story. Gap: if a piece picture came from a different app exporting to RisuAI currently leaves .charx with no images; media only survives same-app Risu round trip where original archive files ride untouched. A picture from elsewhere has nowhere to land in a Risu export yet.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Will the card come back exactly as it was? In every way that matters yes on export to RisuAI: every field asset and script row survives but exact .charx bytes are not guaranteed because Bunzai rebuilds the archive and re-prints JSON so spacing compression and entry order can shift. Imported .charx returns as .charx. Picture bytes always ride on same-app round trip; live Workbench preview depends on Risu internal refs. Regex and trigger scripts stay on the Risu card and are not copied cross-app; on Workbench they are data never run. Bundled .risum can be opened and looked at; leave alone to export whole; edit currently blocks export so change packages in RisuAI for now. Embedded worldbook is pulled for solo edit and put back into character_book on export to apps that read one.

### RoleCall

- id: `guide/platforms/rolecall`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/rolecall.md`
- This guide covers importing RoleCall character cards and lorebooks, editing them in Bunzai, and exporting to RoleCall or another format. RoleCall cards add casting details, presentation settings, and sprites to the shared character fields. Bunzai reads character cards as PNG with embedded text or as JSON, and reads RoleCall lorebooks as JSON. The Library receipt reports what it recognized; card type detection uses file contents rather than the filename. The guide lists the fields available for editing and explains that parsed source data is retained as escrow. A same-format export preserves original field values and applies edits, while JSON formatting or the output container may change. Cross-format conversion carries shared character fields and drops RoleCall-specific extras when the target has no matching fields. Workbench saves preserve untouched field values semantically. The Press exports staged pieces to one chosen target, and linked lorebooks can travel with supported character cards. Common questions cover JSON output, portrait retention, RoleCall-only presentation data, and embedded lorebook editing and export.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads the two file types RoleCall gives you. Character cards arrive as PNG where RoleCall tucks card text inside the image or as plain JSON; both hold Character Card v2 or v3 with an extra block for casting card, presentation layer, and sprite pack. Lorebooks arrive as JSON in RoleCall's own export format. Drag the file onto the Library and drop anywhere in the room; Bunzai reads and shows a plain-words receipt of what it read and kept. It tells a RoleCall character card from a RoleCall persona card even though shapes are almost identical by reading inside the file for what marks a card as a character, not the filename or a guess. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Bunzai retains the parsed source card data in escrow and exposes supported fields for editing: name, tagline, description, casting-card identity, personality, scenario, greetings, examples, prompts, creator details, tags, rating, genre, fandom, presentation settings, depth injections, sprites, and any embedded lorebook. The card image is retained as the portrait. The original data provides the basis for same-format export; changed canonical fields are applied when exporting. This describes parsed field data, not byte-for-byte retention of the source file, and the output may use different JSON formatting or a different container.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  A same-format RoleCall export preserves the source card's field values and applies edits, but the JSON formatting or output container can change. During conversion to a different app, RoleCall-specific fields with no shared target home are dropped: casting-card details, presentation settings, rating, genre, fandom, and internal ids. Core character fields cross over as far as the target format supports them. Depth injections, sprites, and a linked lorebook are included only when the target provides a corresponding field or feature. The conversion keeps source-specific data in escrow rather than blindly copying it into another app's file. Where the target has fewer fields, the conversion report describes what it cannot represent.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Open a Library piece in a Workbench tab to edit its name, description, personality, scenario, first message, and examples. Saving writes the RoleCall card with edits applied; untouched source field values remain semantically preserved, though serialized formatting may change in the output file.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage a piece for the Press by choosing Stage for the Press or dropping it from the Press rail. Each run exports staged pieces to one selected target format. RoleCall character cards are written as JSON, which RoleCall can import alongside PNG cards. A linked lorebook rides with the character when supported. Plain text or Markdown output is also available for reading. Choosing another app as the target applies the conversion rules: shared character fields cross over as supported, while RoleCall-only fields without a target equivalent remain in the original piece rather than being copied into the other format.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Does the card return exactly as it was? A RoleCall export preserves the original field values alongside edits, but JSON formatting or the output container may differ. Imported PNG cards export as JSON, which RoleCall can read. Bunzai retains the card art as the portrait and keeps source data in escrow. Casting-card and presentation fields stay with the RoleCall format and are not copied into unrelated app formats. An embedded lorebook can be edited separately and re-embedded when exporting to a format that supports it.

### SillyTavern

- id: `guide/platforms/sillytavern`
- path: `docs/guide/platforms/sillytavern.md`
- This guide covers importing SillyTavern character cards and worldbooks, editing their supported fields in Bunzai, and exporting them to SillyTavern or another format. Character cards arrive as PNG with embedded text or as JSON; worldbooks arrive as JSON. The Library receipt reports recognized content, and card detection uses the file contents rather than its name. Bunzai retains parsed source data in escrow while exposing character fields, linked-world settings, and embedded worldbooks for editing. A same-format export preserves original field values and applies edits, but JSON formatting or the output container may change. Cross-format conversion carries shared character fields as supported and omits SillyTavern-specific settings and scripts that have no equivalent in the target. The Workbench applies edits while retaining untouched source values semantically. The Press writes SillyTavern cards as JSON and can re-embed a linked worldbook. Common questions explain JSON output, retained portrait art, format-specific regex data, and separate editing of embedded worldbooks.

- **H2 What Bunzai reads** (`what-bunzai-reads`)
  Bunzai reads the two file types SillyTavern gives you. Character cards arrive as PNG where the picture is the card with character text tucked inside the image or as plain JSON; both hold the same thing. Worldbooks, which SillyTavern calls World Info, arrive as JSON. Drag the file onto the Library and drop anywhere in the room; Bunzai reads and shows a plain-words receipt of what it read and kept. It recognizes a card by looking inside the file not by the name, so renaming a card does not confuse it. Full walkthrough lives in the importing guide.
- **H2 What comes across cleanly** (`what-comes-across-cleanly`)
  Bunzai retains parsed source card data in escrow and exposes supported fields for editing: name, description, personality, scenario, greetings, examples, prompts, creator details, tags, talkativeness, linked-world name, and any embedded worldbook. The card image is retained as the portrait. Source fields not represented as editable canonical fields remain in the original data for same-format export. This is field-level preservation, not a promise to retain source-file bytes; JSON formatting and output container can change.
- **H2 What changes when you convert to another app** (`what-changes-when-you-convert-to-another-app`)
  A same-format SillyTavern export preserves original field values and applies edits, but JSON formatting or the output container may change. Conversion to another app omits SillyTavern-specific data with no target equivalent, including extension settings, add-on fields, regex scripts, internal ids, saved layout, and embedding vectors. Shared character fields cross over as far as the target format supports them. An embedded worldbook, talkativeness, or linked-world name crosses over only when the target supports the relevant feature. The source-specific data remains in escrow rather than being blindly copied into another app's file; the export receipt reports unsupported fields.
- **H2 Editing on the Workbench** (`editing-on-the-workbench`)
  Open a Library piece in a Workbench tab to edit its name, description, personality, scenario, first message, and examples. Saving writes the card with edits applied; untouched source field values remain semantically preserved, though serialized formatting may change in the output file.
- **H2 Exporting back out** (`exporting-back-out`)
  Stage a piece for the Press by choosing Stage for the Press or dropping it from the Press rail. Each run exports staged pieces to one selected target. SillyTavern cards are written as JSON, which SillyTavern can load alongside PNG cards. An embedded or linked worldbook can ride with a character when supported. Plain text or Markdown output is available for reading. Choosing another app applies the conversion rules: supported shared character fields cross over, while SillyTavern-specific settings and scripts without a target equivalent are not written into the other format.
- **H2 Common questions** (`common-questions`)
  Does the card return exactly as it was? A SillyTavern export preserves original field values alongside edits, but JSON formatting or the output container may differ. Imported PNG cards export as JSON, which SillyTavern can read. Bunzai retains the card art as the portrait and keeps source data in escrow. SillyTavern regex scripts are not copied into another app's file. An embedded worldbook can be pulled out for editing and re-embedded when exporting to a format that supports worldbooks.

## reference

### Architecture

- id: `reference/architecture`
- path: `docs/reference/architecture.md`
- This reference maps Bunzai's canonical conversion engine, scene runtime and host layers. Every external format maps through one canonical superset; escrow retains original source and binary carriers without carrying private or executable payloads across formats. KindAdapter contracts, exhaustive shape parity and strict canonical decoders reject schema drift, while serialization reports describe loss. Detection ranks kind-correct adapters and folder discovery supplies the registry. Bundles extract and re-embed lorebooks. Semantic capabilities cover pure previews, progressive discovery, bounded reads, persistent drafts, result eviction and paging, independently approved documentation, and explicit durable apply with revision checks and re-read verification. Layering reuses the same engine across the CLI, installed Studio and static browser Studio. The encrypted stage separates versioned pieces, Studio documents and chats; folders remain import/export boundaries. Its source map names database, crypto, snapshot, health, asset and search owners. Scale and release claims require bounded, artifact-matched evidence. The reusable roleplay source API keeps the current canonical turn orchestration and injects stores, providers and readers. Its runtime owns live turns, gates, tracker rounds, recovery and accepted host task completion through shutdown. Desktop consumes it; a caller-owned terminal runner is unit-proven. Browser bundling and a scripted Node turn establish source compatibility only. A standalone shared-library terminal command awaits durable cross-process checkpoint ownership; packaged release behavior remains separately unverified.

- **H2 The one idea: hub and spoke** (`the-one-idea-hub-and-spoke`)
  Every external format maps to and from one canonical superset rather than converting directly to every other format. A pairwise mesh requires N times N minus one paths; hub and spoke needs only N adapters. Adding a format therefore adds one adapter without changing existing formats or core conversion logic. Import calls the adapter's toCanonical operation, export calls fromCanonical, and conversion composes those two steps. This is the architectural invariant behind adapter independence and honest interoperability.
- **H2 The canonical entity** (`the-canonical-entity`)
  src/core/canonical.ts defines the shared wrapper: schemaVersion identifies the canonical revision, kind selects the entity family, id is the stable identity, body contains interoperable content, profiles hold sparse per-platform overrides, and original holds source escrow. Character, lorebook, persona, preset, regex, and quickreply are adapter-backed kinds; canonical schemas also define pack and htmldoc entities. Body details live in their entity folders rather than in the wrapper. The superset rule limits body fields to meaning represented by at least one real wire format. Values that exist only in one application's memory or have no portable producer remain in escrow instead of acquiring a misleading canonical slot.
  - **H3 The superset rule** (`the-superset-rule`)
    Canonical bodies contain only fields serialized by real formats. A field earns a first-class slot only when a wire producer exists. Application-only values, including five RoleCall lorebook fields, stay escrowed so Bunzai does not promise unsupported portability.
- **H2 Escrow: semantic round-trips, contained cross-format loss** (`escrow-semantic-round-trips-contained-cross-format-loss`)
  Escrow preserves parsed source snapshots and unmapped fields for semantic same-format round trips. Byte identity is limited to explicitly specified fixture-proven tiers. Entity parts isolate escrow from light editor reads; light media stays sealed with stable references, while legacy blobbed authored text is materialized only when needed. Conversion follows hub and spoke with explicit loss reporting.
- **H2 The adapter contract** (`the-adapter-contract`)
  The adapter contract: src/core/adapter.ts defines one generic KindAdapter<K> (kind, toCanonical returning that kind's canonical piece, fromCanonical with an optional EmitContext that carries embedded lorebooks) and FormatAdapter as the union of it over every kind in the generated kind seam; character cards add extractLorebook and presets add extractRegex and embedRegex. Formats are open drop-in folders; kinds are a closed set that grows by adding an entities/<kind>/ folder with its kind.ts and running bun run kinds. Adapter output carries text or bytes, suggestedExtension, and a SerializeReport naming escrowed, dropped, shadowed, and warning paths; escrowFormatIds declares family-keyed twins such as rolecall-preset owning original.rolecall, so loss reporting excludes genuine same-format escrow. Canonical JSON crosses storage and HTTP through src/entities/runtime-schema.ts, which unions every kind's whole-entity entitySchema (the shared envelope in _shared/envelope.ts plus its body) collected by the generated kind seam. Each runtime schema uses defineExhaustiveShape<T>() for exact input and output parity; missing or extra keys, narrowed unions, coercion, any, and never fail typecheck. Storage, HTTP save, format-corpus verification, and Kit drafts share this ADR-011 boundary.
- **H2 Detection and the registry** (`detection-and-the-registry`)
  src/core/registry.ts holds the in-memory adapter table. Detection runs every adapter, rejects scores at or below 0.5, and selects the single highest-confidence match. Scores encode specificity: a RoleCall card can score 1.0 and outrank the generic SillyTavern reader at 0.9 for the same compatible CCv3 payload. Because character and lorebook adapters share one registry, detectors also enforce a cross-kind firewall. A bare name is insufficient evidence for SillyTavern flat-v1 character data; at least one genuine character signal such as description, personality, scenario, first_mes, or mes_example is required, preventing a name-bearing worldbook from being misclassified.
- **H2 Folders-as-schema (the moddability promise)** (`folders-as-schema-the-moddability-promise`)
  src/core/loader.ts is the impure discovery edge. It scans src/formats/*/index.ts with filesystem primitives, dynamically imports each module, and registers its default export. Names beginning with an underscore, including _shared and _template, are ignored. A folder may export one adapter or an array when a format family supplies multiple codecs. Agnai, Risu, SillyTavern, and RoleCall each include character and lorebook codecs; some arrays also include regex, persona, preset, or quick-reply codecs. FormatId is an open string rather than a central enum. Adding a format means copying the template, implementing detection and both canonical directions, and supplying proof; no core registry list or unrelated format file is edited.
- **H2 Bundles: a card plus its lorebook** (`bundles-a-card-plus-its-lorebook`)
  CCv2 and CCv3 character cards can embed a character_book, but Bunzai models that book as a separate canonical lorebook referenced by the character rather than leaving an opaque nested blob. During import, shared bundle logic extracts the book, creates a CanonicalLorebook, and records its id in knowledgeRefs. During export, the character adapter receives resolved lorebooks through EmitContext and embeds the appropriate target-format representation. The resolved list is authoritative even when empty: removing a link or disabling all linked books clears stale embedded data from the escrowed source twin. convertFile coordinates extraction and re-embedding for ordinary same-kind conversions, keeping bundle behavior format-agnostic at the canonical layer.
- **H2 Semantic content capabilities** (`semantic-content-capabilities`)
  Capability folders provide pure operations returning previews, changes, warnings, and platform impact without writing. Kit projects content, studio, transfer, and diagnostics into one catalog. Only pure content gets exact read-or-draft access; deferred apply and writable bridges fail closed until separate safety policy grants an exact name. Search exposes five matches, browse and describe progressively, hidden or duplicate descriptors fail, and deferred exposure resets each user turn while drafts persist. studio_read uses RFC 6901 pointers and paging; oversized values use result_query handles whose store enforces per-entry and aggregate UTF-8 byte caps, a count cap, oldest-first eviction, character paging, and direct-offset recovery beyond capacity. change_query retains proposals; read-effect tools return deterministic observations and are rejected for changing content or claiming changes. docs_query searches indexed metadata and reads authoritative Markdown without arbitrary paths or network. Summaries enter generated indexes only with independent hash-bound APPROVE receipts; source or summary edits invalidate approval. Focused checks validate an author's sidecar; global checks require every approval and current projections. Security classifies only exact catalog-derived names as preview-only; durable apply remains an explicit compareAndSave write with re-read verification.
- **H2 Layering** (`layering`)
  Core owns pure canonical computation and shared synchronous derivation; entity schemas and capabilities own semantic shape; codecs convert through canonical; Stage storage backends remain opaque. Light editor reads, selected escrow and native asset metadata feed conditional patch saves without full escrow/media opens. D18 prevents backend imports of engine, entity or format code.
- **H2 The stage (chat runtime, in progress)** (`the-stage-chat-runtime-in-progress`)
  Stage stores encrypted pieces and chats behind contracts; scene turns assemble prompts through Kit providers. A SillyTavern chat imports in one transaction (rule 29); a message can be hidden (rule 30); a turn writes a reply, a version, or a continuation (rule 31); a cut message is purged after 1 day, other trash after 3 (rule 11). Guides and saved scene setups are StateStore documents the folder export carries. Runtime, migrations and packaged proof remain unrun.
- **H2 Reusable roleplay core** (`reusable-roleplay-core`)
  createRoleplayCore exposes canonical turns and the Dramaturg with injected stores, providers and readers. One runtime owns controllers, gates, tracker rounds, recovery and accepted host tasks; shutdown cancels and awaits owned writes. ChatStore owns the sealed, revision-guarded Dramaturg conversation in migration 46, preserved by snapshots alongside existing scene state. Desktop supplies library hooks; a terminal runner accepts a configured core. Browser and Node checks prove source compatibility, not published or packaged behavior.
- **H2 The CLI** (`the-cli`)
  The thin CLI provides conversion, inspection, labeling, validation, formats, Studio launch, folder export, derived-cache commands, version and help. A separate injected terminal session runner handles text, continue, swipe and quit using the same core and scene writes. It fails applicable imported regex closed without a worker. Scripted tests cover the runner; a standalone shared-library command is withheld until durable cross-process checkpoint ownership makes recovery safe.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  The source map names canonical, registry, adapter, loader, overlay, MIME, bundle, derivation, persistence, encryption and entity owners, including snapshots, health and care routes. Appearance packages share a contract, inventory and runtime resolver; legacy import has a separate owner. Shared theme reads are in `extensions/theme-read-routes.ts`; Desktop and Pocket call them, while Studio polls revisions. Scale evidence is bounded; CLI launch differs from desktop identity, and runtime metrics and RSS remain unproved.

### The audit suite

- id: `reference/audits`
- path: `docs/reference/audits.md`
- Reference for the runnable UI audit suite under scripts audit. Audits drive the real app in a real browser against a scratch studio seeded through real adapters and enumerate the live app at runtime: room list from the dock, deck and view lists from Library chip rows, lens lists from the editor select. Add an app deck view or lens and the next run walks it with no script edits. The one hand-grown file is walks promises.ts because behavior expectations are human knowledge; every new feature adds a row there act then observable effect. Runs table: audit everything in order; audit rooms every dock catalog app both themes; library every deck times view staging delete context; editors every kind write-for lens binder export book-rules folder inspector; setup first-run wizard steps theme accent; promises act-effect table; clicks every visible enabled clickable flagging dead-click suspects not auto-fail with Quit Restart external blocklisted; legibility dark light style only; watch reruns rooms plus promises on src ui change. The style audit every visited state checks tiny-text low-contrast naked-control plus page errors. Artifacts under audit-out run screenshots and ledger.json as coverage claim; non-zero on findings for CI. Honest boundaries: unrendered never audited; dead-click heuristic; unit suite carries non-promise behavior.

- **H2 Runs** (`runs`)
  bun run audit walks everything below in order. audit rooms walks every dock and catalog app both themes for style audit per room. audit library walks every deck times every view staging bar delete confirm context menu for style per state. audit editors opens one piece of every kind every write-for lens binder views export and book-rules dialogs and folder inspector. audit setup walks first-run wizard every step live theme flip custom accent final. audit promises walks the promise table act then observable effect covering import picker drop count delete staging lenses search updates theme create duplicate rename. audit clicks every visible enabled clickable flags no-effect as dead-click suspects eyeball list not auto-fail; Quit Restart and external links blocklisted. audit legibility dark or light pins theme for style only. audit watch reruns the quick set rooms plus promises whenever src ui changes.
- **H2 The style audit (every visited state)** (`the-style-audit-every-visited-state`)
  Three exhaustive checks over the full DOM from page-audit.ts deliberately DOM-bound: the whole function is serialized into the page and runs there. tiny-text flags any text node under the 10px floor. low-contrast flags text under WCAG AA against effective background 4.5 to 1 or 3 to 1 for large bold text. naked-control flags any button or select wearing browser-default fonts instead of house tokens the unstyled-component regression class. Page errors thrown during a walk are ledgered too.
- **H2 Artifacts** (`artifacts`)
  Each run writes audit-out run folder: one screenshot per visited state theme-stop png and ledger.json holding the visited-state list and every finding. The visited list is the coverage claim: anything not on it was not audited. A run exits non-zero when it found anything so runs can gate CI.
- **H2 Honest boundaries** (`honest-boundaries`)
  A state never rendered is never audited; coverage grows with the walkers and the ledger names exactly what was walked. Dead-click detection is heuristic because some no-ops are legitimate; its output is a suspect list for review. Behavior beyond the promise table is carried by the unit suite not these walks.

### CLI reference

- id: `reference/cli`
- path: `docs/reference/cli.md`
- CLI reference for bunzai over the same canonical engine and format adapters as Studio. src/cli.ts dispatches, cli-validation enforces the exhaustive canonical runtime schema, and cli-io owns conversion I/O policy. Invocation works through source, package bin, or compiled output; bun run dev starts the primary Studio loopback listener. Commands cover convert, inspect, validate, label, formats, ui, export, version, help, no arguments, and unknown input. convert guards paths, detects and converts every same-kind adapter pair, checks container agreement, enforces its serialize report and optional strict-loss contract, then publishes atomically. inspect prints reachable kind-specific fields for character, lorebook, persona, regex, and preset; an internal media-pack formatter exists but no live format adapter can reach it. validate rejects unrecognized input, adapter failures, and invalid canonical output. ui blocks on the primary loopback server while remote listeners remain separately configured. export opens an existing library, never makes one, refuses a password-locked library and any destination that is not empty or sits inside the studio, and writes the plain folder layout the import reads. JSON and positional inconsistencies are named explicitly; export/derived usage errors exit2 and refusals use stderr. Derived stats/clear now expose existing-library cache maintenance without touching canonical data.

- **H2 Invocation** (`invocation`)
  In the repo run bun run src cli.ts command args or package script bun run bunzai command args. package.json bin maps the bunzai command straight to src cli.ts so an installed or linked package exposes it directly. bun run build cli compiles a standalone binary to dist bunzai via bun build compile src cli.ts. bun run dev is bunzai ui 8321.
- **H2 Commands** (`commands`)
  Commands convert, inspect, validate, label and formats use the canonical engine and format adapters. UI starts the loopback Studio; export writes an existing unlocked library as a plain folder. Derived stats/clear inspect or remove rebuildable caches. Version/help print banners, no arguments means help, and unknown input exits1.
- **H2 Global flags** (`global-flags`)
  Standalone version/help flags select those commands. JSON applies to version, formats, validate, convert and derived stats; inspect, label, UI, help and derived clear remain human-readable. Convert alone accepts to for a forced adapter, yes/y for output replacement without replacing input, and strict for refusal on dropped paths.
- **H2 `bunzai derived --stats|--clear`** (`bunzai-derived---stats--clear`)
  Derived stats and clear open an existing unlocked encrypted library without session-copy, Trash-purge or summary-rebuild maintenance. Stats reports process cache hits/misses, persisted rows, estimated bytes and mean derivation cost per family; json emits the array. Clear removes rebuildable persistent and process caches only. Missing or locked libraries fail without creating one.
- **H2 `bunzai convert <in> <out>`** (`bunzai-convert-in-out`)
  Flags may appear anywhere after convert; parseConvertFlags scans the tail for yes y strict and to value leaving exactly two positionals; duplicates bad to unknown flags or wrong positional count exit 1 with usage. Before a byte is written, guardConvertOutput refuses same-file out as in and requires yes when out exists; registry detect fails listing known ids when unrecognized; resolveTarget prefers to format or extension via targetsForExtension and never guesses ambiguous matches; convertFile imports and exports with bundled lorebook extraction and re-embedding; assertContainerAgreement checks the adapter extension and payload shape; the command requires a serialize report; strict then refuses reported drops or undeclared coverage; publishAtomic writes a temp sibling, fsyncs, and renames while leaving the prior destination on failure. Only success honors json with ok, adapter and path fields, payload counts, and report details; undeclared coverage uses unknown with a null dropped count. Every failure prints plain text. Plain reports name confirmed drops, warnings, and unknown loss.
- **H2 `bunzai inspect <file>`** (`bunzai-inspect-file`)
  Detects the adapter, converts to canonical, validates the exhaustive runtime schema and matching adapter kind, then prints format id, adapter label, entity kind, and kind fields. Character prints greeting and tags; lorebook entries and budget; persona content and sections; regex rules; preset prompts, groups, and choices. The formatter contains a media-pack branch, but no live format adapter imports packs, so the CLI cannot currently reach it. Output is always human-readable. File is args1, so a preceding flag becomes the path.
- **H2 `bunzai validate <file>`** (`bunzai-validate-file`)
  Detects an adapter, converts through toCanonical, parses the result through the exhaustive canonical runtime schema, and requires the parsed kind to match the adapter contract in cli-validation before pass or fail. Unrecognized input, adapter errors, invalid canonical output, and kind mismatches are INVALID; otherwise OK prints format kind name. Exit is 0 or 1. Path is the first non-validate non-json arg. json shapes cover success, unrecognized failure, and recognized failure with format plus error.
- **H2 `bunzai label <file>`** (`bunzai-label-file`)
  Guesses a card spec and likely origin app without fully parsing into the canonical model via labelCard sniffContainer in character provenance. Source is best available: detected adapter escrowed raw else JSON parse of decoded text else undefined so label can still report unknown none on unrecognized files. Prints format as card spec chara_card_v3 v2 v1 agnai backyard or unknown not an adapter id; container png charx json or unknown; origin likely authoring app plus confidence or unknown; signals every fingerprint or none. Does not honor json. file is args1 same positional caveat as inspect.
- **H2 `bunzai formats`** (`bunzai-formats`)
  Loads every adapter the loader discovers under formats star index and lists each id kind label and output extensions. Plain text also prints the doc path for the generated coverage matrix docs FORMAT-SUPPORT.md regenerated with bun run matrix and a reminder that a new format is a folder drop-in copy of formats template. json shape is schema 1 with adapters array of id kind label outputExtensions.
- **H2 `bunzai ui [port] [studioDir]`** (`bunzai-ui-port-studiodir`)
  Starts Studio's primary loopback server through startUi and blocks until interrupt. Optional remote listeners remain a separate Studio configuration path. port is Number args1 or 8321; missing, non-numeric, or 0 values fall back. studioDir is args2, defaulting to the user's Documents/Bunzai Studio folder, and requires a port argument first because it is the third token. The command does not honor json.
- **H2 `bunzai export <folder> [studioDir]`** (`bunzai-export-folder-studiodir`)
  Writes the studio's encrypted library out as a plain studio folder through cli-export.ts and exportLibraryToFolder in folder-export.ts: every live piece as kind/id.json and settings, collections, engines, and graveyard documents at the top, the layout the folder import reads. Trash and earlier versions stay in the library. It opens an existing library and never makes one, exiting 1 when there is no library.db. A password-locked library is refused. The destination must be new or empty and outside the studio folder, built beside it and renamed into place when complete. Output warns the folder is not encrypted and lists damaged pieces and collection entries naming deleted pieces left out; the app open empties old Trash first. A missing folder argument prints usage and exits 2; json is not honored.
- **H2 `bunzai version`** (`bunzai-version`)
  bunzai version v and version flag forms are equivalent; the short and long version flags are checked before any other command match ahead of no-arguments and help fallback. Plain form prints just the version string. json shape is version string and schema 1.
- **H2 `bunzai help`** (`bunzai-help`)
  help h help flag and bare no-arguments all print the same built-in HELP banner: usage line command table flag list one example block and a status line naming schema version and format-matrix doc path. No arguments falls through to the same branch as help. Does not honor json.
- **H2 Quirks** (`quirks`)
  JSON is honored by version, formats, validate, convert and derived stats; inspect, label, UI, help and derived clear remain human-readable. Convert failures remain plain text and y aliases yes. Missing export destination or invalid derived action exits2 with stderr usage; operational refusals exit1 on stderr and success uses stdout. Conversion/inspection generally use stdout. Validate accepts its path around JSON, while inspect/label read args1 literally. Label format means the spec; inspect format means adapter id.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  Command dispatch and HELP live in cli.ts; runtime validation and kind agreement in cli-validation.ts; conversion parsing, guards, and atomic publish in cli-io.ts. Registry detection and extension resolution use core/registry.ts. Schema, discovery, and bundle conversion use canonical.ts, loader.ts, and convert.ts. Provenance labeling uses entities/character/provenance.ts, UI startup uses ui/server.ts, and package.json defines invocation surfaces.

### Component catalog

- id: `reference/components`
- path: `docs/reference/components.md`
- Component catalog is the generated inventory of exported Bunzai UI entries. scripts/hooks/component-catalog.ts scans React components throughout src/ui plus exported create/build/swatch/inject helpers under src/ui/_shared. Authors do not hand-edit the markdown: regenerate it with bun run scripts/hooks/component-catalog.ts, and the pre-commit --check gate fails when live exports drift. Each row records Name, source File, TypeScript Signature, optional Doc blurb, and Styles class list. Before building a control, scan this catalog and reuse an existing entry rather than inventing a twin. The two tables distinguish React `.tsx` components from shared `.ts` UI helpers; that distinction is file shape and API role, not a claim that helpers are legacy or awaiting migration. Use the catalog as lookup authority for props and file homes; behavior and layout truth remain in reference/ui and the owning source. Recent entries include the Extension manager, bounded renderer, settings editor, effect-review panel and command menus used by Library and Workbench. ScenePage exposes navigation-ticket and load identifiers for verified host navigation handoff. The catalog also includes the cover composer, context-menu button, reusable proscenium and capped LightRays effect used by the approved premiere layout. It also lists the Context wing, Messenger shell and panels, Booth Chat Summary, shared RoomHeader and Panel, Promptbook editors, Agent and Skill editors, shared Skill conditions and the model picker.

- **H2 Use this for** (`use-this-for`)
  A short table answers the common UI needs before anyone searches the full catalog. Any action with text uses the Button from the button family, with one primary per view, danger for destructive actions and quiet for low emphasis. An icon-only action is an IconButton with a label; a few options are a Segmented; sections of a room or editor are Tabs; a small on/off filter or tag is a Chip; a clickable row is a ListRow, a menu entry a MenuItem, a fold a Disclosure, a small square target a Tile, a larger face a Card, a tiny mark a Dot. It also names the icon door Ic, SwatchRow and PaintPicker for colors, Toggle for switches, InkDialog or DialogCore for dialogs, and the three theme font tokens. For buttons, color pickers and fonts, lint and the style guard block the alternatives; the rest is the house answer that review checks.
- **H2 Components (React, `.tsx`)** (`components-react-tsx`)
  The React table lists exported UI components by name, source file, TypeScript signature and optional documentation and styles links. It covers the shell, Dock, shared controls, Library, Workbench editors, Scenes, Settings, and documentation surfaces. Recent extension entries include the manager, bounded renderer, settings editor, effect-review panel and host command menus. ScenePage exposes navigation-ticket and load identifiers for verified navigation handoff. BookCover composes lorebook presentation, Proscenium frames scene artwork, and LightRays supplies capped premiere beams. Rows are generated discovery metadata, not proof of behavior or a second registry; consult reference/ui and source for the live contract. It also lists Stage AccentRow, Compendium Settings, Participants, Storyboards, the Props wing, Messenger and shared virtualized lists. RoomHeader, Panel, IconPicker, NumberInput, Promptbook views, Agent and Skill editors, shared conditions and model selection also appear.
- **H2 Shared UI helpers (`src/ui/_shared`, `.ts`)** (`shared-ui-helpers-srcui_shared-ts`)
  Secondary table for exported TypeScript UI helpers under src/ui/_shared whose names begin create, build, swatch, or inject. These are ordinary `.ts` functions rather than JSX components; the table makes no deprecation or migration claim. It uses the same Name, File, Signature, Doc, and Styles columns as the React table. Current entries are buildTimeline from version-history, a pure helper the Updates surface uses to derive release relations and behind counts, and createArtCache, the bounded in-memory art cache that dedupes decrypted portraits and revokes its object URLs on eviction or clear (the app's singleton is also cleared on library lock). Regenerating the catalog adds or removes rows as matching exports change. Open this table when a UI task needs shared computation or construction logic, and open the React table when it needs a rendered control.

### Glossary

- id: `reference/glossary`
- path: `docs/reference/glossary.md`
- The glossary explains project vocabulary, folder ownership, and naming traps, then points readers to the generated code map for individual files. Its folder table includes src/shared for cross-domain helpers and describes src/extensions as the package catalog, SDK, approval lifecycle, and bounded QuickJS runtime. It distinguishes Lua and regex test benches from extension JavaScript guest workers; imported script content remains data until its specific supported isolated worker is invoked. The glossary also covers the engine and formats, Studio and Kit, Stage, encryption, Compendium, snapshots, health checks, and documentation process. Its check requires every direct src folder and every named path to remain valid.

- **H2 Where things live** (`where-things-live`)
  One row per top-level folder. core is the pure engine center; entities owns schemas, runtime checks, and capabilities by content kind; formats holds discovered adapters. studio is disk storage, while screens live in ui. kit is the terminal agent; stage is the chat runtime. sandbox contains bounded Lua and regex test benches and extension JavaScript guest workers; imported scripts stay inert until a supported isolated worker runs. shared contains small cross-domain helpers. site is the Pages website; pocket the in-memory browser build; mcp the protocol wire; src/docs checks documentation; generated holds script output; types holds dependency declarations. specs, docs, scripts, and sidecar complete the map. Light-rays owns the capped Vaudeville OGL spotlight and fog renderer and CSS fallback; proscenium owns the reusable theater frame, curtains, sign, and artwork aperture.
- **H2 The engine and formats** (`the-engine-and-formats`)
  The converter's vocabulary. The canonical entity is the one shared shape every format converts to and from. Hub and spoke means every conversion goes format to canonical to format, never directly between formats, and the word spoke also names a Stage provider. An adapter is one format's plug-in that detects its files and converts one kind of content. Escrow is the original field that keeps a format's own fields the canonical model has no place for, stored per source format. The superset rule admits a field to the canonical model only when a real format emits it. Folders-as-schema means adding a folder adds a format, app, or capability, with nothing listed centrally. A capability is a typed edit that previews a change and never writes by itself. The registry scores which format a file is, and the round-trip law keeps meaning on same-format export.
- **H2 The Studio and Kit** (`the-studio-and-kit`)
  The Studio is the app where content is made and kept; its storage folder is src/studio and its screens are in src/ui. Each app inside it is a room: the Library for importing and browsing, the Workbench as the editing home, and Press for export, with the Binder lorebook editor living inside the Workbench rather than as its own room. The Dock is the row of tiles that switches rooms. Kit is the terminal agent over the same content, included by release build commands; its bridge is its one trust-bounded way into Studio content, its belt is the tool list offered to the model, its gate is the pause where a draft is applied or discarded (not the commit checks), its rail shows a preset's prompt blocks in order, and its doctor is a self-check distinct from the planned card health check. The graveyard holds deleted content before purging, and keep-both saves beside an existing copy on a name clash.
- **H2 The Stage (the chat runtime)** (`the-stage-the-chat-runtime`)
  Stage is the encrypted chat/library runtime behind contracts and provider/pipeline owners. Cast is the frozen roster, branch/swipe are forks and alternate replies, and turn is a full generation. PieceStore shares accepted summaries without becoming body authority; portable search contracts differ from native worker/persistence owners. Cold fill is bounded reconstruction with logical admission rather than measured RSS. Performance evidence is revision/artifact-bound observations, currently source definitions without measured results. Background lanes and breathers keep expensive work off the UI path. The health check opens every sealed cell on the lane and sets damaged rows aside, never deleting; freeze gate adds perf-gate-health.ts. A loadout is a saved bundle of a scene's own settings kept as one sealed library document rather than a piece, and a loadout receipt is the record of an apply that lets Undo restore the changed settings for 15 minutes; it is not the delete or import receipt. The Compendium is a scene's typed-entry view of its lorebooks, with filing receipts that are not loadout or delete receipts. Tracker is Props or library.
- **H2 Encryption** (`encryption`)
  The data key is the one key that encrypts everything in the library and never changes. The keyring is where that key is kept wrapped, together with every way to unlock it; it is not the Linux keyring, which is the Secret Service and only one of the key vault's locks. The key vault is Kit's store for secrets, locked by the operating system through DPAPI, the macOS Keychain, or the Linux Secret Service, or by a key file. The device vault is the keyring's use of that key vault to give this computer its own copy of the data key, and a remembered computer is one holding such a copy so it opens without a password. The recovery words are the twelve words that open the library when everything else is lost. A cell is one encrypted value in the database. A stand-in is a test-only fake file named fake-something that app code may never import. A snapshot is an hourly clean copy of the library file that a restore can undo.
- **H2 Process and docs** (`process-and-docs`)
  How decisions and documentation are named. An ADR is an accepted decision record kept under docs/decisions. A docs sidecar is a documentation page's summary file under docs/summaries, which must be approved by an independent reviewer before it counts; the same word also names the Go remote-access companion that lives in the sidecar folder and is built separately. Hoplight is the parent project Bunzai forked from; it has no Stage, and Stage code never flows back to it, while format and entity code stays close to it.

### reference/macro-engine

- id: `reference/macro-engine`
- path: `docs/reference/macro-engine.md`
- RoleCall's macro engine, ported under src/core/macros/ inside the closed world ADR-012 allows. It parses macro templates and evaluates them against a value-only context, producing expanded text plus segments that trace each rendered span back to the authored characters behind it, the contract the editable preview stands on. It behaves as RoleCall's engine does at the pinned commit, proven by a parity test over all 179 entries of RoleCall's own characterization corpus, recorded from RoleCall, plus RoleCall's ported processor and handler tests. Where ADR-012 requires, it differs: the clock, randomness, locale and time zone come from the context, and evaluation is bounded so no cut-off value is stored; four RoleCall families and its interceptors are left out. Callers are the ADR-012 list, now including chat display; codecs and adapters never evaluate, and macro text stays opaque payload at every import and conversion boundary.

- **H2 What it does** (`what-it-does`)
  It behaves as RoleCall's engine at VAUDEVILLE 68afe385, and rc-parity.test.ts proves it on all 179 entries of RoleCall's recorded corpus: same parse tree, text, errors, variable changes, reads and expansions. RoleCall's processor and handler tests are ported, and unknown macros render as text with an error. Normalization applies RoleCall's rewrites through an offset map. Twenty-five RoleCall families register in RoleCall's order from one closed table, since order decides who owns a shared name; one family of Bunzai's own, registered last, adds read-only mentions, lastmention, tally and seeded odds, matched from escaped words, never author regex, plus tracker for linked trackers. Lazy macros take unevaluated arguments; phases span a preset's prompts. ADR-012 differences: clock, randomness, locale and time zone come from the context; strings, writes, runs and node counts are capped, callers may hand in a deadline (soft for a prompt build, with a Settings box), and after a cap trips nothing runs or is stored cut off; a scan checks every file for ambient reads. Author regex is off unless a caller opts in.
- **H2 Segments: the editable-preview contract** (`segments-the-editable-preview-contract`)
  Segments are the editable-preview contract: processMacros returns one segment per top-level node alongside the flat text. A literal segment's value is the authored slice verbatim, escaped braces unrestored and newline runs uncollapsed, so splicing an edited value into its source range can never corrupt the source; the restored, whitespace-collapsed rendering lives in the flat text, which is postprocessed once as a whole, and segments are provenance rather than a partition of it. A macro segment carries its raw authored expression, its resolved value, and for the random family and block-if which option or branch fired, so a preview can offer branch-level edits instead of guessing at a flat string. live-render.ts layers this over buildPreview's engine-truth ordering, threading one variable context across blocks in build order and tagging each line with its block id.
- **H2 Who may call it** (`who-may-call-it`)
  Who may call it is exactly the ADR-012 list: the user-invoked macro test bench, the transfer compatibility report, prompt assembly and its previews such as live-render, and chat display. The external-engine renderer under src/core/preset/render/ remains the cross-check oracle: it answers what the real platform says, while this engine answers what the text says and where each character came from.

### Platform native fields (verified research)

- id: `reference/platform-native-fields`
- path: `docs/reference/platform-native-fields.md`
- Platform native fields is verified research source of truth for building each platforms key.ts native-field schema against real exported platform formats with honest confidence and open questions. A native field is a dot path into entity.original plus a control under rule buckets authored-editor executable-opaque db-social-readonly link-out and canonical-skip. Storage reality explains how Marinara Lumiverse and Chub ride SillyTavern CCv2 under original.sillytavern.raw while Default CCv3 is the thin-host interop shape without fake host extension bags. Marinara HIGH re-audit maps bare extensions rpgStats colors and persona bars not character pools. Chub HIGH maps namespaced extensions.chub including custom_css CssWorkshop and sealed Stages. Lumiverse MEDIUM traces bare extensions plus first-class portable Character LoRA. Character.AI is DROPPED; Crushon SKIPPED; Janitor thin optional; Pygmalion HIGH with landed flat adapter. Default CCv3 interop shape lists portable data fields. New components this research calls for include color CssWorkshop and expression stubs.

- **H2 Storage reality (how each reaches entity.original)** (`storage-reality-how-each-reaches-entityoriginal`)
  Marinara Lumiverse and Chub ride a SillyTavern or CCv2 card so their data lives under original.sillytavern.raw.data.extensions and for Chub the .chub namespace, buildable and verifiable now. Default CCv3 is the portable interop shape for thin hosts such as C.AI Tools dumps Crushon drop-import and Janitor PNG: read and write only via formats sillytavern with no fake extensions host bag and a single default-ccv3 Default tab from extension-platforms.ts. Character.AI is dropped as a native platform with no official export and definition usually withheld. Crushon is skipped as import sink only. Janitor is optional thin later preferring ST Default CCv3. None of those get their own lens tab. Pygmalion has its own flat adapter under formats pygmalion.
- **H2 Marinara (HIGH - re-audited 2026-07-09 vs Marinara-Engine clone)** (`marinara-high---re-audited-2026-07-09-vs-marinara-engine-clone`)
  Root is sillytavern.raw.data.extensions not a fabricated .marinara sub-key, verified against Marinara-Engine CharacterExtensions and CharacterEditor. Exportable CCv2 card carries rpgStats with enabled attributes and hp only no pools array; backstory appearance textareas; nameColor dialogueColor boxColor as CSS color or gradient via SwatchRow; avatarCrop and trackerCardColors on card but not yet in platforms marinara.ts; conversationStatus read-only if present; fav talkativeness depth_prompt world are ST-owned and not re-declared. Colored resource bars are real but different entity: persona personaStats bars name value max color not character pools, and runtime tracker is chat session state. Host-local comment avatarPath spriteFolderPath sprites CharacterRegex personaStats quests stay off V2 extensions. No own catch-all; ST catch-all covers leftover keys.
- **H2 Chub (HIGH - verified vs live Chub API; re-audit 2026-07-09 for jewel)** (`chub-high---verified-vs-live-chub-api-re-audit-2026-07-09-for-jewel`)
  Root is sillytavern.raw.data.extensions.chub namespaced not bare extensions, verified against live Chub API. id and full_path are read-only hub identity. custom_css is CssWorkshop editable plain CSS with sealed preview only never applied in Bunzai chrome. background_image is editable URL. expressions and alt_expressions are sprites milestone stubs with twin kept often empty on download. related_lorebooks refs are read-only link-out without book bytes. preset and Chub Stages extensions are read-only never run. ST core name description greetings character book depth_prompt map to body or ST native. vectorized is a lorebook entry field not a chub-block field.
- **H2 Lumiverse (from exported cards; MEDIUM confidence, expressions key unverified)** (`lumiverse-from-exported-cards-medium-confidence-expressions-key-unverified`)
  Root is sillytavern.raw.data.extensions bare keys with no lumiverse namespace, as observed in exported cards with unverified keys marked not guessed. expressions enabled defaultExpression mappings are asset-manager plus toggle with KEY still unverified versus expression_config. expression_groups are ListEditor of asset-managers. alternate_fields per description personality scenario are ListEditor of label content. alternate_avatars are asset-manager. world_book_ids and databank_ids are lorebook-link or read-only. Character LoRA is first-class portable card mirror under extensions.lumiverse_image_gen_lora distinct from host-local runtime binding; never auto-fetch source_url. catch-all covers remaining unknown extension keys flagged script-like opaque and character_loras is not the canonical LoRA path.
