  - **H3 Character LoRA (FIRST-CLASS - was wrongly buried as catch-all)** (`character-lora-first-class---was-wrongly-buried-as-catch-all`)
    Lumiverse attaches image-gen LoRA via Character editor LoRA tab with dual storage. Runtime binding is per-user app settings characterLora characterId with weight_model weight_clip and is not on the card. Portable card mirror is character.extensions.lumiverse_image_gen_lora PortableLoraReference v1 with lora_filename weight base_tags source_url written when bound and cleared when unbound. Importers surface expected LoRA but never auto-fetch source_url for safetensors phishing risk. Control is structured form not catch-all. character_loras name is wrong. Runtime weight_clip is not mirrored into portable single weight. ComfyUI pipeline uses runtime binding only.
- **H2 Character.AI - DROPPED** (`characterai---dropped`)
  No official card export. Community scrapers expose a flat API object but definition is usually withheld unless the creator set copyable. Building platforms characterai or formats characterai would pretend at a surface C.AI does not give users. Interop if the user already has a Tavern CCv2 or CCv3 file from CAI Tools or converters is open as Default CCv3 via SillyTavern. Honesty: shallow imports when definition was locked are C.AI's gate not ours. Do not invent original.characterai or starter-prompts native jewel. Archaeology lists scraper fields that are not build targets including name title greeting description definition categories avatar visibility copyable starter_prompts img_gen songs voices external_id.

  Import sink only: Create page drops PNG or JSON and fills a short form. No verified first-party export. Gender visibility and rating are site DB not a portable bag. Do not fabricate paths. Interop is emit Default CCv3 or flat V1 subset from the ST path so Crushon can ingest. Rich CCv3 features book alts and extensions are lossy on that host which is export honesty not a native schema.

If a scrape adapter ever lands map name personality scenario first_message first_messages example_dialogs allow_proxy custom_tags with read-only id creator catalog tags visibility flags. Advanced Scripts ES5 are sealed never-run and path often a separate entity do not invent. Profile CSS is not the character bag; use CssWorkshop janitor-profile pack for profile work.

  Root is flat document bare keys such as char_name. Adapter coverage and sample exist under formats pygmalion and samples pygmalion classic.native.json. Do not claim no import adapter. Map char_name text and char_persona world_scenario char_greeting example_dialogue textareas as the five classic prose fields. metadata version created modified source tool is original only. PNG carrier flat JSON in chara chunk is supported via shared card-io. There is no pygmalion_id that lead was wrong. No scripts lorebook assets or social on classic wire. ooba multi-alias and YAML cousins are other format rows not this adapter.

Read and write through src formats sillytavern. Empty extensions is fine. Do not invent host namespaces under extensions for dropped or skipped platforms.

### bunzai documentation

- id: `reference/README`
- path: `docs/reference/README.md`
- Reference documentation index for bunzai, the Vaudeville Studios converter for AI-roleplay content. Docs-as-code: lives in the repo, versioned with the code, updated in the same change that adds or alters a feature. If a doc and the code disagree the code is right and the doc is a bug to file. The contract: based in truth with claims checked against source field tables detect scores and mapping quirks, planned-but-not-built labeled, updated per feature in the same commit, one concept per file mirroring house code rules. Map routes to architecture engine start page, entities character and lorebook supersets, concepts character-book embed extract, formats README coverage matrix and per-format family pages. How to read the field tables: Field is canonical name in vaud-json output, Type is TypeScript from entities schema, Meaning is roleplay client use, Formats lists producers and consumers; a field with no producer would not exist as a canonical slot per escrow rule in architecture. Schema file is type truth; this doc is meaning truth.

- **H2 The contract** (`the-contract`)
  Based in truth: claims checked against source; field tables detect scores and quirks mirror real code; no aspirational behavior; planned-but-not-built labeled. Updated per feature in the same commit: new format needs formats page plus matrix row; new field needs entities row; new concept needs concepts page. One concept per file, mirroring the code house rule.
- **H2 Map** (`map`)
  Start at architecture for the engine: canonical escrow adapters kinds registry folders bundles CLI. glossary gives project words, traps, owning folders, and the code map. entities character and lorebook document supersets. concepts character-book covers embed extract re-embed. macro-engine covers the ADR-012 closed-world interpreter: parser, bounded evaluator, segment contract. token-meter covers the counter that learns each model from reported usage. formats README is the coverage matrix; formats star pages cover detection maps escrow quirks and sources of truth.
- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field is the canonical name in vaud-json output. Type is TypeScript from entities kind schema. Meaning is roleplay client use. Formats lists producers and consumers. No producer means no canonical slot per architecture escrow. Schema is type truth; this doc is meaning truth.

### Scene state

- id: `reference/scene-state`
- path: `docs/reference/scene-state.md`
- Scene state is what a scene remembers beyond its text, kept so that regenerating, choosing a version, cutting, restoring and forking all leave it where the shown history says. A piece of state is a slot declared in one file and listed by a generator. What one reply version changed in a slot is a delta, a value at a path rather than an operation, stored on that version by swipe id so a fork shares its prefix's deltas without copying them. The state is rebuilt from the shown versions, from the nearest keyframe, inside the transaction of whatever changed the history. Slots are single files (the macro variables and the chat summary), one list made from the module table (the Props trackers, whose Story Director updates, Spellbook tick and the person's edits all go through the same two write doors), and two families that serve the library trackers a scene links, whose state a tracker's update tool writes as pending deltas on the reply being written. Its sections cover slots, deltas and the three strategies, storage, rebuild, how a turn uses it (with the variables route and the chat import), the Props trackers, the chat summary, global variables derived from every scene's shown versions, who may write each slot, what is not built yet, and what is tested. A range read of the same data shows what each message changed.

- **H2 Slots** (`slots`)
  A slot is one file under src/core/scene-state/slots that exports defineSlot with an id, a scope, a diff strategy and a default. defineSlot refuses an id outside the allowed pattern and a keyed-list without a key. The command bun run slots writes the generated list from the folder, slots:check fails when it is stale, and a completeness test fails when a slot file is missing from the list or an id repeats. The only scope is scene, meaning rebuilt from history. The vars slot uses the fields strategy, so each variable keeps the time it was first written and the time it was last written, which is exactly what the macro engine's variable record holds. A slot may declare a shape, the rule every value written to it passes, so the stores refuse a malformed value and an undeclared slot. The summary slot is described in its own section, and the Props trackers' slots are the first child below. The family form, for state made by library pieces, is the child section below.
  - **H3 The Props trackers' slots** (`the-props-trackers-slots`)
    Each Props tracker module that keeps state, the twelve legacy trackers, has one slot named tracker.module, made from the module table by one slot file that exports a list; the four Storyboards keep only a setting. A module with a list of rows keeps one wrapper row per list row and per other field in a keyed-list slot; a module with no list keeps one field per delta. A slot's shape comes from the module's own shapes. Whole-state rules stay with the module's parse, and each module has one repair step in its read that keeps every field it can and brings a broken rule inside, so a cut never makes a read show the defaults.
  - **H3 Slot families** (`slot-families`)
    A slot id ending in a colon and a star is a family covering every id with that prefix, so state made by a library piece at run time needs no file of its own and the store never sees library pieces. The slot lookup gives the exact slot if there is one, else the family whose prefix the id has. A family id is one lowercase word, a colon and a star; its file is named without them, and overlapping or repeated families fail the generator. Two exist, tracker-record with the fields strategy and tracker-list with the keyed-list strategy keyed by name; a tracker's own value shape is checked in the core tracker module.
- **H2 Deltas and the three strategies** (`deltas-and-the-three-strategies`)
  A delta names a slot, a path inside it, a value or a removal marker, and the time of the write. It is always a value, never an operation, so cutting a middle message takes back exactly what that message set and nothing a later message set again. Three strategies cut and fold deltas: value keeps the whole value, fields keeps one delta per named field, and keyed-list keeps one delta per row found by a key field. Fields holds the variables, keyed-list holds the summary and the linked list trackers, and all three are implemented and tested. Fold skips a delta for an unknown slot or with a path its strategy cannot read, and the stores refuse such a delta outright. A variable named like an Object property is an ordinary own property.
- **H2 Storage** (`storage`)
  Migration 43 adds five tables and an index. swipe_deltas holds one row per delta keyed by swipe id and sequence and is never changed once written, so a fork that keeps swipe ids shares its prefix's deltas. message_deltas holds what a message carries, and migration 44 adds a swipe id to it: null is a person's edit, and a swipe id is a write made after a commit that names its version, kept with this take's message so a fork copies it. branch_base is what a take starts with, real data that refuses when damaged. branch_state is each take's tip cache, and state_keyframes keeps the whole state after every fiftieth shown message; both are caches rebuilt when unreadable. Paths, values, keyframes and pending deltas are sealed like other chat payloads. The tables join the row fingerprints, the health check and the snapshot lists. A version has no size cap on its deltas; only what an import or a person's edit brings meets a hostile-input ceiling.
- **H2 Rebuild** (`rebuild`)
  The store owns the rebuild and runs it inside the transaction of the change, so no caller can forget it. It starts from the nearest kept keyframe on the shown path, else the take's base, else nothing, folds the deltas of the shown versions after it in order, rewrites the keyframes it passes and writes the tip cache. Committing a reply, adding or choosing a version, and cutting or restoring each rebuild from the message they changed; a fork rebuilds the new take, and deleting a take drops its cache. recordDeltas keeps a write that names a version with this take's message and rebuilds that take only. A continue stamps no delta, and a failed turn has stamped nothing. sceneStateBefore answers what a new version of a message builds from. sceneDeltas is a range read of the same data: the state before the first message without its own deltas, and each live message's deltas in fold order, changing nothing.
- **H2 How a turn uses it** (`how-a-turn-uses-it`)
  loadTurn reads the variables from sceneState for a new reply and from sceneStateBefore for a new version or a continue, so a regenerate never counts its own earlier writes twice. Assembly returns the local variable changes, and scene-turn turns them into vars deltas saved on the checkpoint with the trace; commitTurn stamps them on the version it stores and rebuilds the take. A continue makes no deltas. The library's global variables ride the same pending set, so nothing is written before the request. The trackers' update tool and the skills' open_skill and close_skill add deltas mid-reply through the turn's one door, for the tracker slots and the skills slot only, as the loop's stateScoped writes; a skill's close rule closes it with the turn's own deltas. A write of a value the message already shows, its version's deltas included, adds nothing, so a person's tracker edit back to the value before a reply is kept on the tip message.
- **H2 The Props trackers** (`the-props-trackers`)
  Every writer of a module's state goes through the two doors. The turn's own writes wait on the checkpoint and are stamped when the reply commits; the Spellbook's tick is computed in the turn, never on Stop or Continue, so a new version ticks once and a cut takes the tick with the reply. The Story Director's background round writes for the reply's version with recordDeltas, guarded by what it planned against; a field the person edited on that reply stays theirs, and the module's read repair never overrides that edit, so the module's update is skipped with a line in the activity. The person writes on the newest reply with recordMessageDeltas, several modules all or nothing, guarded by the stored values so a field the repair adjusted can still be edited. Anything the person clicks or accepts is that write, a cast rides only the next reply, and Undo writes the earlier data as the person's own edit.
- **H2 The chat summary** (`the-chat-summary`)
  The summary slot is a keyed-list with a declared row shape and two layers that never write the same path. Generated pieces are rows keyed by the swipe id of the version they end at, written after the reply lands on that version in this take: an Every-exchange line is the row of its reply, and a rolling recap, growing timeline or backfill is a replacing block that removes the other generated rows, so regenerating or cutting its message gives them back. The person's text is one separate row written as an edit of the newest message, with a fresh edit id and the next number on every save. Each generated row records the id and number it saw, which orders the layers without a clock: rows that saw an older text are covered however many there are, and rows written after, or that saw a newer save since deleted, show. A fork inherits the prefix and its writes stay in the fork.
- **H2 Global variables** (`global-variables`)
  The library's global variables are derived from every scene at once. A reply's global writes are operations (set, inc, append or delete) on the version that made them in the shared global slot, stamped at commit, so a failed or dropped turn writes none and a continue writes none of its own. A take's own fold skips a shared slot. A global's value is the fold, in write order, of the operations of every version shown in at least one live take, each counted once however many takes show it, leaving out cut versions and deleted takes or scenes. A new version of a reply builds without the version it replaces. Each operation is the write as the macro or lore Side Effect made it, so a set stays a set and two scenes that add to one number both count; a stored row that is not an operation refuses the read. The old variables document is no longer used, and the read is measured at about 110 ms for 10,000 operations, with no cache.
- **H2 Who may write** (`who-may-write`)
  Every slot names who may write it, and every store door is one writer decided by the door, never by a request: a turn's checkpoint is the reply, recordDeltas a background writer such as the Story Director or the summary's writer, recordMessageDeltas the person, and setBranchBase an import. A delta whose slot does not name that writer refuses the whole write with a sentence. A guard test holds who may call each door and the only places that insert delta rows. Two separations sit above the store: the background class covers both the Story Director and the summary's writer, kept apart by the guard on who calls recordDeltas, and a model's tool is held to library tracker slots by the turn's write door. The Compendium writes lorebooks after checking each book's access level, so it needs no slot. A wrong write is undone by a swipe or a cut.
- **H2 Not built yet** (`not-built-yet`)
  Planned in the scene-state plan and absent from the code: lorebooks copied on fork (built and taken back out, waiting on decisions about per-scene settings, the Compendium wing and copies in the Library), a person's own edit of a global variable, the extension contract and a per-slot tier, which wait for an extension runtime and its manifest, the Story Director updating library trackers, hand edits of any slot other than a library tracker's, a Props tracker's and the summary, and a state inspector. The person's write door is recordMessageDeltas, called by the library tracker edit, the Props tracker write (an accepted Refresh included) and the person's summary text. The door for a write that names a version after its commit is recordDeltas, called by the chat summary's writer and the Story Director's tracker round. A slot's declared shape is checked on every delta either door takes, and a guard test holds the list of callers.
- **H2 What is tested** (`what-is-tested`)
  Unit proofs cover each strategy, the fold and a keyframe rebuild. The contract suite runs on both stores: versions, cuts, restores, forks, turns, refusals and a 2,000-message rebuild held to a 200 ms budget. The SQLite file adds shared delta rows, keyframes, sealing and the dense case. A busy-library test runs a real turn with the write lock held. The Props trackers have their own contract suite on both stores, a core test of cutting into deltas and back, turn tests for the prompt and the Spellbook's tick, two Director round suites, and a suite for the person's write over a repaired state. The chat summary, the range read and the global variables have their own suites, the macro engine is held to recording each global write as the operation it was, and who may write is held on both stores and by a guard on who calls each door. A guard test holds recordDeltas to its callers: the two stores, their contract files including the tracker-state one, the summary's writer and the Director's tracker round.

### The token meter

- id: `reference/token-meter`
- path: `docs/reference/token-meter.md`
- The token meter in src/core/tokens is a pure counter that learns each model from the usage its replies report. It counts every prompt with gpt-tokenizer's o200k base and fits, per model, how that count maps onto the provider's reported prompt total, cache reads and writes included. It gives a midpoint for display and an upper bound for budgeting. It reads every major usage shape without inventing zeros, predicts provider caching and prices it, and trims history in cache-friendly steps. Nothing feeds it yet; the turn loop will. Its learned numbers are stored as the StateStore's meter document, numbers only.

- **H2 Why it learns** (`why-it-learns`)
  No local tokenizer is exact for most models: Anthropic's current tokenizer has no open-source build, Gemini counts exactly only over the network, and each open-weight family has its own vocabulary. Every reply reports what its whole prompt cost, though. So the meter counts each prompt one cheap, consistent way and learns per model how that count maps onto the reported one, getting closer the more you generate, for any model on any provider.
- **H2 The pieces** (`the-pieces`)
  The base counter runs o200k on each message's exact text, split into prose, code, JSON and CJK, treats special-token text as text, and adds 4 tokens of framing per message. countText, the raw total, owns one bounded content-hash cache every caller shares (lore, prompt trace, editor badges), so unchanged text is never re-encoded; countMessage layers its own exact-text LRU on top for the class split. The usage reader maps every major provider shape into one record whose total counts cached tokens once. The fits use recursive least squares from a family prior, with a bound for report wander and fit uncertainty. A cached slice is learned as a second measurement. Reports under half the base are refused, early high reports wait for repeats, and a tokenizer change is re-learned. The meter document holds numbers only. Both token caches empty when the library locks, and a gate keeps them from being read or filled while it stays locked, so a turn that finishes after a lock never caches decrypted text.
- **H2 What is tested** (`what-is-tested`)
  Usage fixtures are built from provider documentation and say so. Synthetic models with a known tokenizer and seeded noise, across 200 seeds, prove the bound covers the truth on at least 97 percent of fresh prompts from zero replies on, including models above their prior, small prompts, normal noise, many short messages, and noisier models checked seed by seed. The bound never overcounts past a sendable multiple of its midpoint. Cached parts, breakpoint or automatic, with hits on older entries, never push a bound under the truth. Each smaller rule has its own test.

### The visual app (bunzai ui)

- id: `reference/ui`
- path: `docs/reference/ui.md`
- Contributor map of installed Studio, temporary Browser Studio, opt-in remote listeners and Kit. GitHub Pages reuses the React shell, adapters, shared engine and documentation with memory-only state and explicit refusal of host-owned operations. Its public landing is a cutout auditorium with click-to-enter dimming and zoom, a photographed tour of scenes and editors, distinct descriptions and full-resolution links. Paper is its single theme example. Node-board slides are excluded. Repository links and static Open Graph metadata support sharing. Reduced motion and interaction suspend animation. Kit owns conversational composition, sessions, providers, capability tools and staged review. Folder-derived apps, setup, menus and documentation define extension points. Infrastructure covers reload, provenance, storage, receipts and revision-checked writes. Library owns import, staging, damaged-file reporting, restorable Trash and composed book-cover presentation. Workbench provides canonical editors, split panes, linked sets and explicit saves. Scenes owns character-started chats, display macros, streaming, picture/list layouts, favorites and participant forms. The Context wing combines Prompt Inspector and Memory. Press owns staged export, linked riders, delivery and loss reporting. Settings derives its sections from folders and provides stage-light choices. One button family owns clickable controls. Security distinguishes loopback, browser and remote boundaries with fail-closed parsing, inert scripts and sanitized marks. The installed velvet landing supplies real Now Playing data, Dock navigation, a proscenium and capped lights and fog. Verification claims distinguish fixture, live and packaged evidence.

- **H2 Velvet premiere landing** (`velvet-premiere-landing`)
  Fresh launches show the approved velvet theater landing with locally shipped panoramic, standard and portrait artwork, selected by pane container queries. A 90% overlay dims the artwork beneath live HTML branding, navigation and actions. Continue resumes the most recently updated scene; Now Playing reads the real scene API and handles empty or failed reads. A reusable black-and-ruby proscenium surrounds frozen scene artwork and HTML controls. Quick Start routes to Library, its import signal and Scenes. The Dock and app shortcuts remain usable and leave the landing. Existing boot rules still skip the title for restored workspaces, reloads, direct links and setup. Two OGL beams and roiling fog share a capped canvas that suspends while hidden. Mouse following uses 35% influence, touch is ignored. The rays appear only here and on the scene chooser. Settings Stage lights chooses Moving, Still (one fixed frame) or Off (nothing drawn); Reduce motion makes Moving act as Still. WebGL failure uses CSS fallbacks.
- **H2 Bottom app navigation** (`bottom-app-navigation`)
  The bottom dock follows RoleCall geometry, with an accent glow and a collapsible edge pill. Every available discovered app appears; unavailable apps are excluded, with Settings and Overlay at the end. Tiles navigate the workspace. The dock sits above apps and below dialogs; its measured bottom reserve clears while hidden. Hidden controls remain inert and outside the tab order. Arrow keys, Home and End move focus. Theme slots remain decorative. Overlay and Ctrl+K or Cmd+K open the temporary overlay with theme and piece counts. The contextual status line holds real messages, failures and reload warnings, disappearing when empty. Narrow shells retain safe-area placement, 44px targets and the selected app in view. Coarse-pointer text focus hides the dock; physical-phone keyboard behavior is unverified.
- **H2 Browser Studio** (`browser-studio`)
  GitHub Pages publishes the public website at its root and the React Studio under studio/. The landing is a cutout auditorium with click-to-enter dimming and zoom, real Scenes and editor screenshots, distinct descriptions and full-resolution links. Paper is the single theme example; node boards are excluded. Header links open GitHub for starring or browsing. Static Open Graph metadata provides canonical URLs and a branded PNG preview; external preview caches remain outside site control. Motion respects interaction, visibility and reduced motion. A separate info page holds details, guides, releases and downloads. Browser Studio reuses discovered apps, setup, adapters, inspect/export actions and documentation without a separate conversion model. Pieces, preferences, previews and portraits remain in memory for the current load; export before reload. Its library gate opens unlocked. Kit, MCP, remote controls, updates, engine checkouts, Lumiverse archives and script execution require the installed app. Static Pages cannot supply the isolated execution origin required by ADR-009.
- **H2 Kit terminal shell** (`kit-terminal-shell`)
  Kit is a terminal shell with a multiline composer, paste cards, bounded follow-up queue, draft-preserving history and search, interruptible streaming, and argument-completing palette. Right-click pastes text; Alt+V attaches readable images or displays them without sending; paths in messages link only to those files. Native scrolling, foldable replies, copy feedback and stage watching support terminal use. Sessions resume, rewind, fork and export; model settings, doctor, inventory and privacy are commands. /rail keeps a preset block list beside conversation: ctrl+B focuses it, ctrl+shift+arrows resize, ctrl+shift+D changes density, and < or > pages presets. Rail content enters ambient context; Enter stages edits as one draft at the same Gate. Bounded tool reads replace model save prose with revision-checked REVIEW CHANGE; conversion uses REVIEW CROSSING. Held calls wait at the Gate and applies serialize. /privacy reports recorded model calls and token counts, not all network activity; the greeting discloses transmission to the selected provider and connected services.
- **H2 Hyper-modularity (the build's spine)** (`hyper-modularity-the-builds-spine`)
  UI applications are drop-in folders exporting a BunzaiApp manifest and component. Bun discovery skips private underscore folders, bundles apps on demand, puts ordinary apps on the Dock, and keeps catalogOnly tools available through the catalog without a central roster. Apps receive AppContext and may reach engine or filesystem behavior only through its API; the shell owns global navigation, tabs, theme, and status. Visual law comes from one token sheet rather than app-local colors or fixed layout. Bright accents remain marks, borders, and spines; any labeled accent fill uses a generated deep companion with stage-white text. Rose, house accent, deck, and per-piece pairs share tested deepening logic.
- **H2 First-run setup (the wizard)** (`first-run-setup-the-wizard`)
  The wizard reuses the bunny-b app icon. First run is assembled from drop-in SetupStep folders that declare question, options, settings key, widget, stage treatment and recap; the wizard derives order, progress, defaults and summary from discovery. Settings are an open record parsed fail-closed per key and stored under the studio directory. Missing setupComplete opens the wizard; completion applies theme and house accent and the first landing, and later launches show the title screen and land on Scenes. Settings > Appearance offers host-verified bundled themes (Paper, GirliePop as stage, Theater) and approved data-only theme packages, with digest preview, disable, revoke, restore and remove; Encore ships uninstalled as an example. The saved theme is only a request: the shell paints the neutral core appearance until /api/themes/effective verifies it, and a local cache never activates one. Serialized PATCH writes merge concurrent changes. Boot paints the landing before the piece list; list loads retry after 2, 5 and 15 seconds, and a lasting failure leaves a sticky status note.
- **H2 The right-click system** (`the-right-click-system`)
  One shared context-menu service grows through registration. Surfaces attach typed targets and data to elements, with the deepest matching element winning and a shell-level target always available. Features register any number of item providers for a target type; each provider contributes a section, an empty result denies by absence, and cleanup receives an unregister function. Shell providers currently cover entity actions such as send, show, open beside, split close, and Workbench removal, plus Dock-app and shell navigation actions. A new feature contributes one provider instead of editing a central switch, preserving room-independent behavior for common entity operations.
- **H2 Apps catalog and documentation** (`apps-catalog-and-documentation`)
  The Dock's Add app control discovers the manifest-marked catalog instead of naming an id, and the catalog derives cards from the running app roster. catalogOnly tools such as CSS Workshop and Help/Docs remain bundled without occupying everyday Dock space. Help/Docs reads the committed corpus through the generated docs index, which can merge frontmatter, semantic page summaries, retrieval topics, and nested section anchors. Navigation separates user workflows and platforms from developer API, architecture, data, format, security, extension, and decision material; ADR filenames remain canonical while titles are reader-friendly. Markdown uses the shared sanitizer, figures and images are restricted to generated or committed docs assets, the right rail follows current headings, and packaged content matches GitHub. External GitHub viewing passes through the leaving gate and URL allowlist. The catalog room is a room header over one framed card per app, marked On your Dock or Included app.
- **H2 Dev live-reload** (`dev-live-reload`)
  Development Studio watches all of src (test files excluded) and broadcasts changes to every open page over the /dev/reload server-sent-event stream. Browser bundles are memoized and rebuilt only when the watcher sees an edit; the Browser shortcut serves production React while bun run dev keeps development React. Packaged executables serve baked assets, return 404 for the development stream, and let the client become quiet without a separate dependency.
- **H2 The card-type chip** (`the-card-type-chip`)
  Library summaries derive sourceFormat and sourceVariant from the first non-Bunzai escrow record. Labels come from the live format registry rather than a duplicated UI map: platform-specific adapters show platform and variant; generic Tavern/CC content shows CC plus the spec version on characters and no chip on other kinds, since CC means character card; pieces authored without a source adapter display no chip. The badge describes provenance without inventing one.
- **H2 The studio store** (`the-studio-store`)
  StudioStore is the Studio view of the encrypted library.db: versioned pieces with a 3-day Trash and retired deleted ids, and Studio documents in the StateStore. A plain folder is imported once; bunzai export writes it back. Calls answer 423 while locked. Saves never overwrite silently; inventory reports damaged rows. Inventory, list and graveyard read sealed summaries, not bodies, falling back to a body only when a summary is missing or stale. Full editor loads use the stored revision from the same stable read as hydrated art, so reopening uploaded pictures does not cause false save conflicts; concurrent changes still refuse writes. This path is HTTP-regression and live-browser proven.
- **H2 The receipt** (`the-receipt`)
  The server renders one plain-language import receipt for both shells, ensuring terminal and visual users receive the same explanation. It uses recognizable platform names, a we-read/we-kept voice, and emits conditional lines only when engine evidence supports embedded books, scripts-as-data, or privileged warnings. Unknown files receive a warm failure message. Receipt rendering adds presentation, not new detection or format logic.
- **H2 Endpoints** (`endpoints`)
  The endpoint map covers authenticated Studio reads and writes, docs, staging, imports, scenes, agents and settings. Light reads retain sealed media references and expose targeted escrow and native metadata; patch saves require the opened revision and return committed identity. A stored Sprite pack prints as a zip through GET export-pack, one picture at a time, and duplicates by reference through POST duplicate, so a big pack never crosses the wire. Local staged uploads are bounded and inert; remote and LAN writes are refused. Asset GET and HEAD and pinned portrait routes compose with the same library lock lifetime. A dropped chat .jsonl rides the same inspect, save-bundle and save-staged routes: its row carries a scene carrier, never a library piece, and a refusal such as a card missing from the library answers ok false with 422 so the sheet lists it by file. Chat rows commit after cards and personas. Source-reviewed implementation and live proof are distinguished.
- **H2 The Library room (the shelves - deep browse)** (`the-library-room-the-shelves---deep-browse`)
  Library shows first-run doors when empty and, when populated, RoleCall's /library layout: a rail with RC's Carousel/Grid switch, deck rows with live counts, collections, and Add, folding into chips below 48rem of pane width. Each non-empty deck gets a numbered section of RC's cards; every section header switches its deck among Cards and the drop-in views. Cards carry the shelf tools; a New tile ends each row. The art dial defaults to 15.75rem-wide cards, search reaches tags, and ranges, Select all, and the agent walk one drawn order; layout, order, views, and size persist per user. Start Scene under a character card opens a new scene. Move to Trash keeps a piece 3 days, frees its name at once, and retires its id; the Trash panel holds deleted pieces, cut scenes, and deleted takes with the time left, and removed versions of replies marked kept until restored, filtered All, Pieces, or Scenes, and Restore brings each back whole.
- **H2 Scenes (the library's chats)** (`scenes-the-librarys-chats`)
  Scenes opens encrypted chats with cast snapshots, takes, cuts and swipes. Full Chat and Messenger reuse wing owners for writing, lore, passes and appearance. Control Booth applies loadouts with receipts and Undo. Tracker state and summaries follow reply versions; rail Props reuse dockable panels. Optional Dramaturg stores a revision-guarded, sealed consultant conversation separate from scene messages. Six read-only tools inspect messages, lore and presets. Shared runtime owns cancellation and shutdown; input estimates and reply caps refuse overflow before egress. Its cache holds four scenes within 32 MiB, clears on lock or app unmount and rejects stale responses. Deterministic-provider UI checks cover tools, reload persistence and floating panels. Narrow scenes use compact portraits and tap actions. Across themes the phone composer uses 16px text and a 5.5rem cap; unchanged drafts refit through a shared inline-size observer. Live browser checks cover 375px RoleCall and Paper, editing and bidirectional resize. Physical phones, installed apps, real models and packaged behavior remain unverified. HTTP refusals cap bodies at 64 KiB, cancelling oversized reads and preserving status. Busy polling pauses in hidden tabs.
  - **H3 Stage Gallery** (`stage-gallery`)
    The Stage wing's Gallery tab ports RoleCall's Image Gallery as a two-column grid of square tiles over the scene's own art: the frozen portrait, the card's extra art and background, the persona's portrait and one pack's pictures, never a link. Tiles use 256 thumbnails and a virtualized window, the panel counts pictures, and the enlarged view offers Download and Use as background, which copies cast art into the scene's pack before setting the backdrop and reports a failed copy without changing it. Generated Received images are left out, and the strip shows no count chip.
  - **H3 Stage Wardrobe** (`stage-wardrobe`)
    The Stage wing's Wardrobe tab ports RoleCall's WardrobeWingPanel and is shown for every scene, since Bunzai has no beta tier. It hangs the message themes the scene's regex sets carry: display rules whose replacement opens with a style block. An outfit is paired with the preset choice option that carries its name, and its chip writes that choice answer. Unclaimed outfits are loose garments. A standalone dress is worn or hung by switching its set for the scene. The look is the scene's display regex in the sandbox worker. A hung dress stays on the scene's own rack across reloads, fixing a RoleCall flaw.
  - **H3 Scene theme variants** (`scene-theme-variants`)
    A theme selects one host-built variant for each of six scene surfaces (layout, message, composer, swipes, wings, hud); variants are CSS keyed on the root surface attribute, with markup differing only for version dots, wing labels and the HUD readout. Every control stays present in every variant.
- **H2 The Workbench room (home by default - the IDE)** (`the-workbench-room-home-by-default---the-ide`)
  Workbench owns persistent tabs, follow policy, split view, recents, dirty-close confirmation and drafts. Editors use light baselines, serialized revision-checked patches, conflict choices and bounded history. Lore folders share undo/save ownership; named assets travel with accepted submissions. Complete exports read full entities. Session-granted uploads are revoked on lock or close; pictures are capped at 10 MiB. Measured lists retain focus and selection. Timer autosaves group by five minutes; manual saves keep version boundaries. Character editing defaults to Grid, with a guided quiz; preset groups form a category tree, lorebooks focus one entry, quick replies are ordered messages, and tracker/tool/dice/formula editors share decoded saves. Studio Strip groups identity and save state above compact platform, mode, view and completion controls. View options retains arrangement, Workshop and scale; Desk/Nodes, focus, Save and Export remain reachable. Controls wrap to their container and preserve theme layout locks. Empty or failed benches show a framed room header. Prior storage paths retain their source-review and pending-live-proof limits.
  - **H3 Editor theme variants** (`editor-theme-variants`)
    editor.frame rearranges eight editors: character, persona, sprite pack, HTML doc, preset, lorebook, regex and quick reply. Each editor declares its sections and hands them to the shared EditorLayout, which renders a two-pane section list, a numbered tabbed menu, a stack of section cards, or one long document with a contents margin. Closed sections stay mounted so unsaved input survives switching, and picking a regex rule or lorebook entry opens its page section. With no pick each editor keeps its own layout, and the character Bento and Playbill choice is disabled while a theme frame is active. editor.fields styles every field drawn through EditorField, except the header name.
  - **H3 Nodes view** (`nodes-view`)
    Desk and Nodes share open pieces without losing drafts. The canvas has Links, Pipeline and Board layers. Links edit stored references through single-field saves and respect unsaved Desk edits. Pipeline opens presets into blocks for toggling and reordering and wires cast and Lore pass into markers. Feature placement controls appear for placed markers; Feature placements reveals unused controls. Hidden controls do not affect Fit or the minimap. Only actual preset markers receive feature wires; controls do not indicate activity or enable features. Dragging a placement port onto a block places its marker; Delete on its wire switches it off. A Request node previews the forecast or a dry run; board items never export.
- **H2 The Press room (batch export - the staged set prints)** (`the-press-room-batch-export---the-staged-set-prints`)
  The Press consumes a shell-owned staged queue from Library or its offer rail, preserving staging across app switches. Linked lorebooks ride with characters; preset behaviorRefs and quickReplyRefs ride as regex and quick-reply sets. Riders can be dropped for one run without changing canonical links. Readiness uses the target coverage declaration and identifies lorebook entries that cannot fire. Each run chooses a platform, declares names and skips, and reports each row. Packs print by reference, one picture at a time; unreadable pictures fail their row. One output is delivered bare, while multiple outputs form a ZIP. Installed exports share fresh timestamped Export folders with Kit and the single-piece dialog. Incomplete copies report failure and leave the download available; remote and Browser Studio clients download locally. Each bundle or solo remains a framed panel edged by its deck color.
- **H2 The Agent window (the model, standing on the screen you came from)** (`the-agent-window-the-model-standing-on-the-screen-you-came-from`)
  A conversation with whatever model the vault holds, carrying the real tool belt. The window owns no provider and no tools: it asks the loopback server, which asks Kit, so there is one vault, one egress ledger and one Gate. Before a write, the Gate opens a host-owned dark-ground alert dialog in the native top layer; the server dispatch loop waits for the person's answer before applying the write. It sees the screen because each app publishes an agentSurface and the mounted app adds what is on it now, joined into a brief that truncates a describe at 300 characters. That snapshot travels through the context both ways, never by importing live-state: every app is bundled separately, so its own import is a second empty copy. The shell publishes a bare snapshot as any app mounts. Replies are drawn from React elements, never from an HTML string. The docked room is one framed panel under a room header showing the model and the screen it stands on; the floating panel keeps its own head.
- **H2 CSS Workshop (catalog-only - assisted CSS authoring)** (`css-workshop-catalog-only---assisted-css-authoring`)
  A catalog-only room for authoring the CSS a card or a host will carry, rather than the CSS this app is built from. It offers starters to begin from, property knobs for the common adjustments, the plain CSS source for anything the knobs do not reach, and a sealed preview showing the result. The output leaves by copy or download for any host, and the room writes no studio state of its own, so nothing here is a piece and nothing needs saving. The preview uses the same seal every untrusted-markup surface in this app uses, which is what lets it show authored CSS without that CSS reaching the application around it. The room header holds only copy, download, clear and tour; the Simple and Advanced switch, Import .css and Undo live in the editor toolbar.
- **H2 Help / Docs (catalog-only - the corpus, in the app)** (`help-docs-catalog-only---the-corpus-in-the-app`)
  The committed documentation corpus is a first-class room, read from the same source GitHub serves so the app and repository cannot disagree about a page. It reads docs/generated/docs-index.json rather than crawling the tree; every discovered Markdown page keeps its structural title, summary, and headings in the app. A semantic sidecar enriches that record only when its current receipt independently approves it. Until then, the page remains listed with semantic summary and topics absent, while docs-index:check reports incomplete review status. The reader starts as one mobile article column: Browse and Sections open the shared bottom sheet, which contains keyboard focus, closes on Escape, and restores the trigger. Container breakpoints add the catalog rail at 40rem and the current-page heading rail at 62rem, making the same room adapt correctly inside a phone or split pane without viewport assumptions. The room opens under a Help / Docs header.
- **H2 The Macro Lab (catalog-only - what a macro actually does)** (`the-macro-lab-catalog-only---what-a-macro-actually-does`)
  A scratch pad for macro text and the only surface that needs no piece open to be useful. It keeps two answers apart on purpose. Reading is pure and local: each token is broken out against the chosen platform catalog, nested tokens report under their container, and where else does it work asks the translator rather than two catalogs, so a shared name meaning different things is a flagged collision instead of a clean trip. A token invoking no name is called that rather than supported, because isMacroSupported fails lenient by design. Resolving runs the text through the platform real engine as a one-block preset behind a host-only route, needs a local checkout pointed at in Settings or named by an environment variable, and refuses by name when absent. The bible lists every macro with click-to-insert, and the operations table derives from the catalogs with gaps sorted first. The room has its own header and a framed text panel; a dot marks platforms whose engine is installed.
- **H2 HTML View (catalog-only - a drawing at full size)** (`html-view-catalog-only---a-drawing-at-full-size`)
  A catalog-only app that draws one htmldoc piece as a whole tab. It is reached three ways: opened by id from the Library, handed a piece by another surface, or handed the raw text of a fenced html block from an agent reply. A piece travels by ID and is re-read from the studio, so the tab shows what the drawing says now; only an unsaved reply is carried whole, and the room says so rather than showing an empty frame. Everything drawn passes through SealedHtmlPreview, the component behind the transcript preview and the Workbench editor pane too, so one boundary serves three sizes with nothing to drift. The tab and the editor pane fill their room while the transcript keeps the inline ceiling, and either goes fullscreen, natively where allowed and as an overlay where not. All CSS renders, head styles included. JavaScript never runs, no remote image or webfont loads, and form controls are stripped, since the same component draws untrusted card backdrops. The header title is the drawing's name.
- **H2 Settings (drop-in sections)** (`settings-drop-in-sections`)
  Settings tabs derive from section modules and share AppContext persistence. The rail groups Your studio, The agent, The vault and The house, collapsing to chips in narrow panes; protected pages retain recognizable lock lines. Extensions owns package review, operation and appearance grants, enable/disable/revoke, private-data usage and redacted receipt activity. Effect requests use expiring host-owned review tickets, and contributed descriptors render through shared host controls rather than extension HTML or CSS. Appearance owns theme selection, accents, chat layout and stage lights; package lifecycle and scopes use Extensions. Studio owns home app, first deck, publish targets and engine roots. Models shares the provider vault with Kit; Connections manages MCP, and Spotify uses the owner's Client ID. Security owns locking, passwords, recovery and health checks; Backups owns copies and restore. Workbench, Memory and Summaries, Remote access, Updates and About remain separate sections. The library gate replaces the app while locked.
  - **H3 Settings theme variants** (`settings-theme-variants`)
    Settings theme variants restyle the page body, rows and segmented control through data-sx hooks. They no longer touch the rail of acts, which belongs to the room. Stylesheets live under the settings variants folder.
- **H2 Shared components (`src/ui/_shared/` and `src/ui/components/`)** (`shared-components-srcui_shared-and-srcuicomponents`)
  Every clickable control is a named part of the button family in src/ui/components/button (Button, IconButton, Segmented, Tabs, DocTab, Chip, ListRow, MenuItem, Disclosure, Tile, Card, Dot, Toggle, InlineValue, Scrim), each tagged with data-control (and data-kind where it has kinds) as theme hooks; lint blocks raw buttons and the style guard blocks button CSS elsewhere. Shared visual controls are extracted once for setup, settings, and future editors. SwatchRow exposes the house palette as quick choices and optionally opens PaintPicker for a custom solid color. ColorPicker provides a branded saturation/value square, hue strip, and hex input while retaining HSV internally to avoid grayscale-state loss. Pure color math normalizes and converts values fail-closed and shares pointer fractions across color and gradient controls. The Paint model is a validated discriminated union for solid, linear, and radial output; gradients require at least two valid stops. PaintPicker composes color editing, stop add/drag/remove, angle adjustment, and curved previews behind allowed-mode gates. Solid accent use is live; tested gradient editing awaits its first entity or pack background consumer.
  - **H3 House style (Premiere)** (`house-style-premiere`)
    Every room wears a room header with a gold kicker, a display-face title, one lede line and right-aligned actions. Groups sit in a single framed panel with an offset shadow and an optional piece-kind deck edge; a panel nested inside another flattens to its colored top edge, so frames never nest. The Workbench editor head adds a kind kicker and deck color. Colors come from the theme's brand, action and select roles, so rooms follow the user's accent. Monospace is reserved for code, ids and numbers, and the core UI font fallback is Atkinson Hyperlegible.
  - **H3 Control variants (`ui.controls`)** (`control-variants-uicontrols`)
    ui.controls is a global variant painted once into the head by the shell; it sets the button family offset and frame through the data-control hook on Button and IconButton, field frames and the focus ring, and every element rule excludes .host-protected. variant-css-check.ts holds the proofs each variant owes. The app.room surface rearranges the setup wizard, Press, Agent room and CSS Workshop per theme, and ui.empty frames every element marked data-empty, including the workbench empty room.
- **H2 Approved jewel Library and Scenes** (`approved-jewel-library-and-scenes`)
  Library and Scenes adopt the approved black surfaces, gilded rules and jewel accents; the other page proposals remain awaiting approval. Lorebooks without artwork get stable compositions from 60 transparent semantic PNG ornaments and ten painterly paper layers. Design book cover uses the existing lorebook menu to edit layers and cover title or restore original artwork. These are local Studio presentation preferences, separate from canonical and escrowed content, and are not baked into exports; imported artwork is retained by default. The catalog is derived from asset folders. Scenes persists Pictures or List through scenes.view. Recent lists have This Week and Earlier headings while message-ranked lists retain their ordering without date groups. Both layouts share the measured virtualizer, frozen art, selection and context menus; narrow rows retain time, take count, active take and a visible menu. The overview includes a Continue hero with real tail preview and a measured Fan Favorites shelf ordered by star time.
- **H2 Security** (`security`)
  Authenticated server routes enforce path and request boundaries; imported Lua/regex remain sealed data until user-invoked isolated test benches. Upload staging bounds formats, bytes, grant count and lifetime; inert SVG/CSS rules reject active payloads. Lock or close aborts active uploads and revokes grants. Library, mentions, status and recents share PieceStore; exact summary identity and lock lifetimes fence delayed metadata and recovery. These integrated paths are source-reviewed, with focused fixture results recorded in delivery evidence and live proof pending.

### Updates and version rollback

- id: `reference/updates`
- path: `docs/reference/updates.md`
- Developer authority for Settings Updates: a newest-first version timeline from each release commit log and a one-button switch that updates or rolls back. Reads GitHub only when the page opens or a button is pressed; nothing runs on its own. The timeline is read-only and live-proven: fixed GitHub URL releases fetch with cache and ETag, pure version-history parsing from fenced commits blocks only, and compareVersions so bare versions match v-prefixed tags. The switch is built and unit-proven while the full apply path is not yet live-provable: host-only POST validates real release tags single-flight returns 202 with status poll; source checkout refuses dirty tracked trees names files restores prior ref on failure; installed build downloads allowlisted asset verifies SHA256SUMS and stages without auto-swapping the running executable. Restart-outcome marker confirms next boot via pending endpoint. Rollback safety refuses crossing storage changes via classifyDataChange SCHEMA_BUMPS, the releases that changed library storage, and a storage-shape tripwire hash; a switch never writes the library.

- **H2 The timeline (read-only, live-proven)** (`the-timeline-read-only-live-proven`)
  GET /api/updates/releases fetches the repo releases from a fixed GitHub URL with no caller input in host or path so SSRF is out of scope. Five-minute in-memory cache plus ETag prevents burning unauthenticated hourly limits; 403 or 429 answers with try again in N min from server-updates. Pure core version-history.ts parses the payload into the timeline. Commit data comes only from a fenced commits block each release ships emitted by the release workflow as shorthash subject lines; a release without the block renders commit log unavailable never a guess from install-guide prose. Every version comparison goes through compareVersions so the bare running version still matches its v-prefixed tag.
- **H2 The switch (built + unit-proven; the apply path is not yet live-provable)** (`the-switch-built-unit-proven-the-apply-path-is-not-yet-live-provable`)
  POST /api/updates/switch is host-only refused to remote or LAN devices, validates a real release tag blocking force-shaped values before git argv, refuses current version, starts one switch behind single-flight lock, and returns 202 while the client polls switch-status. Source checkout refuses a dirty tracked tree and names files never stashes work, captures prior ref, checks out the tag, reinstalls, and relaunches; on failure restores prior ref and reinstalls. Installed build downloads the matching asset over https with GitHub-host allowlist on URL and every redirect hop plus size cap, verifies SHA256SUMS, stages and opens the folder, but does not swap the running executable because auto-swap can brick and is deferred. Restart-outcome marker is written before source relaunch so next boot confirms via GET pending which clears on read. Older rollbacks cannot show the popup so confirm becomes the receipt. Guard rails and timeline are live-proven; full apply is unit-tested not yet live-provable until shipped.
- **H2 Rollback safety** (`rollback-safety`)
  A rollback that crosses a release which changed how the studio is stored is refused by classifyDataChange and SCHEMA_BUMPS in switch-decision. SCHEMA_BUMPS lists each release that changed how the library is stored, and rolling back across one is refused because the older build cannot read what the newer one wrote. A storage-shape tripwire test pins a hash of the files that define on-disk shape, including entity schemas database migrations and library storage, so a storage change cannot ship without a deliberate schema bump or SCHEMA_BUMPS entry. A switch never writes the library, library.db or anything else in the studio folder; only the program changes.

## reference/concepts

### Bundles

- id: `reference/concepts/bundles`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/bundles.md`
- Bundles explain how a character card that embeds a lorebook becomes two linked canonical entities instead of one blob. On import the embedded book splits into a standalone CanonicalLorebook while the character keeps only body.knowledgeRefs id links; on export the target adapter re-embeds resolved lorebooks into its native slot. The page is the join mechanism and call-site lifetimes, not the CCv2/v3 wire map (see character-book) or the shared wrapper (see canonical-model). knowledgeRefs: the join field is an ordered id list distinct from worldName, which names an external worldbook by title. Import: extract at the boundary runs readPieces with shared extractCharacterBook or an adapter extractLorebook override such as Agnai. Export: re-embed at the boundary runs emitBundle with EmitContext lorebooks and multi-book merge when the wire has one slot. Two call sites, two lifetimes contrast the CLI one-shot convertFile path (readPieces, emitPieces) with Studio persist-resolve-export through core/links.ts. Source of truth tables convert.ts, adapter contracts, studio bundle, and CLI wiring.

- **H2 knowledgeRefs: the join field** (`knowledgerefs-the-join-field`)
  CanonicalCharacter body.knowledgeRefs is an ordered string array of linked lorebook ids that export adapters embed when the format requires it. It is not worldName: worldName is the creator intent to auto-load an external worldbook by name, while knowledgeRefs links an embedded or library book by canonical id. The field is a list because KnowledgeRail lets authors attach, detach, and reorder multiple library books on one character; attachKnowledgeRef appends only if absent. Import extraction is narrower: a single card import yields at most one extracted book, so ConvertResult.lorebooks is zero or one entry per pass. Multi-book knowledgeRefs lists are therefore Studio-authored, never produced alone by one import, and they are exactly what forces export-side merge when the target wire supports only one character_book slot.
- **H2 Import: extract at the boundary** (`import-extract-at-the-boundary`)
  readPieces runs once per import. First source.toCanonical leaves any embedded book inside entity.original in the source raw shape. Extraction then chooses: if CharacterAdapter implements extractLorebook for a dialect the shared mapper cannot read, that override runs; otherwise extractCharacterBook on primaryOriginalRaw handles only CCv2/v3 character_book. Finally knowledgeRefs is set to the new lorebook id. Agnai is the concrete override case with native characterBook MemoryBook and its standalone lorebook codec; SillyTavern, RoleCall, and Risu fall through to the shared path. The shared extractor also escrow-stashes the raw book on the lorebook under key character-book, not the host format id, so later standalone export can still overlay that twin. Wire field mapping lives on the character-book concept page.
- **H2 Export: re-embed at the boundary** (`export-re-embed-at-the-boundary`)
  Extraction is shared; re-embedding belongs to the target adapter. emitBundle builds EmitContext with resolved lorebooks and passes it to target.fromCanonical. EmitContext is optional and cross-entity, resolved by CLI or Studio, never by core. Adapters that embed knowledge write context.lorebooks into their slot; formats without embedding or that only use worldName ignore it. Three-way contract: omitted lorebooks leaves the twin alone; nonempty list replaces the embedded book; empty list clears both CCv2/v3 slots so resolution stays authoritative without breaking direct same-format adapter round trips. When multiple linked books hit a single slot, lorebooksToCharacterBook collapses them: one ref overlays or encodes that book; many refs concatenate entries under the first book's settings and record names plus entry counts in extensions.vaud_source_books so the split stays recoverable.
- **H2 Two call sites, two lifetimes** (`two-call-sites-two-lifetimes`)
  knowledgeRefs is the same field, but resolution differs by caller. The CLI bunzai convert path is one ephemeral convertFile pass: readPieces then emitPieces in memory, no lorebook file on disk; if the target embeds knowledge the book rides inside the single output and CLI only reports entry counts. The Studio persists character and lorebook as separate Library entities. inspectBytes returns the piece and its related pieces; save-bundle writes the related pieces first, records keep-both renames and rewrites every link field naming a renamed piece with withLinksRenamed, then writes the character. Later export (exportCanonical) resolves only the embedded links (a card's lorebooks, a preset's regex sets) with resolveLinks and, for a format that carries them, fails closed with 422 naming each missing embedded piece by kind and id rather than exporting a silent book-less card; links the file never carries never block an export, and what a target cannot carry is a report warning in the CLI as in the app. Disabled books are omitted with knowledgeRefs named as not carried so export is not a false success.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  Ownership map for bundle code. convert.ts owns readPieces, emitPieces, emitBundle and convertFile. core/links.ts follows and renames links, read from each kind's links in kind.ts. adapter.ts defines EmitContext and extractLorebook. character-book.ts owns shared CCv2/v3 extract and re-embed. character schema owns knowledgeRefs and worldName. KnowledgeRail session ops attach and reorder refs. studio/bundle.ts persists related pieces then the character. server-engine.ts wires Studio inspect and export. cli.ts wires convert. book-ops filters enabled books. Agnai index is the non-CCv3 override example.

### The canonical superset model

- id: `reference/concepts/canonical-model`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/canonical-model.md`
- The canonical superset model is the one hub every format converts through, never format to format. This page defines the shared wrapper and the superset rule that decides what body may hold; whole-engine hub-and-spoke context lives in architecture.md and per-kind body detail on entity pages. Structure lives in src/core/canonical.ts while per-kind fields live under src/entities. The wrapper is CanonicalEntity with schemaVersion, kind, id, body, optional profiles, and original escrow. The superset rule first-classes only fields a real supported wire format serializes; everything else rides escrow. Escrow: what the rule leaves out is original as Record of OriginalEntry with raw, unmapped, and optional sourceMedia for carriers. Profiles: one superset, many apps stores sparse per-FormatId Partial body overrides so authors set only deltas without full duplicate records. Source of truth lists canonical.ts, adapter kind union, character and lorebook schemas, and architecture map.

- **H2 The wrapper** (`the-wrapper`)
  CanonicalEntity is the stable shape every entity shares: schemaVersion fixed at 1, kind as a closed union of character lorebook persona or regex today, id as a storage-safe slug from canonicalId, body as the superset content, optional profiles as sparse per-FormatId Partial body overrides, and optional original as the escrow map. FormatId is intentionally an open string so drop-in adapters claim any id without core hardcoding the set. canonicalId normalizes names with NFKC, lowercases, collapses non-alphanumerics to hyphens, caps length, and falls back to character when empty so path policy always accepts the result. kind stays closed for compile-time safety across FormatAdapter; preset and pack schemas exist under entities without adapters yet. Formats are open; entity kinds grow only by union member plus entities folder.
- **H2 The superset rule** (`the-superset-rule`)
  Body is the union of what real formats express, not any one wire shape. A field earns a first-class canonical slot only if some real currently supported wire format serializes it; otherwise it rides original. Not serialized means no slot: platform and database baggage such as creator stats publishing timestamps and runtime state stay out of body, and in-memory-only residue such as RoleCall allowRecursion scanPreset probabilityMode verified absent from serializers stays in escrow. Single-platform is not a reason to relegate: if even one format serializes an authored field it earns a slot because the schema is the editor. Lorebook first-classes SillyTavern-only scan sources vectorized group toggles automation and displayIndex on that ground. The policed distinction is authored-and-serialized content versus derived-or-runtime state; character schema states the same gate.
- **H2 Escrow: what the rule leaves out** (`escrow-what-the-rule-leaves-out`)
  Fields that fail the superset rule are contained, not dropped. The wrapper original field is a Record keyed by FormatId of OriginalEntry values: raw source payload verbatim, unmapped fields canonical did not express, and optional sourceMedia for binary carriers such as PNG card pixels. Same-format round trips stay field-lossless; cross-format conversion is contained-loss by design. Superset rule and escrow keep body portable while nothing outside it is lost silently.
- **H2 Profiles: one superset, many apps** (`profiles-one-superset-many-apps`)
  One superset body still allows per-app differences without full duplicates. profiles is a sparse Record of FormatId to Partial body, empty by default. Authors write the entity once and set only fields that should differ for a given app; the projected app version is body plus those overrides via the adapter. Both profiles and original share the open FormatId key space, so overrides and escrow residue thread the same format identity model across the wrapper.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  canonical.ts owns the wrapper, schema version, id policy, escrow envelope, primaryOriginalRaw, and profiles. adapter.ts owns the closed entity-kind union. character and lorebook entity schemas state the superset rule for their bodies. architecture.md supplies whole-engine hub-and-spoke, escrow, and detection context for this model page.

### The embedded character_book

- id: `reference/concepts/character-book`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/character-book.md`
- The embedded character_book concept explains how a card-carried lorebook becomes a linked CanonicalLorebook on import and is re-inlined into the target format book slot on export. Source is formats/_shared/character-book.ts with convert.ts orchestration; this implements architecture bundles for card-plus-book. A different dialect from the worldbook file covers CCv3 field names versus standalone ST world info, including dual-encoded position under extensions. Import: extract at the boundary finds, maps, escrows under character-book, and links knowledgeRefs, with Non-CCv3 dialects override the extractor for Agnai MemoryBook. Export: inline at the boundary uses EmitContext lorebooks and twin overlay, with Quirks preserved faithfully for use_regex and multi-book merge via vaud_source_books. End to end walks convertFile readPieces plus emitPieces for CLI and Studio callers. Source of truth lists mapper, adapter, enums, convert, and canonical files that own each concern for implementers reading the wire path end to end.

- **H2 A different dialect from the worldbook file** (`a-different-dialect-from-the-worldbook-file`)
  Embedded character_book is not the same wire shape as a standalone SillyTavern world info file. It uses CCv3 entry field names at top level and tucks ST-extended fields into each entry extensions bag: keys and secondary_keys instead of key and keysecondary, insertion_order instead of displayIndex and order, use_regex force flag plus inline regex, coarse before_char after_char strings with precise value in extensions.position, and depth role group probability selective_logic scan sources inside extensions. Because ST-extended fields can appear top-level or in extensions, entryToCanonical reads both with extensions winning via pick. Only selective logic, injection role, and character filter share worldbook codecs through lore-enums; position and keyword parsing stay local. Position is dual-encoded, not degraded so export writes coarse string and precise extensions.position together and import prefers extensions then falls back.
  - **H3 Position is dual-encoded, not degraded** (`position-is-dual-encoded-not-degraded`)
    On export positionToCoarse writes CCv3 top-level before_char or after_char while positionToExt writes the precise slot into extensions.position in parallel. Spec-only readers see the coarse floor; ST-family readers recover the exact slot. On import parseBookPosition reads extensions.position first and falls back to the top-level string only when extensions carries none, so encoding is lossless for any reader that understands extensions.
- **H2 Import: extract at the boundary** (`import-extract-at-the-boundary`)
  When a character imports, a format-agnostic layer finds an embedded book and extracts it. findCharacterBook checks CCv3 slot, CCv2 extensions.character_book, and flat V1, returning raw so the caller controls escrow. characterBookToLorebook maps to LorebookBody. extractCharacterBook stashes raw under the new lorebook original key character-book, not the host format id, so dialect owns the escrow shape across host formats. readPieces sets knowledgeRefs to the lorebook id. Non-CCv3 dialects override the extractor via CharacterAdapter extractLorebook; Agnai embeds native characterBook MemoryBook and reuses its standalone lorebook mapper so embedded and file imports canonicalize identically.
  - **H3 Non-CCv3 dialects override the extractor** (`non-ccv3-dialects-override-the-extractor`)
    Shared extractCharacterBook understands only CCv2/v3 character_book. Formats with another dialect implement extractLorebook on CharacterAdapter; readPieces calls that override when present. Agnai is the case today: native characterBook MemoryBook mapped through agnai-lorebook so books read the same embedded or as files.
- **H2 Export: inline at the boundary** (`export-inline-at-the-boundary`)
  Re-embedding is the target format job. Adapters that embed knowledge receive resolved lorebooks in EmitContext and call embedCharacterBook to write data.character_book and clear stale extensions.character_book. Empty resolved list clears both slots; omitted context leaves the raw twin intact. SillyTavern RoleCall Risu and Lumiverse use the shared helper; Agnai applies the same three-way rule to native characterBook. lorebookToCharacterBook overlays when a character-book twin exists so unedited books preserve parsed fields and raw-only residue; JSON formatting may normalize. Without a twin it full-encodes from canonical for cross-format paths. Quirks preserved faithfully covers entry-level use_regex only when every keyword is regex, and multi-book concatenation with vaud_source_books boundaries.
  - **H3 Quirks preserved faithfully** (`quirks-preserved-faithfully`)
    use_regex is entry-level only when every keyword in both trigger lists is regex. Multi-book refs concatenate under the first book's settings with extensions.vaud_source_books recording each source name and entry count.
- **H2 End to end** (`end-to-end`)
  convertFile runs the same-kind character path: readPieces extracts and links books, then emitPieces through emitBundle hands resolved lorebooks to target fromCanonical. CLI calls convertFile; Studio uses the same primitives with store-resolved knowledgeRefs so both re-embed through identical code and lorebooks survive format hops.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  character-book.ts owns mapping extract and re-embed. adapter.ts owns extractLorebook and EmitContext. lore-enums.ts shares selective logic role and filter. convert.ts orchestrates readPieces, emitPieces, emitBundle and convertFile. canonical.ts owns wrapper escrow and ids. VAUDEVILLE apps/rc notes are interop facts only for this dialect.

### Detection and the registry

- id: `reference/concepts/detection`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/detection.md`
- Detection and the registry explain how Bunzai sniffs file content rather than trusting extensions. Every adapter scores AdapterInput confidence; registry.detect keeps the single best score at or above DETECT_THRESHOLD 0.5 or returns undefined. Content, not extension builds bytes always and text only for valid UTF-8 json or txt. The registry pass is one winner with strict-greater selection and deliberate score spread so order does not decide ties. Confidence scores ladder specific readers over generic shared shapes, with weak in-band and sub-threshold soft nos. Not detection: the origin labeler is a separate bunzai label pass after format choice. The cross-kind firewall forces zero on other kinds so worldbooks never steal character readers, backed by detection.test.ts and kind-discriminated convert. Adding a format never edits detection: folders-as-schema registers each detect locally without core changes. Source of truth maps registry adapter loader tests CLI and provenance ownership.

- **H2 Content, not extension** (`content-not-extension`)
  Adapters receive AdapterInput with bytes for binary, text for JSON, and optional filename. Reads always fill bytes and decode text only for valid UTF-8 .json or .txt; extension never picks the winner. Binary detectors use magic and chunks; JSON needs text or returns 0. sniffContainer is a separate wrapper sniff under provenance.
- **H2 The registry pass** (`the-registry-pass`)
  registry.ts is the pure in-memory adapter table. detect walks every registered adapter, tracks the highest score, and returns that adapter only if score clears DETECT_THRESHOLD 0.5 inclusive; otherwise undefined. One pass one winner against the same input with no format-to-format probing. No match is real: callers report unrecognized rather than guess. Selection uses strict greater so equal later scores never displace earlier ones, which is why confidence ladders are deliberately spread. convert inspect validate and label CLI commands plus studio inspect all call registry.detect first.
- **H2 Confidence scores** (`confidence-scores`)
  detect returns 0 to 1; zero means not mine. Scores are hand-chosen so specific readers outrank generic ones on shared wire shapes. SillyTavern generic Tavern reader scores 0.9; RoleCall claims 1.0 only with extensions.rolecall and returns 0 on plain CCv3 so sillytavern wins. Backyard scores 0.9 on legacy cards but 0.55 on bare persona objects, a weak but intentional clear of the floor. vaud-json returns 1 for real canonical entities and 0.1 for other JSON, which never clears 0.5 and cannot win. Per-adapter rosters live on FORMAT-SUPPORT and format pages.
- **H2 Not detection: the origin labeler** (`not-detection-the-origin-labeler`)
  bunzai label runs a second scoring pass after detection already chose the format. labelCard guesses which app likely authored the card by testing app-namespaced extension blocks and reports the winning rule weight as 0 to 1 confidence. That answers origin heuristic where detect confidence answers which adapter reads the file. They are different numbers from different passes and must not be conflated.
- **H2 The cross-kind firewall** (`the-cross-kind-firewall`)
  Character lorebook persona and regex adapters share one registry pass, so each detector must return 0 on other kinds. Character-versus-lorebook is load-bearing: ST worldbooks are name-shaped JSON that would false-positive a naive V1 character path. The flat/v1 branch therefore requires real character signals description personality scenario first_mes or mes_example so worldbooks route to sillytavern-lorebook. detection.test.ts registers every format and asserts samples route correctly; new folders may add detectors but must not steal. A type-level complement is FormatAdapter kind-discriminated union with convertFile throwing on cross-kind pairs.
- **H2 Adding a format never edits detection** (`adding-a-format-never-edits-detection`)
  Each format folder owns detect. The registry never hardcodes formats; the loader discovers src/formats/*/index.ts default exports. Adding a format means writing detect in the new folder and choosing a score that fits the confidence gradient with no core edits. The firewall test is the safety net against scores that invade another format territory.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  registry.ts owns the table detect and threshold. adapter.ts owns detect contract and kind union. loader.ts registers folders. detection.test.ts is the firewall property. convert.ts guards kind on conversion. CLI and server-engine build AdapterInput. provenance.ts owns the origin labeler. FORMAT-SUPPORT.md lists the generated adapter roster.

### Escrow and round-trip

- id: `reference/concepts/escrow-and-roundtrip`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/escrow-and-roundtrip.md`
- Escrow and round-trip document how every canonical entity carries a parsed import twin in original so same-format fields are preserved and cross-format conversion loses only non-portable residue by design. The envelope holds raw, unmapped, and optional sourceMedia per adapter key, with primaryOriginalRaw selecting the source twin. A three-state overlay re-encodes edited fields while retaining other parsed fields; it does not promise byte-identical containers. PNG pixels remain available to PNG export, which regenerates card chunks. Cross-format targets read only their own original key and full-encode from the body without blind-copying foreign extensions or scripts.

- **H2 The escrow envelope** (`the-escrow-envelope`)
  Escrow is per-source original on the wrapper: Record of FormatId to OriginalEntry. raw is the entire parsed source payload untouched as the exporter twin. unmapped holds source facts canonical does not first-class such as Tavern round-trip variant. sourceMedia is the binary carrier such as PNG card pixels kept as a raw-bytes twin, not body content. original fills automatically on import and is never hand-edited; each adapter stores under its own format id. primaryOriginalRaw reads the first entry raw so labeler and bundle layers cannot drift. Superset rule sends non-wire residue here rather than inventing body fields.
- **H2 Same-format round-trips preserve fields** (`same-format-round-trips-preserve-fields`)
