  Adapter symmetry: toCanonical stashes the parsed payload while reading; fromCanonical re-emits from that original for the round trip. SillyTavern character stores the whole card twin, variant, and PNG carrier, then overlays the body onto a twin clone in the original variant. applyBodyToData is three-state: equal values leave parsed wire values untouched, changed values write exact wire homes, and values the twin had but canonical dropped clear those homes. Absent on both sides leaves raw alone, protecting unrepresentable shapes and unknown keys. This is field-level fidelity; JSON serialization may normalize formatting. PNG pixels remain available to the PNG export path, which regenerates card chunks. Byte identity applies only where a format specification and fixture establish that tier.
- **H2 A cross-format conversion is contained loss by design** (`a-cross-format-conversion-is-contained-loss-by-design`)
  convertFile same-kind paths pass the full entity but only the canonical body plus re-embedded lorebooks project into the target file. Per-id keying keeps the source twin under original source while the target adapter looks for original target, finds none on a fresh conversion, and full-encodes from body alone. Private extensions scripts layout and internal ids stay in escrow and are not written into another app file. PNG carrier pixels remain available as the entity portrait and to the PNG export path, but are not written into unrelated target formats. Loss is bounded to non-portable extras while the portable core crosses.
- **H2 Per-kind coverage of the same idea** (`per-kind-coverage-of-the-same-idea`)
  Every entity kind carries escrow the same way. Format pages document same-format field fidelity and cross-format collapses. SillyTavern covers character and lorebook twins, injection-slot collapse on cross-format lorebooks, and persona plus regex codecs that seal multi-record files so untouched parsed records retain their fields. Entity pages define what each canonical slot means for character and lorebook bodies.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  canonical.ts owns original OriginalEntry and primaryOriginalRaw. adapter.ts states the stash and re-emit contract. convert.ts projects only body across formats. tavern-fields applyBodyToData is the three-state overlay example. sillytavern index stores and re-emits twins. architecture.md maps escrow in the whole engine diagram.

### Concept: the persona editor

- id: `reference/concepts/persona-editor`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/persona-editor.md`
- Persona editor is the Workbench {{user}} sheet that reuses the character editor chassis: the three-column grid, BentoCard tiles, and portrait face plate. PortraitCard writes the persona portrait and KnowledgeRail manages knowledgeRefs. Identity contains the tagline, pronouns, brief, section tiles, and flat Identity Text. The right column has shared color controls, prompt-injection stops selected by the Write-for profile, a preview from core/persona/inject.ts, and the default-persona star. The compiler contract keeps brief out of injected content and prevents content from becoming the card blurb. The page records the component-catalog reuse rule and describes the Library persona shelf. Its Wires section covers rcpersona, RoleCall's V2 persona export, SillyTavern persona backups, Lumiverse, Marinara, and the absence of a standalone Agnai persona file wire. For SillyTavern backups, Bunzai imports the default persona and retains the other personas' field values for export; this is semantic value preservation, not byte-identical output.

- **H2 Reuse law on this surface** (`reuse-law-on-this-surface`)
  Every element on the persona editor was walked against the component catalog. Anything new on this surface must come from docs/reference/components.md first before inventing a twin control. The law matches house UI reuse: scan the generated catalog and share chassis pieces already used by character and regex editors such as WriteForStrip BentoCard and KnowledgeRail.
- **H2 The shelf** (`the-shelf`)
  Library persona shelf lives in persona-shelf.tsx and persona-shelf-ops.ts. Cards show portrait or monogram, put the brief as the card line which is its one honest home, and surface pronouns section count and lorebook facts from the meta loader. A gilt Default badge marks the studio default persona preference.
- **H2 The wires** (`the-wires`)
  Supported persona wires include rcpersona and RoleCall's V2 side-channel export; SillyTavern persona backups; Lumiverse account objects with pronoun triplet data; and Marinara personas whose theming and stat bars remain in escrow. For a SillyTavern backup, Bunzai imports the default persona and retains the other personas' field values for export; serialized output is not promised to be byte-identical. Codec headers document detection firewalls. Agnai has no standalone persona file wire and uses the structured-persona component on character cards.

### Concept: the regex editor

- id: `reference/concepts/regex-editor`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/regex-editor.md`
- Regex editor is the Workbench surface for authoring a regex set under src/ui/apps/workbench/regex, transcribed from locked wireframes, with Library shelf in regex-shelf views. Everything shown about a pattern is engine truth from explainAst verified examples validate and travelLint; the UI never fabricates match claims. Three authoring modes, one rule keeps Guided Plain words and Pattern as lossless views on one pattern source. Platform honesty in the frame re-lenses Write-for phases fine-print fields and travel notes from capability matrices only. The full check (health) runs six linters with shadowing proven by real chain execution and safe duplicate fix. The recipe gallery offers thirty-seven engine-proven starters grouped by purpose. The test bench hosts live applyRules step cards and dialect import preview beside the editor. Fluid grammar collapses by pane container queries not viewport width. Source of truth is the workbench regex folder plus core/regex helpers shared with the engine page.

- **H2 Three authoring modes, one rule** (`three-authoring-modes-one-rule`)
  Mode switch is per-rule and lossless: the pattern is the single source and modes are views. Guided uses quiz cards in binder grammar with three steps, an it-will-catch card from template plus verified examples and one computed miss, confession line, no raw pattern, and escape to Pattern. Plain words is RoleCall example-driven builder for match-these-words family analysis stems and match-by-example with syntax-colored slash readout token chips and inline try-it. Pattern is raw find flags replace with live plain-words reading under the box and an honest partial note when the pattern exceeds builder grammar.
- **H2 Platform honesty in the frame** (`platform-honesty-in-the-frame`)
  Header Write-for tab strip re-lenses the whole editor per profile. Where it runs pills come from phasesForProfile so unsupported phases disappear and foreign phases already set show dashed amber. Fine-print fold shows only fields platformOwnsField. Travel-lint notes render dashed amber inline. All per-platform truth is read from capability matrices never hardcoded UI literals.
- **H2 The full check (health)** (`the-full-check-health`)
  health-pane plus core/regex/inspect runs six linter checks over the whole set with problems first. Headline is shadowing detection: execution-verified examples per rule run through the real chain; a rule flags only when it provably never fires while an earlier rule did, and indeterminate runs stay silent. Duplicates get one-click safe fix switching the copy off in session until Save. Reordering advice is always fix by hand. Per-rule Health card shows that rule slice; heavy patterns wear quiet slow chips on TOC rows and N slow counts on Library shelf cards.
- **H2 The recipe gallery** (`the-recipe-gallery`)
  gallery-pane plus templates and template-catalog provide starter recipes opened from New rule. Thirty-seven recipes group Cleanup Guardrails Formatting Style Compatibility Roleplay. Each pitch includes Before After pairs the engine computed; catalog tests re-run every example so unproven recipes do not ship. Picking appends a pre-filled rule focused in Plain-words when the pattern fits words grammar otherwise Pattern with reading; Start blank keeps empty-rule path.
- **H2 The test bench** (`the-test-bench`)
  bench-pane hosts beside the editor never as a modal. A sample line runs through real applyRules on every keystroke with one step card per rule showing before after word diff, engine-span capture chips, overlay chips, or plain skip reason, plus a final what comes out card. Second tab is import preview: drop any of five dialects, see per-rule before after against the sample, and stage-pick rules into the open set. Narrow panes become full-screen takeover so the chain stays legible while editing.
- **H2 Fluid grammar** (`fluid-grammar`)
  Editor collapses by its pane width via container queries not viewport media queries. Below 34rem TOC becomes a Contents bottom sheet with pager, Write-for strip folds into head kebab, and bench takes full screen. Bench-open has its own 44rem stack point because two columns need less room than three.

### Concept: the regex engine

- id: `reference/concepts/regex-engine`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/regex-engine.md`
- Regex engine documents how Bunzai runs a regex set in pure src/core/regex with no side effects. Rules are data executed as budgeted String.replace never eval or new Function; HTML-looking output only through SealedHtmlPreview and Risu cbs tokens stored then stripped before compile. The pipeline (`apply.ts`) runs applyRules in sortOrder with d-flag spans, per-rule and set budgets, condition chaining, overlay non-mutating matches, and firstMatchOnly, plus replace-ops group and case transforms. The AST layer (`ast/`) is a clean-room u-mode parser powering explainAst examplesFor analyzeRedos and dialect flag parsing. Travel honesty (`travel-lint.ts`) reports cross-platform lint for JS engines without forking parsers. The builder (`builder.ts` + `word-boundary.ts` / `word-family.ts`) turns word lists into verified patterns for Guided and Plain-words modes. Entity shape is entities/regex.md; platform phase truth lives in capabilities and platform-fields matrices, never as UI literals for pickers or the position-picker law itself. Execution now refuses unsupported syntax or flags and suspicious structures, checks substituted patterns, and bounds text and trace construction. Native matching still needs an external worker for preemption.

- **H2 The pipeline (`apply.ts`)** (`the-pipeline-applyts`)
  applyRules processes text and ordered rules deterministically. Match and capture spans come from the native engine through its d flag. It supports phase and condition gating, overlay results, first-match-only replacement and per-rule or set clocks. Structural admission applies after find-side macro substitution; shared limits bound text construction, capture counts, match enumeration and retained traces. A clock between steps cannot interrupt one native match. Studio callers add a terminable worker, whereas direct core callers do not acquire that deadline. Replacement operations implement numbered and named groups, match sugar, JavaScript dollar tokens and case transforms. Dollar tokens work in Bunzai but may become literal text in other engines, so travel lint reports the resulting portability risk. The linked safety page distinguishes logical limits, tested behavior and unverified packaged isolation.
- **H2 The AST layer (`ast/`)** (`the-ast-layer-ast`)
  The clean-room Unicode-mode parser produces typed nodes with spans and supports explanation, generated verified examples and structural backtracking reports. Explanation remains honest about ASCII word boundaries, and example chips must be confirmed by the real engine. Execution refuses both suspicious and dangerous analysis rather than treating suspicion as permission to run. Unsupported parser syntax or flags fail closed instead of using the weaker heuristic fallback. Stored v flags remain unchanged for conversion, but execution is refused until the analysis grammar implements them. Dialect parsing still preserves foreign flag tokens as data.
- **H2 Travel honesty (`travel-lint.ts`)** (`travel-honesty-travel-lintts`)
  All five platforms execute JS RegExp, so divergence is a lint, not a parser fork. travelLint reports with spans: replace and flag extensions the destination will not run, engine-only rule fields, and host-age risks. The dollar-ampersand family reports on every lens but this engine's own, because it works while a rule is edited and breaks once it travels. The editor shows these dashed amber under Write-for; nothing is blocked or mangled.
- **H2 The builder (`builder.ts` + `word-boundary.ts` / `word-family.ts`)** (`the-builder-builderts-word-boundaryts-word-familyts`)
  Plain-words layer under Guided and Plain-words modes: word and phrase lists become pattern plus explanation plus execution-verified examples. Unicode-aware boundaries handle space-less scripts and the whole-word toggle drops out honestly where word-boundary would lie. Structure atoms cover numbers anything-between and line anchors. Round-trips through explainPattern which carries full AST reading for any parseable pattern.

### Concept: safe content rendering (the render toggle)

- id: `reference/concepts/safe-rendering`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/safe-rendering.md`
- Safe content rendering explains how character fields that carry untrusted Markdown, HTML, or plain text are shown in the editor and inspector without dumping raw tags or running XSS. Authority lives in render-markup and render-policy plus the RenderBox and leaving-gate components. The pipeline (sanitize last) caps input, routes by format through marked or escape, and always finishes with DOMPurify before dangerouslySetInnerHTML. Hardening forbids script style iframe object embed and form controls, keeps sanitized style attributes, drops non-allowlisted hrefs, pins noopener blank targets, and memoizes results. The leaving-gate (external links) intercepts rendered anchor clicks, shows the real destination via a CustomEvent, and enforces open only through POST open plus safeExternalUrl with OS browser argv; recognition uses a local platform registry without network unfurl. The RenderBox component is the reusable toggle that takes value and RenderFormat, defaults render ON, and falls back to source for plain empty or editable blank fields.

- **H2 The pipeline (sanitize last)** (`the-pipeline-sanitize-last`)
  renderMarkup in render-markup.ts is the DOM shell and render-policy.ts holds pure decisions. Pipeline order is fixed: capInput truncates to RENDER_INPUT_CAP of 200k so giant fields cannot stall parsing; then format branches so markdown runs marked then sanitize, html sanitizes as-is, and plain HTML-escapes with no parsing; DOMPurify.sanitize is always the last step and its output goes straight to dangerouslySetInnerHTML with no post-mutation. The sanitizer is never hand-rolled because allowlist walks miss mutation-XSS. Policy purity enables unit tests without a DOM for isSafeHref http https mailto, # anchors and single-slash paths only plus the input cap. The XSS battery in render-markup.test.ts runs real DOMPurify under jsdom and asserts sanitized output lacks script on handlers and javascript schemes rather than relying on alert absence.
- **H2 Hardening** (`hardening`)
  Forbidden elements include script style iframe object embed and form controls while the style attribute is kept so authored text-align on notes survives after DOMPurify value sanitization. An afterSanitizeAttributes hook drops non-scheme-allowlisted anchor hrefs and pins target blank with rel noopener noreferrer so rendered links cannot share an opener or navigate the shell in place. The hook registers lazily on first render never at import so the module stays side-effect-free for desktop boot smoke that evaluates without window. Results memoize in a bounded map keyed on format plus raw so unchanged fields do not re-run marked and DOMPurify.
- **H2 The leaving-gate (external links)** (`the-leaving-gate-external-links`)
  Rendered links are untrusted for destination and visible text so none navigate directly. RenderBox onRenderedClick intercepts plain left-clicks and routes to the full-window leaving-gate page that shows the real host and url and warns on externalLinkInfo mismatch when visible text names another host. Modified ctrl meta middle clicks stay native as deliberate open anyway. Because RenderBox and gate live in separate bundles the click crosses as a window CustomEvent via link-gate.ts not a shared module bus that would split-brain. Enforcement is server-side: POST only api open re-runs safeExternalUrl for http https normalized and spawns the OS browser with an argv array no shell. DOMPurify forbids form so content cannot auto-submit. Bare URLs in plain fields are linkifyEscaped through the same gate. Destination recognition uses a local curated platform-registry seed with monogram tile or neutral globe and never fetches favicons or unfurls which would be SSRF; widen by seed row only.
- **H2 The RenderBox component** (`the-renderbox-component`)
  RenderBox under components render-box is the reusable surface with a per-box render toggle default ON. It accepts value and declared RenderFormat never a field id so field identity does not leak into the component; format is data-driven from characterFields InspectField tags and editor markdown for prose cards. Rendered mode shows sanitized HTML; source mode shows the raw editable textarea or raw text for read-only fields. plain fields have nothing to render so they show inline without a toggle. An empty editable field falls back to its source surface so editing a blank preview never costs an extra click.

### Concept: the tag taxonomy

- id: `reference/concepts/tag-taxonomy`
- path: `docs/reference/concepts/tag-taxonomy.md`
- The tag taxonomy concept page defines how free-string character tags from discovery.tags become KIND-colored chips without any wire format carrying a category. Authority is pure categorizeTag in src/core/tag-taxonomy.ts with tests in the sibling test file; presentation colors and icons live only in workbench Editor TAG_CATEGORY_STYLE. Categories lists the eleven TagCategory values identity trait role genre theme setting pov mood kink warning and meta, each with coverage and editor color, after normalize folds hash lowercase and separators. Deliberately a seed, not the full taxonomy states the dictionary is a curated RoleCall-mirrored seed, unrecognized tags fall to meta slate, and the Open Tagsheet milestone widens SEED rows only never code branches so lookup stays one normalized Map.

- **H2 Categories** (`categories`)
  categorizeTag takes a tag string and returns TagCategory after folding to a lookup key by dropping a leading hash, lowercasing, and collapsing underscore hyphen slash and whitespace to single spaces, then reading a seed dictionary. Categories are identity for gender sexuality species cyan, trait for personality dere archetype violet, role for occupation dynamic relationship rose, genre indigo, theme for trope theme pink, setting for place era environment emerald, pov for narrative POV sky, mood for mood tone amber, kink red, warning for trigger content warning orange, and meta for authoring provenance plus the unrecognized fallback slate. Editor colors are presentation only; the pure classifier returns the category token.
- **H2 Deliberately a seed, not the full taxonomy** (`deliberately-a-seed-not-the-full-taxonomy`)
  The dictionary is a curated seed of common tags mirroring RoleCall display categories, not the full roughly thousand-tag taxonomy. Unrecognized tags resolve to meta with neutral slate and are never mis-colored. The discovery milestone Open Tagsheet widens coverage only by adding SEED rows, never by adding code branches, so lookup remains a single normalized Map.

## reference/entities

### Agent entity

- id: `reference/entities/agent`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/agent.md`
- An Agent is one canonical Library piece containing instructions, optional model settings and Tool and Skill reference IDs. Promptbook phases compose these through the existing scene runtime with phase model overrides taking precedence. Final Cut uses the same Library authority and migrates active legacy records only after exact identity creation and complete escrow verification. Collisions retain the source, tombstones and banks remain untouched, and revision checks protect saves and transactional deletes. Final Cut loads instructions and model settings as templates rather than executing linked capabilities; Director integration remains absent. Isolated tests cover migration and revision safety, with live and packaged behavior unverified.


### Character entity

- id: `reference/entities/character`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/character.md`
- Character entity is the field-meaning authority for CanonicalCharacter as CanonicalEntity character CharacterBody, with schema.ts owning types and this page owning what each field means and which formats produce it. How to read the field tables explains generated Field Type Producers Meaning cells from docs-fields. Composition lists eight always-present groups and optional presentation settings behavior plus bias worldName knowledgeRefs behaviorRefs variants. Identity covers name nickname and RoleCall casting attributes. Persona and voice holds personality scenario appearance Voice ImagePrompt and structured encodings. Prompts and injections covers system postHistory depth prefill additionalText and DepthInjection. Greetings and examples covers firstMessage alternate group greetings and mes_example. Media covers portrait assets Sprite visualKind faceLabel and MediaAsset. Presentation is casting-card color palette background spoilers. Settings and behavior first-classes dials and CharacterBehavior scripts never executed by converter. Attribution and discovery separates credit notes and ContentRating tags. Composition and variants defines body assembly links and CharacterVariant overlay. What rides escrow, not a field and See also close the map.

- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field tables are generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; never hand-edit a cell, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the schema TypeScript type. Producers lists source formats from the doc comment or dash when untagged, including universal core fields whose provenance is prose. Meaning explains purpose; blank cells are self-evident primitives without schema annotation. Richer explanation lives only in surrounding prose never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  CharacterBody groups fields into eight always-present sub-objects identity persona prompts greetings examples media attribution and discovery, plus optional presentation settings and behavior sub-objects and five standalone fields bias worldName knowledgeRefs behaviorRefs and variants. Grouping mirrors how a card is authored not any one app storage layout. Full CharacterBody and CharacterVariant tables live in Composition and variants at the end. Sections walk each group with tables pasted from docs/generated/fields.character.md.
- **H2 Identity** (`identity`)
  Identity is who the character is: required name plus authored attributes. char macro resolves to name; nickname from CCv3 Risu Backyard overrides display without changing canonical name. tagline is a short one-liner. description is the main CCv2/v3 persona block. characterVersion is unannotated version text. RoleCall adds fullName title age as free text never int pronouns. Agnai culture drives default language and voice selection. Together these separate display casting metadata from the portable name slot.
- **H2 Persona and voice** (`persona-and-voice`)
  Persona carries how the character behaves sounds and looks: personality scenario and Agnai appearance free-text, plus nested Voice for TTS selection not audio bytes, ImagePrompt for image-gen hints, and structured as a discriminated union that preserves attribute maps such as W++. Voice child covers provider voiceId rate pitch disabled and open extras. ImagePrompt child covers prompt prefix suffix negative template instructions emotionInstructions and labeled rows. Structured kinds text without attributes versus attributes wpp sbf boostyle requiring attributes so illegal states are unrepresentable. Other formats often fold appearance into description.
  - **H3 Voice** (`voice`)
    Voice is the authored TTS selection and config, never model bytes which ride assets or escrow. provider is an open union including elevenlabs webspeechsynthesis novel agnaistic vits and more. Optional voiceId rate pitch and Agnai disabled keep config when voice is turned off. extras is an open Record for provider-specific knobs such as stability similarityBoost model seed and vits config.
  - **H3 ImagePrompt** (`imageprompt`)
    ImagePrompt is creator text that steers image generation for the character. Sampler and provider knobs are platform config and are not modeled. Fields include Risu full base prompt, prefix suffix negative, Agnai template, Risu instructions and emotionInstructions, and Risu sdData-style labeled rows of label value pairs.
- **H2 Prompts and injections** (`prompts-and-injections`)
  Prompts and injections hold instruction-layer text sent to the model. systemPrompt is the universal system slot. postHistoryInstructions is SillyTavern jailbreak post-history. depthInjections is an array of DepthInjection entries. Agnai prefill prepends to the assistant reply common with Claude. Risu additionalText is a simple plain append not a depth injection. The DepthInjection child defines text depth optional role origin for re-export home and enabled toggle where undefined means enabled.
  - **H3 DepthInjection** (`depthinjection`)
    DepthInjection places text at a fixed chat depth. Required text and depth; optional role system user or assistant. origin records which source mechanism produced the injection among depth_prompt rolecall_details and worldinfo so export re-emits to the original home. enabled is a creator toggle with undefined meaning enabled.
- **H2 Greetings and examples** (`greetings-and-examples`)
  Greetings and examples cover opening messages and example dialogue. firstMessage is the primary opening. alternateGreetings is an array of Greeting values. groupOnlyGreetings from CCv3 are group-chat-only and distinct from alternates. Greeting child is text plus optional title and format-local id for editing provenance that other writers drop, emitting only text and title. Examples child holds exampleMessages as the mes_example string slot.
  - **H3 Greeting** (`greeting`)
    One greeting with optional creator title. text is required content. title is optional display labeling. id is stable editing identity and format-local provenance, not user-facing portable content; other format writers emit only text and title.
  - **H3 Examples** (`examples`)
    Examples holds optional exampleMessages as a single string corresponding to the wire mes_example dialogue block used for few-shot style character dialogue samples.
- **H2 Media** (`media`)
  Media holds image references and the Agnai composite-avatar recipe; bytes live in the asset store or data URIs. portrait is the main MediaAsset. assets from RoleCall and Risu carry extra images and expression outfit pose packs. sprite is the Agnai composite recipe when visualType is sprite. visualKind is open avatar or sprite mode. faceLabel prefers an expression-pack label for the body or variant and binds the portrait stage without a second asset store. MediaAsset child defines role name label primary ref mime. Sprite child is parts map plus gender eye body hair colors as recipe not rendered pixels.
  - **H3 MediaAsset** (`mediaasset`)
    MediaAsset is one image reference and its purpose. role is portrait emotion outfit pose background or other. Optional name and RoleCall label for expression outfit pose variants. primary marks the main asset of its role. ref points into the entity asset store or a data URI. mime is optional content type.
  - **H3 Sprite** (`sprite`)
    Sprite is the Agnai builder-authored composite avatar recipe: named part selections in parts plus optional gender eyeColor bodyColor and hairColor. Rendered images are assets; this shape is only the authored recipe.
- **H2 Presentation** (`presentation`)
  Presentation is the optional casting-card visual identity omitted by formats without the concept. signatureColor holds a single solid RC or Risu color; a two-to-three color blend rides gradientColors instead as one concept two storage shapes. Account-level accent is not modeled. palette is Swatch arrays; background is Background with optional video rate. fieldOrder is creator display order. spoilers is RC reveal config with fields boolean map preserved not collapsed. mediaLinks are RC external reference links. Swatch child is label name hex. Background child is ref overlayOpacity videoPlaybackRate.
  - **H3 Swatch** (`swatch`)
    Swatch is a named color in a card palette with optional label and name plus required hex value for the casting-card color system.
  - **H3 Background** (`background`)
    Background is a creator-curated backdrop reference with optional overlayOpacity and videoPlaybackRate when the asset is video, plus required ref into media storage.
- **H2 Settings and behavior** (`settings-and-behavior`)
  Settings and behavior hold authored inline dials and on-card behavior. Converter and editor NEVER execute any behavior payload: no eval require or DOM injection; execution only inside a later capability sandbox. Behavior re-emits only on its own format wire and is never blind-copied across formats. talkativeness is SillyTavern group turn weight 0 to 1. risu groups single-platform display toggles. responseSchema is Agnai structured-output config carried editable likely migrating to Preset later. CharacterBehavior child first-classes regexScripts as RegexScript twin, triggerScripts virtualScript background markup defaultVariables prebuiltAsset moduleToggles and privileged as warning and sandbox gate never execution. TriggerScript child is event conditions effects state machine rows.
  - **H3 CharacterBehavior** (`characterbehavior`)
    CharacterBehavior carries card scripts and background markup as editable data. regexScripts are card-embedded find replace twins using RegexScript from the regex entity with one definition and no separate table here. triggerScripts virtualScript backgroundHTML backgroundCSS defaultVariables prebuiltAsset and moduleToggles are mostly Risu payloads. privileged records that the card requested low-level script API as a UI warning and sandbox gating input never an execution trigger.
  - **H3 TriggerScript** (`triggerscript`)
    TriggerScript is a trigger state-machine script carried verbatim-editable with optional label, required event such as Risu type output input start, and conditions and effects as unknown arrays of condition and effect rows.
- **H2 Attribution and discovery** (`attribution-and-discovery`)
  Attribution and discovery cover credit provenance and how the card is found. Attribution fields include creator originalCreator source sourceUrl license creatorNotes as CCv2/v3 single-language notes creatorNotesMultilingual as CCv3 ISO map preferred over single-language and publicNote as RoleCall separate public from-the-creator note distinct from creatorNotes. createdAt and updatedAt are unix seconds. Discovery child holds free tags genre fandom ContentRating as all-ages mature or explicit kept out of free-text tags and contentWarnings as portable trigger warnings.
  - **H3 Discovery** (`discovery`)
    Discovery categorizes the card for browsing. tags genre and fandom are free strings. rating is explicit ContentRating all-ages mature or explicit and must stay out of free-text tags. contentWarnings are optional portable trigger warnings on the card.
- **H2 Composition and variants** (`composition-and-variants`)
  Composition and variants is the top CharacterBody assembly of every group. worldName is SillyTavern intent to auto-load an external worldbook by NAME while knowledgeRefs links embedded lorebooks by canonical id. behaviorRefs links standalone regex script entities by id versus behavior which carries on-card CharacterBehavior content. bias is Risu NovelAI phrase weight pairs a different axis than lorebook loreBiasGroups. presentation settings optional. variants from RoleCall are CharacterVariant alternate versions. CharacterVariant child uses deep partial overrides via applyVariant with present keys counting as overrides including empty clear; mirrorBase true deep-overlays while false replaces whole defined sections.
  - **H3 CharacterVariant** (`charactervariant`)
    CharacterVariant is an alternate version of the character. overrides is DeepPartial of the body without nested variants; applyVariant in variant.ts deep-merges onto base so one field or a whole section may change. A field is overridden when its key is present including empty string clear; absent keys inherit. mirrorBase true or undefined is default deep overlay; false means each defined section replaces the base whole section while untouched sections inherit and name falls through when omitted. id is required; label optional.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Anything a single source keeps without a canonical field stays in escrow formatId raw and survives same-format round-trip. Cross-format conversion carries only the canonical body so private app data never copies into another app file. Escrow riders include RoleCall private ids, unmapped SillyTavern extension keys, app-local layout state, and source bytes such as PNG pixels in sourceMedia served as portrait. Authored on-card behavior does NOT ride escrow because it is first-classed in behavior as CharacterBehavior editable data re-emitted only on its own format wire and never executed by converter or editor.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related authority pages close the character map. architecture.md owns the canonical model escrow hub-and-spoke and the adapter contract. entities/lorebook.md is the knowledgeRefs target superset. entities/regex.md owns behaviorRefs targets and the RegexScript shape of the card twin. concepts/character-book.md explains embedded character_book extract and re-embed. formats/README.md is the coverage matrix of every format and the fields it produces.

### Dice entity

- id: `reference/entities/dice`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/dice.md`
- The dice entity page covers CanonicalDice, something a person rolls, made once and used anywhere. It is one kind with three types, a roll, a table or a pool, and each carries only the field its type rolls. Rolling itself is not a piece but math in src/core/dice.ts, the same module the roll macro, the battle tracker and the spellbook use, while rollDicePiece in src/core/dice-piece.ts says what each type rolls on randomness the caller hands in. The page lists the types with example results, says a tool links a dice piece by id so a model can roll it with the turn's seeded randomness, gives the rules checked when a piece is saved, and describes the dice editor, while New dice saves a blank 1d20 roll.

- **H2 Types** (`types`)
  A roll carries dice, notation such as 2d6 or 1d20+3, and totals it, answering with the piece's name, the notation, each die, the modifier and the total. A table carries rows, of at least one, each a text and a whole weight of one or more; it rolls one die the size of all the weights together and lands on the row that number falls in, so a row of weight two is twice as likely as a row of weight one. A pool carries a count of dice, their sides of two or more, and a target; each die at or over the target counts as a success, and the answer lists the dice and the number of successes. A roll is never refused or shortened for being big: the answer lists each die up to twenty dice, then gives the count, lowest, highest and total, and the trace keeps every die.
- **H2 Where dice are used** (`where-dice-are-used`)
  A tool links a dice piece by its id through the tool's dice action, described on the tool page, so a model writing a reply can call that tool and roll the piece. The roll is decided by the turn's seeded randomness, which means the same seed rolls the same result. This is the one place the page names where dice pieces are used today: a dice piece is made once in the Library and any number of tools can link it, instead of each tool carrying its own fixed dice notation. The page does not describe any other host, such as a scene or a promptbook, using dice pieces directly yet.
- **H2 The rules** (`the-rules`)
  A piece carries exactly the field its type rolls and no other field: a roll carries dice, a table carries rows, and a pool carries pool. A roll's dice must be readable notation with at least one die of at least two sides, and a pool's target may not be greater than its number of sides. All of these rules are checked when the piece is saved, by the dice kind's strict decoder, so a stored piece can always be rolled and never fails at the moment a tool or anything else asks it for a result. The only other bounds are hostile-input ceilings, set about a hundred times past real use and refused as unusually large, not design limits.
- **H2 Editing** (`editing`)
  A dice piece opens in the Workbench's dice editor, one small page with Roll, Table and Pool, each with a one-line roll card. A roll has its dice, a table has rows with a weight, a result and a chance equal to the weight over the total, plus add-a-row and paste-a-list, which makes one row per line at weight 1, and a pool has its dice, sides and the number it counts at. Roll uses the real piece roll that a tool's action also uses, shows the result on the card, and lights the table row it picked until the piece is edited; it is off while the draft cannot be saved and never changes the piece. A piece keeps exactly the field its type rolls, and switching type sets the others aside and brings them back if the type is switched back. A save with nothing edited sends nothing. New dice saves a blank roll of 1d20.

### Formula entity

- id: `reference/entities/formula`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/formula.md`
- The formula entity page covers CanonicalFormula, a saved macro expression, bounded only by a hostile-input ceiling, such as a word count over the chat plus a roll, made once and run anywhere. A formula is macro text and nothing else, so what it can do is what the macro engine gives it, including Bunzai's own mentions, lastmention, tally and odds macros, and its decoder checks only shape and size. The page explains that a tool runs a formula through its formula action on the macro context the turn built its prompt from, handed to the tools once the prompt is built, and that the run happens on a copy, with any variable write refused out loud and blank or failing formulas refused too. The Editing section describes the formula editor and its Try rail, which runs the text on a copy of a scene through the formula-try route.

- **H2 Where formulas run** (`where-formulas-run`)
  A tool runs a formula through its formula action, described on the tool page, and the model is told what the formula renders. The formula reads the macro context the turn built its prompt from: the card, the persona, the history before the message being answered, and the variables as the prompt left them. That context is handed to the tools through ToolsContext.macros once the prompt is built, because tools are made before the prompt exists but called after it, and a call that somehow arrives before the context is there is refused instead of running against an empty context. Tools are the only place formulas run today.
- **H2 Safety** (`safety`)
  runFormulaReadOnly runs the formula on a structuredClone of the macro context, so nothing it touches reaches the scene. A run that would change a variable, through setvar, state.set, once, ask_in_reply, pick and the like, is refused in words naming the macros, and a message holding a write macro makes formulas that read message text fail. Blank formulas and macro errors are refused. A formula has no step number of its own: the tool loop owns a time budget for all of a reply's tool calls, a setting with a default (shown in seconds in Settings), hands each run what is left as a deadline, and the macro engine checks that clock between nodes, after every handler and inside the word macros' loops, so a run that passes it answers with the error, never as finished. A formula shares the chat's messages, so the positional word index is built once and a phrase costs what its rarest word costs. Each call takes its own seed.
- **H2 Editing** (`editing`)
  A formula opens in the Workbench's formula editor: what it is for, the formula text in the app's code editor with macro chips under it, covering the chat-watching macros, odds and tracker plus an All macros reference, and a Try it rail. Try runs the text as it stands, saved or not, on a copy of the scene you pick through the formula-try route, which reads the scene as it stands with the assembler a send runs, then runs the text through the read-only formula path inside the tool time budget. It answers the result, or the words for why it stopped, such as a variable it would change, a macro error or the spent budget, with how long it took. It needs no connection and builds the whole scene with no window to fit, so entries a send would trim can still feed the macros. Nothing in the scene changes and no model is asked. A save with nothing edited sends nothing. New formula saves a blank one, refused when run until it has text.

### Drawing entity

- id: `reference/entities/htmldoc`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/htmldoc.md`
- A drawing (htmldoc) is an HTML document kept as a canonical studio piece under studio/htmldoc/<id>.json, so the existing create, read, save, delete, duplicate, collection and Library tools act on it without new verbs. The html field is model output and is stored VERBATIM and unsanitised on purpose: cleaning at rest would make the file differ from what was authored and would add a second sanitiser that could drift from the one that renders. The seal is at the point of drawing - a srcdoc iframe with sandbox="", a CSP of script-src none, connect-src none, img-src data: blob:, over DOMPurify-cleaned markup - so all CSS renders while JavaScript never runs and no remote image, font or fetch resolves. Three surfaces draw a piece through that one component: the agent transcript inline, the HTML View tab, and the Workbench editor pane. HTMLDOC_CAP is 200,000 characters, matching the preview ceiling, and a longer document is refused rather than stored and drawn part-way.

- **H2 Why it is an entity at all** (`why-it-is-an-entity-at-all`)
  A wireframe living in a chat reply cannot be renamed, filed, reopened or edited, because every one of those verbs in this app acts on a piece. Making the drawing a kind means it inherits create, read, save, delete, duplicate, collections and the Library with no new tool beyond studio_htmldoc_create; the alternative was seven bespoke tools over a transient value that loses its content on reload.
- **H2 It is untrusted content, and storing it changes nothing** (`it-is-untrusted-content-and-storing-it-changes-nothing`)
  The html field is model output and nothing in the repo ever evaluates it. It is stored unsanitised because cleaning at rest is the wrong boundary twice: the stored file would differ from what was written, and a second sanitiser could drift from the one that renders. Enforcement lives at the point of drawing - sandbox="" iframe, script-src none, connect-src none, img-src data: blob:, DOMPurify with script/iframe/object/embed/form/input/button/textarea/select/link/meta/base forbidden, that tag list held in core/render/seal-policy.ts so Kit can read the same policy without a DOM - which makes it a static drawing surface by construction. A whole document keeps its design because every style block is hoisted before sanitizing, which returns the body alone; form controls are the one thing an author designs around, so a button is drawn as a styled div. The frame is not allowed to keep the keyboard either: clicking an iframe focuses it and every keystroke then belongs to its document, killing the shell chords, so focus is handed back a turn of the loop later - a synchronous blur loses to the browser assignment.
- **H2 Where it is used** (`where-it-is-used`)
  The Workbench edits one with source and live drawing side by side, autosaving like any piece; the divider drags with pointer or keyboard and the source hides outright, both remembered, since a drawing being looked at should not spend half the room on markup. HTML View draws one at full size, opened by id from the Library or handed text from a reply; it and the editor pane fill their room rather than stopping at the inline ceiling, and both go fullscreen - the browser's own where granted, an overlay where not, Escape leaving either. Kit composes one with studio_htmldoc_create as a preview-only draft the Gate turns into a file, and edits one through htmldoc.document.update, a deferred capability found by search that patches markup, name, summary, tags or notes; that half was missing at first, a dead end rather than an inconvenience, since create-only apply rightly refuses an occupied id. regex_from_drawing turns a drawing into a regex rule's replacement.
- **H2 Size ceiling** (`size-ceiling`)
  HTMLDOC_CAP is 200,000 characters and matches the sealed preview own ceiling, so a longer document is one whose end could never be drawn. The decoder refuses it rather than storing something that renders three quarters of the way and stops, and the editor says so while the text is still editable rather than as a failed save.
- **H2 Fields** (`fields`)
  The field table on this page is generated from src/entities/htmldoc/schema.ts by scripts/docs-fields.ts and pasted verbatim, so a cell is improved by editing the schema doc comment and regenerating rather than by hand. HtmlDocBody carries five fields. Name is what the drawing is called, because every canonical kind titles itself from its body rather than its envelope. Html is the document as authored, kept exactly as written. Summary is one optional line describing what the page draws, so a shelf of drawings reads as something other than a wall of filenames. Tags are optional free-form labels for grouping without a folder, and notes is the same author-notes field every other entity carries. Producers are dashes throughout because this kind is canonical-only: no foreign format writes one.
  - **H3 `HtmlDocBody`** (`htmldocbody`)
    The generated field table for HtmlDocBody: name, html, and the optional summary, tags and notes, each with its type and the schema doc comment explaining what it means. Generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and pasted verbatim, so a cell is improved by editing the schema comment and regenerating.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Pointers out of this page to the three documents that carry the rest of the picture. Architecture.md holds the canonical model, the entity envelope shared by every kind, and the hub-and-spoke shape this entity follows in storing under studio/htmldoc by id. Ui.md documents the surfaces a person actually meets: the Workbench editor with source and live drawing side by side, the catalog-only HTML View tab that draws one at full size, and the agent transcript preview that offers a fenced html block as a drawing. Security/safe-rendering.md holds the seal itself, which is the load-bearing part of this kind and the reason storing untrusted markup verbatim is safe rather than reckless.

### Lorebook entity

- id: `reference/entities/lorebook`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/lorebook.md`
- Lorebook entity is the field-meaning authority for CanonicalLorebook as CanonicalEntity lorebook LorebookBody, the superset for world info character book and memory formats baselined on RoleCall entry type. schema.ts owns types; this page owns meanings and producers. How to read the field tables repeats generated Field Type Producers Meaning rules from docs-fields. Composition is book settings plus flat LorebookEntry list and optional categories. Book settings covers identity globals budget and LorebookCategory folders. Entries is the large flat roughly 48-field entry with concern groups for identity triggers injection ordering recursion scan filters CharacterFilter side effects ST-lineage toggles NovelAI extensions EntryContextConfig LoreBiasPhrase LoreBiasGroup and metadata. What rides escrow, not a field lists RoleCall in-memory-only fields and wire twins. See also links architecture character character-book RoleCall NovelAI and formats matrix.

- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field tables are generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; never hand-edit a cell, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the schema TypeScript type. Producers lists source formats from the doc comment or dash when untagged, including universal core content enabled triggers position depth. Meaning explains purpose; blank cells are self-evident primitives without schema annotation. Richer explanation lives only in surrounding prose never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  LorebookBody is book-level settings for identity categorization matching defaults and budget plus entries LorebookEntry array and optional categories LorebookCategory array for folders. Unlike CharacterBody the schema keeps LorebookEntry as one flat interface of roughly forty-eight fields matching RoleCall in-memory entry type without named sub-interfaces. This page groups fields by concern for readability while schema and generated table stay flat. Tables come from docs/generated/fields.lorebook.md.
- **H2 Book settings** (`book-settings`)
  Book settings hold identity categorization matching defaults and injection budget. name description lorebookType as open world character scenario rules utility other genre fandom and tags identify the book. enabled is RoleCall shelf and Press export filter default true deny by absence elsewhere. globalCaseSensitive globalMatchWholeWords globalScanDepth and globalRecursion are book-wide matching defaults entries inherit when null. tokenBudget budgetMode token or entry and entryBudget set the active cap. entries and categories complete the body. LorebookCategory child is id name sortOrder optional enabled folder grouping via entry categoryId.
  - **H3 LorebookCategory** (`lorebookcategory`)
    LorebookCategory is a folder grouping entries within a lorebook. Required id name and sortOrder with optional enabled. An entry joins one category via categoryId referencing this id. Organizational only, unrelated to activation groups.
- **H2 Entries** (`entries`)
  A LorebookEntry is one triggerable chunk with stable id referenced by categoryId and escrow twin matching. The full flat field table is followed by concern groups not separate types. Identity and content covers title comment content enabled constant. Triggers and matching plus Trigger shape gate activation with selectiveLogic and null inherit globals. Injection and placement sets position depth role. Ordering timing and grouping separates sortOrder placement priority eviction displayIndex list sticky cooldown delay groups probability. Recursion and budget covers exclude prevent delayUntil ignoreBudget useMemo. Scan sources extend match targets. Filters and side effects plus CharacterFilter EntrySideEffect EntrySideEffects are declarative never executed. ST-lineage toggles first-class vectorized group scoring automationId displayIndex. NovelAI extensions include keyRelative contextConfig and lore bias groups with EntryContextConfig LoreBiasPhrase LoreBiasGroup. Metadata is the open bag.
  - **H3 Identity and content** (`identity-and-content`)
    title is the display name mapping to SillyTavern comment. comment is a separate RoleCall internal authoring note with no ST home so ST import always nulls it. content is the injected text itself. Together with the table fields enabled and constant they form the entry identity and payload core.
  - **H3 Triggers and matching** (`triggers-and-matching`)
    triggers and secondaryTriggers are keyword or regex lists gating activation. selectiveLogic combines secondary with primary as and_any and_all not_any or not_all. triggerMode advanced when triggers carry per-trigger probability overriding entry fallback else simple. caseSensitive matchWholeWords and scanDepth are per-entry overrides of book globals where null inherits.
  - **H3 Trigger** (`trigger`)
    Trigger has required keyword and isRegex plus optional flags for regex g i m s u y, frequency as min messages before re-activation, and probability for advanced mode per-trigger chance 0-100 overriding entry fallback. Wire regex is slash pattern flags; canonical carries isRegex and flags structurally instead of a delimited string.
  - **H3 Injection and placement** (`injection-and-placement`)
    position chooses where content lands: world and character are the portable floor every format has as SillyTavern before after character definition; richer positions degrade to that floor on downcast to CCv2 character_book. depth applies when position is depth or append. role is system user or assistant for the injected message voice.
  - **H3 Ordering, timing, and grouping** (`ordering-timing-and-grouping`)
    sortOrder is placement order in the assembled prompt the universal axis every format names differently. priority is eviction priority when budget is exceeded higher survives a separate axis. displayIndex is a third list-order axis detailed under ST-lineage. sticky cooldown and delay are turn-counted timing, and delay also keeps recursion from waking an entry early. groupName with groupWeight is activation mutual exclusion; categoryId is organizational only. probability is entry-level activation chance fallback for simple triggers. sideEffects are applied to the scene's variables when an entry fires; boostIds and boostAmount raise other entries' chance.
  - **H3 Recursion and budget** (`recursion-and-budget`)
    excludeRecursion keeps the entry from being found by recursive scan of other entries so only direct matches apply. preventRecursion stops this entry content from triggering others. delayUntilRecursion activates only at recursion level N with zero as first pass. ignoreBudget always injects bypassing token budget. useMemo marks client-specific memo behavior.
  - **H3 Scan sources** (`scan-sources`)
    scanCharacterDescription scanCharacterPersonality scanUserPersona and scanScenario extend keyword matching beyond chat to those character and user texts. scanCharacterDepthPrompt and scanCreatorNotes are SillyTavern-only extras where undefined means the format has no field not that scanning is off. scanCreatorNotes lacks its own producer tag because the schema comment covers both ST sources together; the SillyTavern lorebook codec confirms matchCreatorNotes round-trips like its sibling.
  - **H3 Filters and side effects** (`filters-and-side-effects`)
    characterFilter whitelists or blacklists the entry to specific characters or character tags. sideEffects lists variable mutations to run when the entry activates. Both are declarative data shapes; Bunzai never executes them. Detailed field shapes follow in CharacterFilter EntrySideEffect and EntrySideEffects children.
  - **H3 CharacterFilter** (`characterfilter`)
    CharacterFilter has names and tags string arrays plus isExclude boolean to whitelist or blacklist which characters an entry may activate for, matching by character name or tag membership.
  - **H3 EntrySideEffect** (`entrysideeffect`)
    EntrySideEffect is one variable mutation row: type among setvar addvar incvar decvar delvar, variable name, optional value for setvar, optional amount for addvar incvar decvar, and scope local or global. Declarative data only; converter and editor never execute.
  - **H3 EntrySideEffects** (`entrysideeffects`)
    EntrySideEffects wraps effects as EntrySideEffect array plus onlyOnFirstTrigger and clearOnDeactivate booleans controlling when mutations apply relative to activation lifecycle. Still declarative never executed by Bunzai.
  - **H3 ST-lineage toggles** (`st-lineage-toggles`)
    vectorized groupOverride useGroupScoring automationId and displayIndex are authored SillyTavern-lineage fields first-classed under schema-is-editor even though only ST serializes them today. vectorized is the RAG setting not the vectors which stay escrow. groupOverride and useGroupScoring extend groupName groupWeight mutual exclusion. automationId binds Quick-Reply automation by id only automation set stays escrow. displayIndex is creator manual list order distinct from sortOrder; the synthetic PNG fixture exercises different values without a real-corpus claim, and displayIndex is never regenerated from sortOrder.
  - **H3 NovelAI extensions** (`novelai-extensions`)
    keyRelative makes keys match relative to the entry insertion point not story tail. nonStoryActivatable allows activation outside story text such as UI actions. contextConfig is NovelAI per-entry assembly prefix suffix trim and budget dials detailed in EntryContextConfig. loreBiasGroups carries NovelAI phrase bias groups applied while the entry is active or whenInactive not active.
  - **H3 EntryContextConfig** (`entrycontextconfig`)
    EntryContextConfig is NovelAI authored per-entry assembly: prefix and suffix text, tokenBudget and reservedTokens, trimDirection doNotTrim trimBottom trimTop open, insertionType newline space token, maximumTrimType sentence newline token, and insertionPosition as signed offset within the target section not canonical depth. NAI budgetPriority is deliberately not modeled here because placement lives in sortOrder one home per axis.
  - **H3 LoreBiasPhrase** (`lorebiasphrase`)
    LoreBiasPhrase holds optional sequence string sequences string array and type as NovelAI phrase type integer open enum on the wire for members of a lore bias group.
  - **H3 LoreBiasGroup** (`lorebiasgroup`)
    LoreBiasGroup is a NovelAI phrase bias group with enabled boolean, signed bias strength, phrases as LoreBiasPhrase array, and optional whenInactive generateOnce and ensureSequenceFinish controls for when and how the bias applies relative to entry activity.
  - **H3 Metadata** (`metadata`)
    metadata is an open Record string unknown bag for format-specific data that has no first-class canonical home on the entry, extensible without inventing body slots.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Per the superset rule a field needs a real wire serializer. Rides escrow because no serializer emits them: three RoleCall in-memory-only fields allowRecursion scanPreset and probabilityMode (boostIds and boostAmount also fail the gate, but the engine reads them and the World wing authors them, so they have Bunzai-authored slots) verified absent from packages/lorebook serialize and parser with hard-coded defaults and corroborated by this repo RoleCall lorebook codec never naming them. Format-specific wire bytes also ride raw twin for lossless same-format round trip: SillyTavern entry uid derives canonical id then re-emits from twin, RoleCall export envelope tree exportDate unsupportedFields and trigger runtime state. Does NOT ride escrow: ST extra scan sources vectorized groupOverride useGroupScoring automationId and displayIndex which are authored wire fields first-classed by schema-is-editor.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related authority: architecture for canonical model escrow hub-and-spoke and adapter contract; character for knowledgeRefs linking; character-book concept for embedded character_book extract and re-embed; rolecall format as baseline of the canonical entry shape; novelai format as source of contextConfig loreBiasGroups keyRelative nonStoryActivatable; formats README for the producer coverage matrix.

### Pack entity

- id: `reference/entities/pack`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/pack.md`
- Pack entity is the field-meaning authority for CanonicalPack as CanonicalEntity pack PackBody, a studio-only reusable expression sprite pack with no format adapter, no toCanonical fromCanonical, and no formats matrix row. schema.ts owns the PackBody type; this page owns meanings. How to read the field table notes every Producers cell is dash because fields are studio-authored not wire-imported. Composition is a flat name brief pack and optional groups map with no eight-group CharacterBody layout. Pack body table covers library name shelf brief the main SpritePackValue pack and per-character groups. The sprite pack hub shape defines SpritePackValue and SpritePackItem in core media pack.ts shared with character media.assets role emotion including resolvePackFace defaults. What rides escrow, not a field states packs never carry original escrow. See also links architecture character Media and formats matrix.

- **H2 How to read the field table** (`how-to-read-the-field-table`)
  The field table is generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; never hand-edit a cell, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the schema TypeScript type. Producers are always dash on this page because pack has no adapter and fields are studio-authored not carried from a wire format. Meaning explains purpose; blank meaning cells do not occur here. Richer explanation lives only in surrounding prose never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  PackBody stays flat with name optional shelf brief the pack SpritePackValue and optional groups map of named SpritePackValue entries. There are no always-present sub-objects like CharacterBody eight groups. pack and each groups entry share SpritePackValue the label-to-image hub that character media.assets role emotion also project through. SpritePackValue and SpritePackItem live in src/core/media/pack.ts not pack schema so docs-fields does not generate their table; prose covers them under The sprite pack hub shape. The body table comes from docs/generated/fields.pack.md.
- **H2 Pack body** (`pack-body`)
  PackBody fields: name is the library and tab name. brief is a short shelf card blurb never host wire. pack is the emotion pack as SpritePackValue label to image hub shared with character media. groups is optional Record string SpritePackValue for per-character groups where the flat pack is the default group when groups is absent.
- **H2 The sprite pack hub shape** (`the-sprite-pack-hub-shape`)
  SpritePackValue in core media pack.ts is the label-to-image hub for character cards and library packs. items is SpritePackItem array of id label ref and optional mime. enabled is pack-wide on off. defaultLabel picks the resting face; resolvePackFace falls back to neutral then the first item. packFromMedia and emotionAssetsFromPack convert character media.assets role emotion through this shape. groups holds one SpritePackValue per named member. An item whose mime is audio is a sound file the Stage wing's Music tab plays: held to the 32 MiB media cap, served with Range support cut from one bounded read, shown as a note glyph, printed under the same cap and imported back byte for byte from a zip.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Pack is never a toCanonical fromCanonical target so it never carries a foreign source payload. Every save path PackEditor Workbench New pack and Save as library pack from Manage Sprites writes bare CanonicalEntity pack PackBody with no original entry; body is the whole record. Rides escrow nothing because there is no wire format and original never populates. Item refs are authored inline data URIs or asset store references not binary carriers beside fields like character PNG sourceMedia.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related pages close the pack map. architecture.md covers the canonical model the adapter contract and escrow rules that packs never use. entities/character.md Media section documents the character-attached form of the same SpritePackValue hub shape. formats/README.md is the coverage matrix and notes pack has no listed adapter because it is studio-native only.

### Persona entity

- id: `reference/entities/persona`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/persona.md`
- Persona entity is the field-meaning authority for CanonicalPersona as CanonicalEntity persona PersonaBody, the user identity counterpart to character sharing prompt injection but first person user not third person char. schema.ts owns types; this page owns meanings and producers. How to read the field tables repeats docs-fields conventions. Composition is flatter with required name content and optional nested shapes; four adapters RoleCall SillyTavern Lumiverse Marinara ship today with RoleOut PNG pending. Core content separates brief never inject from content inject text and keeps sections both. Identity covers free-text age and Lumiverse pronounSet. Presentation is RoleCall theming and imageUrl. Knowledge, rating, and chat injection links lorebooks ContentRating and dual position dialects. Attribution uses ISO createdAt. Media is studio portrait only. PersonaBody assembles all fields. What rides escrow, not a field and See also close multi-record and modeled fields.

- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field tables are generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; never hand-edit a cell, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the schema TypeScript type. Producers lists source formats or dash when untagged including universal name content brief. Meaning explains purpose; blank cells are self-evident primitives. Richer explanation lives only in surrounding prose never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  PersonaBody is flatter than CharacterBody: required name and content plus eleven optional fields. Six nest named shapes sections identity presentation chatInjection attribution media; the rest are scalars or reference lists. Full table is under PersonaBody. Four adapters produce personas today RoleCall two shapes SillyTavern Lumiverse and Marinara. Legacy RoleOut PNG and foreign-V2/V3-card-as-persona are specced in specs formats personas but have no shipped adapter and rolecall format lists them pending. Tables come from docs/generated/fields.persona.md.
- **H2 Core content** (`core-content`)
  Core content is the persona voice and shelf discovery. name is the canonical identifier. brief is the short library-card blurb and is NEVER the injected text. content is the full first-person text injected as user voice. Swapping brief and content was a live RoleCall production bug; every codec replicates the fix not the bug. sectionOrder is creator display order for sections; traits are RC personality-descriptor tags. sections is a structured alternative source for content; canonical keeps both unlike source formats content-wins rule so the editor can structure-edit flat sources. Compiling sections into content is codec serialize work in fixed order appearance body personality quirks history as Label text blocks. Sections child lists those five optional string fields.
  - **H3 Sections** (`sections`)
    PersonaSections holds optional appearance body personality quirks and history strings that codecs may compile into content at serialize time in that fixed order as labeled blocks for structured editing of the user persona voice.
- **H2 Identity** (`identity`)
  Identity holds RoleCall casting-card attributes: tagline age as free text never int height pronouns as free-text display for RC ST round-trip and pronounSet as Lumiverse structured triplet additive elsewhere. Pronoun set child is subjective objective possessive strings engines can conjugate with, distinct from free-text pronouns.
  - **H3 Pronoun set** (`pronoun-set`)
    PersonaPronounSet is Lumiverse structured pronouns with required subjective objective and possessive strings for engine conjugation, additive and absent on other producers unlike free-text pronouns.
- **H2 Presentation** (`presentation`)
  Presentation is RoleCall visual identity: signatureColor, colors as character Swatch array, and imageUrl as external image URL for JSON export. PNG export instead carries the image as the file body itself using the same binary-carrier pattern architecture describes for character portraits.
- **H2 Knowledge, rating, and chat injection** (`knowledge-rating-and-chat-injection`)
  knowledgeRefs links ordered canonical lorebook ids by reference never inline copy same as CharacterBody. rating reuses ContentRating; RoleCall wire is two-way all_hours after_dark so mature narrows to after_dark on RC export a documented one-way loss. chatInjection places content with position open union covering RoleCall world character scene depth and SillyTavern prompt author_note_top author_note_bottom in_chat none plus depth role and RC wrapper injection_prefix. Platform lens visibility is injectionsForProfile in core persona platform-fields never UI hardcodes.
- **H2 Attribution** (`attribution`)
  Attribution holds credit and provenance for the persona: optional creator version createdAt and source. createdAt is ISO 8601 text not unix seconds because the rcpersona wire itself carries strings. source is an open origin platform tag such as rolecall sillytavern or custom. This is distinct from CharacterBody attribution createdAt which uses numeric unix seconds.
- **H2 Media** (`media`)
  Media is studio-side only. media.portrait is the same slot CharacterBody media.portrait fills so the studio portrait resolver can read a data-URI MediaAsset from in-editor upload. Codecs map explicit fields only so this portrait never leaks into a foreign wire format.
- **H2 PersonaBody** (`personabody`)
  PersonaBody assembles every field: required name and content; optional brief never inject; sections both kept with content; sectionOrder traits; identity presentation knowledgeRefs rating chatInjection attribution and media portrait. Table meanings restate brief content distinction sections compile rule knowledgeRefs reference-not-inline rating map and studio media. This is the top shape codecs and the studio editor read and write.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Unmodeled source facts stay in original formatId raw for same-format lossless round trip; cross-format carries only body. Rides escrow: RoleCall private ids and unmapped extensions.rolecall keys; every other persona in a SillyTavern personas backup because one entity per file imports only default or first persona and export overlays the edited one so the sealed rest re-emit byte-true; Lumiverse folder is_narrator metadata timestamps; Marinara whole game layer theming stat bars avatar crop tags and scenario with no canonical home. Does NOT ride escrow: pronounSet chatInjection sections identity presentation which are first-classed and survive cross-format.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related authority pages: architecture.md for canonical model escrow hub-and-spoke and adapter contract; entities/character.md as the sibling superset sharing ContentRating Swatch and MediaAsset; formats/rolecall.md for both RoleCall persona shapes and the cross-kind firewall against character; formats/sillytavern.md for the personas-backup codec; formats/README.md for the producer coverage matrix.

### Preset entity

- id: `reference/entities/preset`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/preset.md`
- Preset entity is the field-meaning authority for CanonicalPreset and PresetBody, the canonical prompt-manager model shared by the SillyTavern, RoleCall, Marinara, and Lumiverse adapters. It covers ordered prompt blocks, system, user, assistant, and tool roles, hierarchical groups, sampler and generation settings, templates, behavior, API and media options, and interactive choices. Generic CLI commands, Library, Workbench, and Kit lifecycle tools operate on the same body. LiveBuild preview orders enabled blocks and estimates tokens without resolving macros, groups, or choices. behaviorRefs links standalone regex sets, while quickReplyRefs links standalone quick-reply sets; Press resolves both by kind and id when exporting a preset bundle. Escrow remains reserved for source-wire details that editors do not author. Other SillyTavern preset files land here when Bunzai can use them: KoboldAI, TextGen and NovelAI sampler sets onto samplers with a samplerSet flag, system prompts as the main block, and reasoning templates as a reasoning field the scene transcript uses; each is seeded with ST's stock prompt order. Instruct and context templates are the separate promptformat piece. A preset is a writing setup with the Script Sheet: separate kinds read one way through asPreset.

- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field tables are generated by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; never hand-edit a cell, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the schema TypeScript type. Producers lists wire formats verified for the shape even without a shipping adapter, or dash when untagged. Meaning explains purpose; blank cells are self-evident primitives. Richer explanation lives only in surrounding prose never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  PresetBody requires a name and ordered prompts manuscript plus thirteen optional fields: groups and choices collections; samplers, systemPrompts, templates, behavior, apiOptions, media, and generation sub-objects; description and enabled scalars; and behaviorRefs plus quickReplyRefs link arrays. Grouping mirrors authoring rather than app storage. The complete generated field table lives under Full composition.
- **H2 Prompt blocks and groups** (`prompt-blocks-and-groups`)
  PresetPrompt is one manuscript row mapping directly to SillyTavern, RoleCall, or Marinara sections. id remains the verbatim source identifier because ordering, references, markers, and isDefault depend on it. role accepts system, user, assistant, or tool; adapters must preserve a supported tool role or report its loss instead of silently coercing it. Per-block systemPrompt differs from preset-level systemPrompts. placement uses the shared position-picker union, while marker blocks use markerSlot; the feature markers trackers, linkedTrackers, storyboards and skills put that feature's messages where the block stands with its role (an append marker becomes its own message at that depth, the block's own text is not sent), and a preset without one keeps the feature's own spot. SillyTavern import reads the slot from the id, so only a marker whose id is its slot survives that round trip. Injection, grouping, XML wrapping, trigger, default, and extras fields complete the block. PresetGroup first-classes authored folders; SillyTavern derives divider ids for native drafts and escrows original divider ids only for imported groups.
  - **H3 Groups** (`groups`)
    PresetGroup is a category or folder of blocks first-class because Marinara groups and RC categories are authored. ST has no real groups and encodes dividers; the ST codec derives divider identifier deterministically from group id for vaud-authored groups and escrows original divider id only for imported ST groups so add rename move never invent escrow. Fields include id name optional content parentGroupId order enabled extras.
- **H2 Sampler and generation settings** (`sampler-and-generation-settings`)
  Sampler and generation knobs are all optional: absent stays absent because round-trip forbids emitting values the source never had. Build applies defaults at compile time none stored on the entity. Surfaces match presets-core SamplerSettings and GenerationSettings. temperature topP topK topA minP penalties maxContext maxTokens promptPostProcessing contextMode tokens or messages and maxMessages when messages mode, which a scene honors: it sends the newest N prior messages plus the one it answers, greeting kept, and a scene override wins. Generation child covers seed completions maxContextUnlocked biasPreset.
  - **H3 Generation** (`generation`)
    Generation settings include optional seed completions as number of completions presets-core n maxContextUnlocked boolean and biasPreset string. All optional following the same absent-stays-absent round-trip rule as samplers.
- **H2 System prompts, templates, and behavior** (`system-prompts-templates-and-behavior`)
  Three optional sub-objects assemble requests around the manuscript with every nested field optional too. systemPrompts carries always-on utility prompts impersonation newChat newGroupChat newExampleChat continueNudge groupNudge assistantPrefill assistantImpersonation. Templates child has worldInfoFormat scenarioFormat personalityFormat Handlebars-ish card field strings. Behavior child has wrapInQuotes namesBehavior as ST numeric enum kept verbatim sendIfEmpty continuePrefill continuePostfix message-assembly flags.
  - **H3 Templates** (`templates`)
    PresetTemplates holds optional worldInfoFormat scenarioFormat and personalityFormat Handlebars-ish format strings for card fields matching presets-core TemplateFormats used when assembling requests around the manuscript.
  - **H3 Behavior** (`behavior`)
    PresetBehavior message-assembly flags: wrapInQuotes, namesBehavior as ST names_behavior numeric enum kept verbatim not decoded to a named union, sendIfEmpty, continuePrefill, and continuePostfix. All optional.
- **H2 API and media options** (`api-and-media-options`)
  API and media options hold provider and request-level toggles as two optional sub-objects with every nested field optional too matching presets-core APIOptions and MediaSettings. apiOptions covers streamResponses for streaming replies, claudeUseSystemPrompt and useMakerSuiteSystemPrompt for provider system-prompt shapes, squashSystemMessages, functionCalling, showThoughts, reasoningEffort as a string dial, enableWebSearch, and requestImages. Media child covers imageInlining, inlineImageQuality string, and videoInlining for how media is embedded into provider requests. Absent fields stay absent under the round-trip law the same as samplers; nothing here is executed by the converter or LiveBuild preview.
  - **H3 Media** (`media`)
    PresetMedia optional imageInlining boolean inlineImageQuality string and videoInlining boolean control how images and video are inlined into provider requests for the preset.
- **H2 Choices walkthrough** (`choices-walkthrough`)
  PresetChoice unifies Marinara choiceBlock and RC ChoiceGroup: authored now run later when a run surface exists. Answers feed getvar key gate blocks via enablesPrompts or write scoped state via set. type one many toggle input with default shaped to match; options min placeholder suggestions separator randomPick sortOrder extras.
