Options child carries value enablesPrompts and set first-class so each source keeps its effect without dropping the others. In a scene the answers are kept per preset and group (ChatStore rule 42) and applied by the assembler as RoleCall's loader does: groups are checked as parseGroup checks them, the answers are the baseline for the blocks a group names, the scene's own switches win, choice and chosen macros read them, and set is kept but never applied.
  - **H3 Options** (`options`)
    PresetChoiceOption has id label optional description Marinara value for the choice variable RC enablesPrompts block ids gated while selected and RC set scoped state writes. All three effects are first-class; a source using one leaves others empty rather than collapsing shapes.
- **H2 Full composition** (`full-composition`)
  PresetBody contains name, description, enabled state, ordered prompt blocks, group categories, samplers, systemPrompts, templates, behavior, apiOptions, media, generation, and choices. behaviorRefs links canonical regex sets and quickReplyRefs links canonical quick-reply sets without copying either payload into the stored preset. Disabled presets skip export. Dividers are never prompt blocks; groups model structure directly.
- **H2 Linked regex sets** (`linked-regex-sets`)
  behaviorRefs names standalone regex pieces by canonical id, keeping each set independently editable. SillyTavern and RoleCall can embed linked regex rows under extensions.regex_scripts; Lumiverse attaches scripts by preset_id; Marinara has no preset-level regex attachment. Each adapter owns that decision, and Press reports links that cannot ride instead of dropping them silently. quickReplyRefs applies the same canonical link model to quick-reply sets. Workbench exposes both link collections as checkboxes, and Press resolves every rider by kind and id so identical ids across different content kinds cannot collide.
- **H2 The writing family** (`the-writing-family`)
  A preset is one of the writing setups a scene can follow, with the Script Sheet: separate kinds that read one way. Each kind.ts gives asPreset, a preset's being its own body, and src/core/writing.ts reads the family from that. The scene's Writing slot records the kind with the id so one picker lists presets and sheets together, and a sheet joins the preset deck in the Library, shown as Writing.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Design principle: model what the editor or domain authors canonically; escrow pure source-wire fidelity no editor touches. groups and choices are canonical not escrow because Marinara and RC author them. Rides escrow: ST per-character prompt_order overrides, Marinara variable layer, original divider identifier for imported ST PresetGroup, and extras bags on prompt group and choice sealed leftovers. Does NOT ride escrow: groups and choices themselves first-classed.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related authority is architecture.md for the canonical model escrow hub-and-spoke and the adapter contract. That contract currently excludes preset from the FormatAdapter union so no toCanonical fromCanonical pair ships yet while Library and Workbench still author PresetBody directly through createAndOpenPreset and PresetEditorView.

### Promptbook entity

- id: `reference/entities/promptbook`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/promptbook.md`
- Promptbooks are ordered workflows built from private, reply and after phases. Each phase owns preset blocks, linked Library cards, optional Agent and model, conditions, budgets and forward hand-offs. Phases, Cards and Board share one revision-checked draft. Runtime composition reuses scene assembly, permission gates, tools, Skill activation and state writes; incomplete workflows fail closed while completed or stopped checkpoints recover. Inspector records phase requests and usage. Native JSON preserves reference IDs without embedding dependencies. Rehearsal checks phase conditions only, Board wires are visual, and attachments, card versions and arbitrary branching remain unbuilt. Evidence is unit and source based; live and packaged verification remain unavailable.


### Prompt format entity

- id: `reference/entities/promptformat`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/promptformat.md`
- Prompt format entity is the canonical piece for SillyTavern instruct and context templates: a name plus optional instruct and context halves, each ST's own field names with scalar values, and optional notes. One piece holds both because ST binds them by name. Nothing applies it, since Bunzai has no text-completion connection; the Library card says so in a computed line and the Workbench opens it read-only. Extensions may stage reviewed changes to the stored name, scalar template fields and notes, but the templates remain inert and are not applied to prompt assembly. The sillytavern-promptformat codec reads either file or a master wrapper and writes one half back as its file or a pair as the wrapper; pairing happens in the Library import.

- **H2 Fields** (`fields`)
  Four fields: name; instruct, a flat record of ST instruct keys such as input_sequence, output_sequence, stop_sequence, wrap and names_behavior; context, the same for story_string, example_separator and chat_start; and notes. Every value ST wrote is kept under ST's own key name.
- **H2 Import and export** (`import-and-export`)
  The sillytavern-promptformat codec reads either template file, an instruct template or a context template, and also ST's master wrapper that holds both under instruct and context keys, provided the wrapper carries no sysprompt and no prompts array. Pairing a same-named instruct and context into one piece happens in the Library import through pairPromptFormats, not in the codec. Export writes one file when the piece holds one half, identical to the source file, and the master wrapper when it holds both. The format spec lives.

### Quick-reply set entity

- id: `reference/entities/quickreply`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/quickreply.md`
- Quick-reply set entity is the canonical piece for SillyTavern quick-reply sets: CanonicalEntity quickreply with a named list of buttons, each a label and the message pressing it sends, usually a slash-command script that nothing in Bunzai executes. It is a kind so a preset can link one through quickReplyRefs and the Press can print them together. The body keeps what a person authors; every other per-button switch rides extras and the source file rides original, so export writes it back. Its descriptor is kind.ts. It shows as the Quick replies deck with a New quick reply action, the Workbench quick-reply editor, and Kit's create tool; Kit has no editing capabilities for it by design. The sillytavern-quickreply codec reads and writes the files.

- **H2 Fields** (`fields`)
  Fields: the two tables on the page are generated from the schema's doc comments by scripts/docs-fields.ts and pasted verbatim from docs/generated/fields.quickreply.md, so a cell is never edited by hand: the page says to improve the schema doc comment and regenerate rather than editing a cell. The first table is one button, QuickReply, with its id, label, message, title, hidden and extras fields, and the second is the set that holds the buttons, QuickReplyBody, with its name, replies, extras and notes. Each row gives the field, its type, the producers named in the comment and its meaning.
  - **H3 `QuickReply`** (`quickreply`)
    QuickReply is one button: id, minted at import when the wire has none; label, which may be empty for icon-only buttons; message, what pressing it sends, data never executed; optional title as hover text; optional hidden, ST's isHidden kept for round-trips; and extras, unmodelled wire fields re-merged on export.
  - **H3 `QuickReplyBody`** (`quickreplybody`)
    QuickReplyBody is the set: name, which titles it in the Library; replies, the ordered buttons; extras for set-level wire fields such as disableSend and color; and notes, the author's own notes.
- **H2 Where it shows** (`where-it-shows`)
  Where it shows: the Library's Quick replies deck with a New quick reply action that saves an empty set; the Workbench quick-reply editor; and Kit's quickreply-create tool. Kit has no editing capabilities for a set, which the Workbench owns; the kind completeness test lists that exemption.
- **H2 Import and export** (`import-and-export`)
  Import and export: the sillytavern-quickreply codec reads and writes SillyTavern quick-reply set files, so a set comes into the studio from SillyTavern and goes back out the same way. The page's earlier sections explain what that round trip keeps: the label and message the canonical body models, every other per-button switch in extras, and the source file in original on the envelope, so export writes it back. For anything about the files themselves, the page points to the SillyTavern format reference, formats/sillytavern.md, and says no more here.

### Entities

- id: `reference/entities/README`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/README.md`
- Entities indexes Bunzai's canonical content kinds: CanonicalEntity Kind Body wrappers where body is the superset for one kind. The kinds are a closed set: exactly the src/entities folders holding a kind.ts descriptor, which bun run kinds collects into src/generated/kinds.ts for identity and src/generated/kind-schemas.ts for decoders and storage parts, and every list of kinds in the app (storage folders, the parse boundary, adapter contracts, decks, search, receipts, Kit and New actions) reads those seams; adding one is extending/new-kind. Which kinds have format adapters is the generated format matrix. Every kind stores at studio/<kind>/<id>.json via resolveStudioPath. Generated field tables explains each page's verbatim tables from docs-fields.ts. Reference pages link character, lorebook, pack, persona, preset, Script Sheet, regex, quick-reply set, tool, dice, formula, tracker, prompt format and drawing. Cross-cutting ties the sprite pack hub shape, the RegexScript twin, knowledgeRefs and behaviorRefs, and persona as character's opposite-voice counterpart.

- **H2 Generated field tables** (`generated-field-tables`)
  Each entity page embeds its field table verbatim from docs/generated/fields.<kind>.md. That file is generated from live schema JSDoc by scripts/docs-fields.ts via bun run scripts/docs-fields.ts and verified with --check. Do not hand-edit a table cell on an entity page; improve the doc comment in src/entities/<kind>/schema.ts and regenerate so the published table stays the schema truth.
- **H2 Reference pages** (`reference-pages`)
  Reference pages: index table of the entity field references. Character covers identity, persona and voice, prompts and injections, greetings and examples, media, presentation, settings and behavior, attribution and discovery. Lorebook covers the world info entry set, triggers, position and depth, and per-entry and per-book settings. Pack is a reusable expression sprite pack attachable to several characters. Persona is the user identity counterpart with name, content, sections and chat injection. Preset is a chat-completion prompt-manager preset with ordered blocks, samplers and behavior. Regex is a named ordered find-and-replace rule set, the card twin and standalone files. Quick-reply set is a page of buttons that send prepared text, linked from presets. Tool, dice and formula are small pieces a model may call, roll or run, and Tracker is state a scene keeps: named typed fields as a record or a list, shown to the model every turn and updated by one tool. Prompt format holds SillyTavern instruct and context templates kept whole, read by nothing yet. Drawing is a sealed HTML page shown in HTML View rather than a Library deck.
- **H2 Cross-cutting** (`cross-cutting`)
  Shared shapes span kinds. SpritePackValue and SpritePackItem in core media pack.ts are the hub for Character media.assets role emotion and Pack pack groups. RegexScript makes Character behavior.regexScripts the card-embedded twin of the standalone Regex set. knowledgeRefs and behaviorRefs let Character link standalone Lorebook or Regex entities by canonical id. Persona is Character opposite-voice counterpart sharing prompt-injection machinery as first person user versus third person char.

### Regex entity

- id: `reference/entities/regex`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/regex.md`
- Canonical field reference for CanonicalRegexSet: CanonicalEntity of kind regex around RegexSetBody, the superset union of real find/replace-script formats. src/entities/regex/schema.ts owns the types; this page owns what each field means and which formats produce it. A set is a named ordered rule collection mapping to ST standalone or card bundle, RC script, Lumi folder or pack slice, Risu card or module list, or Marinara collection, stored under studio/regex id json. A rule is data applied as budgeted String.replace never eval; engine and editor concepts live on sibling pages. How to read the field tables explains generated docs-fields embedding and Field Type Producers Meaning columns. Composition frames RegexSetBody as small name description enabled rules, with nearly all surface on flat RegexRule identity content placement and vaud-engine-only controls plus nested RegexRuleCondition. The set table covers name description enabled and rules. The rule table stores find and flags separately, documents all 21 fields, and nests Rule chaining for condition ruleId matched. The character-card twin is CharacterRegexScript for Risu customScripts, re-exported as character RegexScript without full RegexRule alias. What rides escrow, not a field covers original format raw versus rule-level extras, and firstMatchOnly condition overlay as first-class non-escrow engine fields. See also links architecture character regex-engine regex-editor and formats README. Where a scene uses a set covers the Final Cut wing's Regex tab, the order sets run in, the display and prompt phases and their depths.

- **H2 How to read the field tables** (`how-to-read-the-field-tables`)
  Field tables are generated from the schema by scripts/docs-fields.ts and embedded verbatim; do not hand-edit cells, improve the schema doc comment and regenerate so docs follow. Field is the canonical name with trailing question mark for optional. Type is the TypeScript type in the schema. Producers are source formats named in the field schema doc comment; a dash means no specific producer tagged, covering universal core fields such as id label find flags replace phases enabled sortOrder or provenance described only in surrounding prose. Meaning is purpose; blank Meaning is a self-evident primitive without schema annotation, while non-obvious fields pull meaning from the schema comment. Richer explanation lives in prose around a table never inside a cell.
- **H2 Composition** (`composition`)
  RegexSetBody is small: name, optional description, set-level enabled switch, and ordered rules list. Nearly all entity surface lives inside RegexRule, a flat interface whose 21 fields still group as three concerns: identity and content for what the rule is and does, placement for when and where it runs in the pipeline, and vaud-engine-only controls for behavior no wire format can carry. RegexRuleCondition is the small nested shape used by the single condition field. The composition figure and following sections walk each shape; every field table is pasted verbatim from docs/generated/fields.regex.md.
- **H2 The set** (`the-set`)
  RegexSetBody is the top-level shape: the set name and description, its on/off switch, and the rule list. name and description are unannotated string primitives with empty Meaning cells. enabled is optional boolean set-level on/off matching the Library shelf enable switch and the lorebook enabled precedent; absent means on, always read as enabled not equal false; an off set skips export the way an off lorebook does; additive and optional so the risu character codec and in-flight set editor stay compatible without breaking older writers. rules is the ordered RegexRule array that holds nearly all of the entity surface.
- **H2 The rule** (`the-rule`)
  find and flags store separately as Risu RC Lumi encoding; the SillyTavern codec folds and unfolds its single slash pattern flags wire string. flags are stored verbatim including Risu extension tokens such as gu cbs never stripped except at compile time. Reading the table: id through trimStrings is identity and content; phases through characterIds is placement; firstMatchOnly through overlay are three vaud-engine-only controls; enabled and sortOrder are membership state; extras is the escrow bag. Key fields include stable id, bare find without delimiters, default g flags, useFlags for Risu ableFlag, replace that may carry groups match sugar CBS or HTML rendered only via SealedHtmlPreview, trimStrings, phases, Lumiverse targets, substituteFind, min and max depth, runOnEdit, Marinara characterIds, firstMatchOnly condition overlay as engine-only with travel lint under every lens, and extras for per-platform leftovers. Rule chaining nests under condition: earlier-rule gate with ruleId and matched, one pass sortOrder never forward reference.
  - **H3 Rule chaining** (`rule-chaining`)
    condition is the only field that uses a nested shape. It chains a rule firing on whether an earlier rule in the same set did or did not apply on the same pass: one pass, sortOrder order, never a forward reference. A ruleId naming a rule that has not run yet or does not exist skips the carrying rule with an honest trace reason instead of guessing. Nested fields are ruleId string and matched boolean.
- **H2 The character-card twin** (`the-character-card-twin`)
  CharacterRegexScript in the same schema file is the narrow card-embedded Risu customScripts shape: singular phase instead of phases, and no id enabled or sortOrder because the card wire has none. entities/character/schema.ts re-exports this type as its own RegexScript so there is one definition not two. It is deliberately not RegexRule itself: a full alias would change CharacterBehavior.regexScripts wire contract and break the Risu codec round-trip. It stays the small card-embedded twin until a real migration replaces customScripts with references to standalone regex entities. See character entity CharacterBehavior. Fields: optional label, find, replace, phase as open pipeline string union, optional flags and useFlags for Risu flag and ableFlag.
- **H2 What rides escrow, not a field** (`what-rides-escrow-not-a-field`)
  Anything a single source format keeps but no canonical field expresses stays in escrow formatId raw and survives same-format round-trip byte-for-byte under the shared entity doctrine. For SillyTavern this is original sillytavern-regex raw script rows for exact re-projection. A second finer-grained mechanism is rule-level extras: a canonical RegexRule field sealing per-platform leftovers with no first-class slot such as Lumi telemetry scope script_id or ST id uuid, re-merged untouched on export at row grain so edited or reordered rules keep their own leftovers rather than whole-file original replay. Does not ride escrow because modeled: firstMatchOnly condition and overlay have no wire home on any platform and travel-lint marks all three under every lens, but they are first-class canonical fields the Workbench can set and applyRules executes; export emits nothing for them on formats that cannot carry them.
- **H2 Where a scene uses a set** (`where-a-scene-uses-a-set`)
  A scene switches library sets on one by one (the Final Cut wing's Regex tab, Chat.regex.sets, ChatStore rule 42) and they run in that order, each set's rules by sortOrder. Two phases run, RoleCall's two live placements: display, drawn on stable messages and never stored, with depth 0 the last message of the take, and prompt, the conversation messages of the request a turn sends, with depth 0 the message being answered. Rule targets are honoured, disabled rules and sets are skipped, and a prompt rule naming message_roles in extras runs for those roles only. The input, output, lorebook and reasoning phases are not run by a scene. Every run is in the sandbox worker.
- **H2 See also** (`see-also`)
  Related authorities: architecture for canonical model escrow hub-and-spoke and adapter contract; character entity for CharacterBehavior.regexScripts and the RegexScript card twin defined here; regex-engine for how a set runs applyRules AST travel-lint; regex-editor for Workbench authoring three modes and test bench; formats README for coverage matrix of every format and fields it produces.

### Script Sheet entity

- id: `reference/entities/scriptsheet`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/scriptsheet.md`
- The Script Sheet entity page covers CanonicalScriptSheet: a name, one instruction, an optional description and optional samplers of the preset kind, ported from RoleCall's single-instruction setup. The writing family section explains that presets and sheets are separate kinds that read one way through each kind.ts asPreset in core/writing.ts: one scene slot recording kind and id, one reading through the family reader presetReader, one Writing deck, export through every platform's preset format via formatsFor except RoleCall's own sheet format that writes script_sheet and reads the older promptbook key, and Open as preset. What a sheet sends lists sheetPrompts' RoleCall order. The rules section covers refused context macros, blank text, the send-time refusal from sendProblem with RoleCall's words, and shape-only reading with the 100,000-character cap. Editing names RoleCall's sheet editor in the Prompting wing and on the Workbench with description and samplers, and the Library's blank New.

- **H2 The writing family** (`the-writing-family`)
  Presets and Script Sheets are separate kinds that read one way: each kind.ts gives asPreset, read by src/core/writing.ts, and a promptbook will join the same way. The scene's Writing choice records kind and id in ChatPreset.kind under rule 19, so one picker lists both, and loadouts and a character's starting setup carry the kind. The turn reads a sheet through the family reader presetReader as its blocks, so assembly, Final Cut, the Booth and the trace treat it as a preset. The sheet joins the preset deck, shown as Writing. Every platform's preset format writes a sheet via formatsFor except RoleCall, whose own Script Sheet format writes script_sheet beside the blocks and reads such files back, including the older promptbook key. The Workbench's Open as preset makes a preset from a saved sheet.
- **H2 What a sheet sends** (`what-a-sheet-sends`)
  sheetPrompts in core/preset/script-sheet.ts lays the instruction out as ordinary blocks in RoleCall's order: the instruction, the card system prompt as charPrompt, the context slots for description, personality, scenario, persona, lore, summary, memories and examples, the chat history, and the card post-history instructions as charInstruction, last.
- **H2 The rules** (`the-rules`)
  Because context is seated automatically the instruction may not insert it: context macros such as description, lore and message_history in any spelling are refused, while char and user are fine, and blank text is refused. The editors check as you type; a turn with a stored sheet that breaks the rules stops with RoleCall's words through the kind's sendProblem. Reading checks only the shape and the 100,000-character cap, so a later rule change never makes a stored sheet unreadable.
- **H2 Editing** (`editing`)
  The kind's one editor is RoleCall's Script Sheet editor in src/ui/_shared/script-sheet-editor.tsx, opened from the Prompting wing and on the Workbench through script-sheet-bench.tsx with the description and samplers above it. The Library's New script sheet saves a blank sheet that the editor will not send until it has an instruction.

### Skill entity

- id: `reference/entities/skill`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/skill.md`
- The skill entity page covers CanonicalSkill: instructions the model gets only when they matter, with a name, a one-line description the model reads when it may pick the skill, and macro-rendered text. A skill turns on always, when its triggers match, or while the model has it open, and its triggers are a lorebook entry's own fields matched by the lore engine's own matcher. A scene links skills by id in its prompting like tools and trackers, a turn reads them through its one library reader, and the ones on ride as one Skills block at the preset's skills marker, reserved by the budget so they are never dropped. The model opens and closes picked skills with open_skill and close_skill, kept in the skills scene-state slot. The Workbench editor supports activation and trigger editing. Extensions may stage reviewed changes to the name, description or authored instructions as inert canonical text; draft review and apply own persistence. Promptbook cards provide named placements and Together groups with fixed budget protection. Live verification remains unavailable.

- **H2 When it is on** (`when-it-is-on`)
  A skill's when is always, mentioned or picked. Always puts it in the prompt every turn while linked. Mentioned uses a lorebook entry's own trigger fields, decoded by the lorebook's trigger decoder and matched by the lore engine's matchTrigger and selectiveLogicMatches over the newest scanDepth messages, the one being answered included, newest first, with regex triggers vetted; no triggers or a scan depth of 0 never match, and a trigger's frequency and probability are kept but unused. Picked is on while the model has it open, and with closeWhenUnmentioned it closes when its triggers stop matching, unless it has no triggers or a scan depth of 0. Each verdict carries a plain sentence of why, returned on the assembly result.
- **H2 Open and closed** (`open-and-closed`)
  Which skills are open is scene state: the fields slot skills, one field per skill id holding true while open, written by the turn and the person, so a swipe, regenerate, cut or fork shows the open set of what is shown. A scene linking picked skills gives the model open_skill and close_skill while Narrator Tool Access is on. open_skill lists every picked skill's name and purpose in its description, answers with the skill's instructions rendered read-only with unanswered macros stripped, and opens it once through the turn's tool door. close_skill closes an open one and says so of one that is not. Both are stateScoped writes at tier narrator.skill; an unknown name is refused with the list. A close rule that holds is closed by the turn itself.
- **H2 In a scene and in the prompt** (`in-a-scene-and-in-the-prompt`)
  A scene links skills by id in ChatPrompting.skills, in order with no duplicates, by the same rule as tools and trackers. A turn reads them through its one library reader, leaving out gone, unreadable and same-named skills. The skills that are on ride as one system message headed Skills, each name a heading over its rendered text, in the scene's order, and a blank one adds nothing. The block goes where the preset's skills marker stands with that block's role, otherwise after the leading system messages after the trackers' and storyboards' blocks. The budget reserves it like the library trackers' block, so history gives way and it is never dropped. The Tokens sheet labels it Skills.
- **H2 Editing and placement** (`editing-and-placement`)
  The Workbench editor uses the existing revision-checked piece session for instructions, activation and shared triggers. Extensions can stage a reviewed update to name, description or authored instructions through the semantic draft API; those values remain inert and require explicit host review and revision-matched apply. Primary matches can require any or all, with older pieces defaulting to any. Promptbook Skill cards occupy named positions and can join adjacent Prompt cards in one message, retaining existing activation and fixed budget protection. Duplicate named-slot placement through include is refused. The dedicated scene Skills panel and manual close route remain unbuilt. Editor and placement evidence is unit based, with live UI verification blocked by browser access.

### Tool entity

- id: `reference/entities/tool`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/tool.md`
- The tool entity page covers CanonicalTool, a tool a model may call while it writes, kept as data and never code: a name, a description the model reads, a list of typed fields the model fills in, and one action from a fixed first-party set in src/core/tools.ts. The fields section explains field names, the four types, choices and required flags, and how the function schema and the strict argument check are both made from the one field list so they cannot disagree. The actions section covers roll, which rolls the dice the model writes into a text field, dice, which rolls a linked library dice piece, formula, which runs a linked formula on a copy of the turn's macro context, and record, which only records the call; all four are read-only. The scene section explains how a scene links tools by id in its prompting, the gates they share with the lorebook lookup, how linked tools are read and checked, how call names are made safe and unique, why the same seed rolls the same dice, and that the Control Booth links them, each as a tile with its icon, cost and a switch. Editing describes the tool editor, with its action choice, the shared field editor, and a rail that shows the tool as the AI meets it and tries it. Every connection takes tools except refusing models, and a recognized server refusal is reported in one plain sentence, never retried without tools.

- **H2 Fields** (`fields`)
  Each field has a name made of a lowercase letter then lowercase letters, digits and underscores, a description, a type of text, number, boolean or choice, and a required flag. Only a choice field carries choices, at least one and unique, and it must carry them. A tool's field names must be unique, and no product limit caps how many fields it has; the names keep the providers' sixty-four character rule. The function schema sent to the model, toolParameters, and the check of the model's arguments, readToolCall, are both built from this one field list, so the two can never disagree. Unknown arguments, missing required arguments, values of the wrong type and choices outside the list are all refused, and the refusal is told to the model.
- **H2 Actions** (`actions`)
  There are four first-party actions. Roll rolls the dice the model writes into one of the tool's text fields through the dice module, any dice with a die of two or more sides (a long roll is summarised past twenty dice), other text refused when the arguments are read, and the trace keeps each die and the total. Dice rolls a library dice piece by its id, a roll, a table or a pool, answered as that piece rolls. Formula runs a library formula by its id on a copy of the turn's macro context and answers with what it renders; one that would change a variable, has a macro error or is blank is refused, and the refusal is told to the model. Record changes nothing and answers Recorded. All four are read-only.
- **H2 In a scene** (`in-a-scene`)
  A scene links tools by id in its prompting settings, ChatPrompting.tools, in order and without duplicates. They are offered only when Narrator Tool Access is on, the connection takes tools and the turn is not a Continue, the same gates the lorebook lookup uses. Every connection takes tools, local and custom servers included, except a model that refuses them whatever its host. When a server still refuses them, a list of recognized wordings (Ollama, llama.cpp, vLLM and two generic ones) gives one sentence naming the model and saying to turn off Narrator Tool Access or pick another model; any other refusal shows the server's own error. Nothing is saved as a reply and the turn is never retried without tools. The Story Director reports a recognized refusal as no tool calling, and a Final Cut pass advises tagged mode. library-tools.ts reads each linked tool through strict decoders, call names are made safe and unique, rolls are seeded per turn, and the Control Booth links tools as tiles.
- **H2 Editing** (`editing`)
  A tool opens in the Workbench's tool editor: the shared header, which says in how many scenes it is linked and lists them on a click, when the AI should use it, a choice of what the tool does (roll a field's dice, roll a dice piece, run a formula, or only note the call), and the fields in the field editor a tracker shares, where a field also says whether the AI must fill it. A piece choice starts with none picked. Renaming the field a roll reads carries the roll from the name the field began with, and deleting it leaves the roll with no field picked. The rail's first tab shows the call name, what it takes as plain lines read off the provider's parameters, and the token cost; its Try tab calls the tool through the real reader and runner and shows exactly the answer the AI would get. A draft the decoder refuses cannot be saved or tried. New tool saves a blank record tool.

### Tracker entity

- id: `reference/entities/tracker`
- path: `docs/reference/entities/tracker.md`
- The tracker entity page covers CanonicalTracker, a library piece for state a scene keeps, such as a character's health or a party's roster, kept as data and never code. A tracker has a name, a description, a layout of record or list, typed fields, a note telling the model how to update it, an optional macro template for its prompt line, an optional icon and a change style the Messenger draws. The page explains the field rules shared with tools and held in one small module the decoder and the core both use, including a number's top taken from another field and field icons from the shipped glyph set, the absence of product limits beyond hostile-input ceilings, the two layouts, how the state lives in two scene-state slot families, how a scene links trackers, gets a Trackers block the budget measures and never cuts, and can switch change visuals off, the tracker macro, the single update_trackers tool, which applies a batch all or nothing and writes only deltas on the reply being written, and the read that answers what each reply changed. The Editing section describes the tracker editor, with its settings, the field editor a tool shares, and a rail showing what the AI gets, and says the Messenger's Trackers panel links, shows and edits them and that each reply shows what it changed in the style the tracker chose.

- **H2 Fields** (`fields`)
  A field has a name of a lowercase letter then lowercase letters, digits and underscores up to the providers' sixty-four character rule, a description, and a type of text, number, boolean or choice. The name rule, types and choices rule are shared with a tool's fields. A choice field has unique choices. A number field may have a min and a max, or instead take its top from another number field of the same tracker through maxField, which the decoder refuses when it names no field, a non-number, itself, a loop, or comes with max. A value above such a top is brought down to it, never refused, the top settled first, and a write that moves a top stores the clamped dependents in the same batch, so stored equals read; a top below min leaves the min. A field may have a well-formed icon id, not checked against the glyph table, a tracker an icon and a change style the Messenger draws; and any field a default that must fit. There are no product limits beyond the hostile-input ceilings.
- **H2 Layouts** (`layouts`)
  A record is one set of fields. A list is rows, each keyed by a text name that is not blank and any length, holding every field; the name key is reserved on a list, so no list field is called name. A new row starts from the fields' defaults. The model finds a row whatever the case of the name it gives, and a row keeps the name it was made with, because the state folds by the exact name. The layout decides which slot family holds the tracker's state, so a record and a list never share one.
- **H2 State** (`state`)
  A linked tracker keeps its state in a scene-state slot: tracker-record followed by the piece id uses the fields strategy with one delta per field, and tracker-list followed by the piece id uses the keyed-list strategy with one delta per row. These are two slot families declared once, and the store only folds them. Every shape check lives in the core tracker module, which knows the piece: trackerValue reads the current value with defaults filled, and a value that does not fit its field reads as the field's default. Because a write is a delta on the reply version being written, a regenerate, swipe, cut and fork all leave the tracker where the shown history says.
- **H2 In a scene** (`in-a-scene`)
  A scene links trackers by id in its prompting, in order without duplicates. A turn reads each through the tracker kind's strict decoder and leaves out one that is gone, fails the decoder, or whose name another linked tracker has. The prompt carries one system block headed Trackers right after the Props block, or where the preset's linkedTrackers marker stands, every turn, with each tracker's text: its template run read-only in the turn's macro pass, with its errors in the macro report, or the default rendering. Values the model wrote sit on one line, nothing is cut, and the budget measures the block so a tight window drops history. Templates read each other whatever the order, and the tracker macro reads the same state. The scene's trackerChanges switch turns the change visuals under each reply off, which the Messenger reads; only an off switch is stored. The Messenger's Trackers panel links and unlinks trackers, draws each value at the take's tip and edits it as the tool's own update, written as message-level deltas on the tip message.
- **H2 The update tool** (`the-update-tool`)
  When Narrator Tool Access is on, the connection takes tools and the turn is not a Continue, a scene with linked trackers offers one tool, update_trackers, taking updates each naming a tracker with an optional row, remove flag and set object. Its description is one fixed sentence plus one line per tracker with every field's type, range or choices and meaning, and the note, never cut, naming a top taken from another field as in hp from zero to hp_max. Items apply in order and the batch is all or nothing; a number past its range or such a top is clamped. The model is answered with only what changed. The deltas join the turn's pending set through the tools' door, which takes the tracker slots and the open skills only, with no size cap, and the loop runs the tool as a stateScoped write, exempt from answer shortening and a spent budget.
- **H2 What a reply changed** (`what-a-reply-changed`)
  A read route answers, for a take and a range of its live messages, what each message changed in the library trackers on the version it shows, with each tracker's name, style and icons, and bars: for each changed number that has a top, its bottom and the top it had when the message left it. A record's change is a field with its value before and after, and a list's is a row, added, changed or removed, with the whole row. Values are exact, never cut. It is one forward pass: the state before the first message is folded forward through each message's deltas, so a person's edit shows as a change on its message, another version shows its own changes, and a cut message drops out. Only an unreadable piece is reported by its id with the stored values, a changed layout keeps the name and style, past lines are read through the current piece, only tracker slots are loaded, a missing branch or message is a 404, a malformed to a 400, an empty to the tip.
- **H2 Editing** (`editing`)
  A tracker opens in the Workbench's tracker editor: the shared header, which says in how many scenes it is linked and lists them on a click, an Identity and settings disclosure (what it tracks, how the AI should update it, shape, icon, how a reply shows changes, and an optional prompt line in macros), and the fields in the field editor a tool shares. A number's To can be a number, another number field, or none. The rail shows the Trackers block as prompt assembly renders it at the starting values, its plain line, its token cost, and the tracker's update tool line. A rename is followed a keystroke at a time from the name it began with, in other fields' tops and in the prompt line's macros, so a name typed through on the way takes nothing; renaming the tracker rewrites its macros. A draft the decoder refuses, or a prompt line whose macros fail, is not saved and is explained in plain words. Linking is the Trackers panel's; replies show changes as set here.

## reference/extending

### Add a format adapter

- id: `reference/extending/add-a-format`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/add-a-format.md`
- Developer how-to for adding one format adapter under hub-and-spoke: every format converts through the canonical model, never format to format; one new adapter touches no other format and no core. A format is a folder under src/formats name; the loader scans the filesystem and skips underscore-prefixed folders such as _template and _shared; index.ts default-exports one adapter or an array for multi-codec families like SillyTavern. The contract in adapter.ts defines AdapterBase id label outputExtensions detect optional native generic coverage lens, then CharacterAdapter LorebookAdapter PersonaAdapter RegexAdapter with toCanonical fromCanonical and optional extractLorebook, discriminated as FormatAdapter on kind; AdapterInput and AdapterOutput are plain bytes text filename data not streams. Steps: copy _template so name is folder id and original escrow key; implement detect returning 0..1 with registry highest at or above 0.5 and reject sibling markers first; implement toCanonical with canonicalId and original raw or sourceMedia under the superset rule; implement fromCanonical clone-overlay twin or minimal object plus extractLorebook and context.lorebooks; declare coverage; done with loadFormats register and matrix regeneration. Declaring coverage explains CoverageDecl carries notes and path-prefix matching for honest platform lenses. Testing copies the Pygmalion suite shape. Non-negotiables ban core edits, demand the cross-kind firewall, escrow not blind-copy, and underscore only for inert folders. Source of truth tables the key core and template files.

- **H2 The contract** (`the-contract`)
  src/core/adapter.ts defines every format plugin. AdapterBase carries id as FormatId and escrow key, label, outputExtensions without dots, detect returning 0..1 confidence, optional native for bunzai storage never a publish target, optional generic for the plain family reader shown as Default, optional CoverageDecl, and optional lens false to hide from the editor platform strip. CharacterAdapter extends with kind character, toCanonical to CanonicalCharacter, fromCanonical with optional EmitContext, and optional extractLorebook. LorebookAdapter PersonaAdapter and RegexAdapter mirror the same shape for their kinds; FormatAdapter is the kind-discriminated union. AdapterInput is optional bytes text filename; AdapterOutput is optional bytes text plus suggestedExtension. Binary formats read bytes; text or JSON read text; fromCanonical returns what it writes plus an extension normally from its outputExtensions list.
- **H2 Steps** (`steps`)
  Six steps complete an adapter. Copy src/formats/_template to src/formats name so name is folder, adapter id, and original name escrow key; pick kind default character. Implement detect 0..1: registry keeps the single highest score at or above 0.5 so specifics must outrank generics; reject sibling known markers before positive signals as Pygmalion does; wrong shape returns 0 never throws. Implement toCanonical: map to canonical body, mint id with canonicalId, stash parsed source at original name raw and PNG pixels in sourceMedia; only first-class wire-serialized fields under the superset rule. Implement fromCanonical: clone escrow twin when present, overlay edited body, fall back to minimal object for cross-format; optional extractLorebook for non-shared embedded books; re-embed via context.lorebooks. Declare coverage in a later section. Done with no core edits: loadFormats globs index.ts files and registers exports; bun run bunzai formats lists it; bun run matrix and matrix:check keep FORMAT-SUPPORT.md honest.
- **H2 Declaring coverage** (`declaring-coverage`)
  src/core/coverage.ts makes per-platform editor lenses honest: the editor knows zero platforms, so each format folder declares which canonical body path prefixes its wire reads and re-emits, and every lens state is computed from that claim. CoverageDecl has carries string path prefixes and optional notes as per-path tooltip remarks. Path prefixes match on dot boundaries only so greetings covers greetings.firstMessage never greetingsX. Add coverage.ts default-exporting CoverageDecl and set coverage on the adapter; Pygmalion is the whole pattern with identity persona greetings examples media paths and honest notes. Absent coverage is not an error: the lens marks the tab not declared and dims nothing, deny-by-absence. In this codebase every non-native character adapter declares coverage; lorebook persona regex codecs and native vaud-json do not. Coverage also feeds export dialog honesty lines, so undeclared coverage understates that dialog for your format.
- **H2 Testing** (`testing`)
  Format tests live beside the adapter as src/formats name name.test.ts under bun:test. Copy the Pygmalion suite shape: shape guard accepts the real form and rejects every sibling; detect scores your format high and siblings zero on your fixture; toCanonical maps every field; same-format unedited round-trip is byte-identical; an edit reaches the wire; cross-format conversion to one other format keeps core text. bun run test covers formats; scope with bun test src/formats name. A lorebook-kind format additionally earns a round-trip fixture under samples/lorebooks name with real input plus expected canonical parse.
- **H2 Non-negotiables** (`non-negotiables`)
  No core edits: adding a format changes only its own folder; if src/core or another format folder seems necessary the design is not drop-in. The cross-kind firewall holds: a character detector must never claim lorebook bytes or vice versa because both share one registry. Escrow not blind copy: only the canonical body crosses formats; extensions scripts and internal ids stay under the source escrow key and never copy into another format file. The underscore prefix means inert only for shared helpers fixtures or the template, never to half-ship a real format.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  Authoritative files: _template/index.ts as starting copy; adapter.ts for contract kinds input output EmitContext; loader.ts for folders-as-schema discovery; registry.ts for detection 0.5 threshold and lookup; coverage.ts for CoverageDecl; canonical.ts for wrapper escrow and canonicalId; pygmalion index coverage and test as smallest worked example; format-matrix.ts for matrix and matrix:check; cli.ts for bunzai formats; architecture.md for whole-engine hub-and-spoke escrow and detection context.

### Generated extension API catalog

- id: `reference/extending/api`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/api.md`
- This generated reference inventories the extension operations currently described by source-owned descriptors. Each row gives the operation ID, human-facing name, effect class, source-declared hosts, budget class and confirmation policy. The table helps locate an API and compare its broad effect boundary, but it does not replace the strict input and output contracts in the adjacent api.json data artifact. The generator derives both views from operation definitions so documentation follows the catalog rather than a second hand-maintained list. A descriptor's host list means that a concrete adapter is declared for that host; it is not proof that every required user flow is complete. The fixed required-family test remains the acceptance authority and may still fail for host combinations the plan requires. Package authors should pair this inventory with the SDK reference and host-aware package check, and users should inspect the operation's exact resource, field, destination and review requirements before granting access.


### Generated extension coverage

- id: `reference/extending/coverage`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/coverage.md`
- This generated coverage report combines discovered semantic capabilities, strict content field groups, source-declared operation availability by host, and per-host catalog totals. It distinguishes capabilities that exist in the application from those explicitly reviewed for extension exposure and backed by a strict operation adapter. Field groups do not imply access without a matching grant. Host rows describe generated descriptors and their adapter claims, not security certification or packaged release behavior. The fixed required-family test compares expected plan IDs and host combinations with the catalog and passes for this source snapshot. Terminal scene-composer draft control is excluded because that host has no scene-bound composer owner. Read the coverage with the normative matrix and host integration evidence; passing the matrix alone does not prove every release boundary.

- **H2 Semantic capability inventory** (`semantic-capability-inventory`)
  This section compares the capability modules discovered in the repository with the subset that has explicit extension exposure metadata and a corresponding strict operation descriptor. Discovery tells the application which semantic capabilities exist; it does not authorize an extension to invoke them. Exposure metadata is owned alongside the capability semantics and identifies the reviewed operation, fields, resources, confirmation, budget and supported hosts. The adapter count then reports whether a generated descriptor currently exists for that exposed operation. This relationship helps reveal capabilities that are still unreviewed or lack an operation adapter, while avoiding the assumption that every internal editor or Kit action should become an extension API. Counts are generated from the current source scan and catalog and should be checked alongside the fixed required-family acceptance test.
- **H2 Strict content field groups** (`strict-content-field-groups`)
  This section lists the reviewed field groups available to scoped content read operations. Each group names a bounded projection rather than exposing an entire mutable stored entity. A package requests exact fields and resources, the grant matcher narrows those values, and the operation projects only the approved subset. Authored body access and binary media use separate operations and grants, keeping large payloads and sensitive authored content distinct from ordinary metadata. The inventory is generated from the field schemas, so changes to a group should be made in its source owner and then reflected by the catalog generator. A listed group is an available contract, not an implicit grant or a promise that every content kind has every field.
- **H2 Host operation inventory** (`host-operation-inventory`)
  The host operation inventory enumerates generated operation IDs and the hosts listed by each source descriptor. It lets readers compare desktop, browser and terminal catalog contents and see operations restricted to one environment, such as graphical actions or native file flows. These are source claims backed by host composition and tests. The independent required-family fixture compares against fixed plan rows; it currently passes for this snapshot. Terminal scene-composer draft control remains excluded because there is no scene-bound Terminal composer owner, while nonvisual turn operations are included. The report is not a packaged-host or release certification.
- **H2 Host totals** (`host-totals`)
  The totals count how many generated descriptors currently include each host in their source metadata. A single operation is counted once per host, so totals across hosts may exceed the operation catalog size. These figures help track the breadth of declared support but do not measure user-flow completeness, test coverage, deployment behavior or security approval. The required-family test checks a different question: whether every plan-required operation is available on each expected host and no unexpected host is claimed. Host-specific integration tests supply evidence that a descriptor's service can actually work at its public boundary. Read the count only as an inventory snapshot, and refresh it from the generator whenever operation sources or supported host composition changes.

### Exercise the native file reader

- id: `reference/extending/file-reader-test`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/file-reader-test.md`
- This guide exercises the extension file reader through a temporary artifact and the isolated desktop Studio fixture. It creates a unique directory and sample text file, packs the checked-in file-reader example, then installs it while granting only file picking and reading. The user selects the exact temporary path through the host action card and reviews the name and read limit before continuing. The result is checked for its bounded text preview, byte count, and digest. The guide states that the WebView packaging boundary has not been verified, avoiding a broader release claim.

- **H2 Build a temporary artifact** (`build-a-temporary-artifact`)
  The PowerShell setup creates a uniquely named temporary directory, writes a two-line sample, and packs the checked-in file-reader example to a file inside that same directory. The user retains both paths in variables for the following host exercise. Because the directory name includes a fresh GUID, these commands avoid clobbering an existing repository artifact or fixture. The guide does not remove the directory, so the selected sample and package remain available for inspection after the run.
- **H2 Exercise the production host path** (`exercise-the-production-host-path`)
  The user opens an isolated desktop fixture, reviews the package in Settings, approves only `files.pick` and `files.read`, and enables it. Running the example presents a host-owned file action card; the user chooses the temporary sample and explicitly reviews its filename and full-file limit before proceeding. The host-rendered result should include the name, byte count, SHA-256 digest, and first 2,000 text characters. Revoking the package should remove its command and result. File data moves as bounded chunks through the authenticated local API, not to a network destination.
- **H2 Limits** (`limits`)
  The selected file is bounded to 65,536 bytes for this example. File-action tickets expire, and the chosen handle is scoped to one invocation, package digest, grant revision, and runtime epoch. The document distinguishes the production QuickJS worker path from packaged deployment evidence: the current implementation has not been verified inside a packaged WebView runtime. A successful isolated desktop run therefore demonstrates the checked host path and its limit, not universal packaging support or a stronger filesystem guarantee.

### Make a theme

- id: `reference/extending/make-a-theme`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/make-a-theme.md`
- A theme is a folder of data and, optionally, one checked stylesheet: a schemaVersion 2 manifest that picks one host-built variant for any of 29 surfaces and sets a lowercase-hex palette held to contrast floors, bundled fonts by id, frame, density and motion, and accent behavior, with optional inert SVG marks. Beyond the descriptor it may add one checked stylesheet, woff2 fonts, PNG and WebP images and static slot content, each behind its own scope; it carries no code and no rule may name the protected layer, which a watchdog guards. Permissions are derived from the fields used and bound to the package digest, any edit needs fresh approval, and the protected layer is never themeable. A copy-paste starter validates in a test. Five themes ship: four identity themes held to a 50 percent surface-difference floor, and RoleCall, the default, which is colour-only on purpose (RoleCall palette, Cinzel and Inter, no surface picks) and exempt from that floor by name. Built-in themes get no power a package lacks: scan:theme-keys rejects host CSS or TypeScript keyed on a theme id. The manual theme:measure gate scores structural difference with recolour and font-only controls and lists the protected gate screens without scoring them.

- **H2 What a package is** (`what-a-package-is`)
  A package is a folder with manifest.json, an optional license, optional images (SVG, PNG, WebP), optional woff2 fonts and an optional styles.css, imported through Settings as one JSON envelope with binary files as base64. The Encore example can be downloaded in that shape, and bundled themes are discovered from folders under src/extensions/themes.
- **H2 The manifest** (`the-manifest`)
  Every manifest field is required except styles, fonts and slots, and any unknown key at any depth is an error. The top level carries the package id, version, display name, requested scopes and the image list, plus the optional styles file, fonts and slots. The appearance object holds the surface picks, the palette, the type choices, the shape settings, the accent behavior and the optional marks naming image ids. Ids follow the bunzai.theme namespace, versions are semantic, names are bounded, and at most twenty-four images may be listed: SVG of ninety-six kilobytes or less, PNG and WebP of one hundred twenty-eight kilobytes or less. The sub-sections below define surfaces, palette, and type, shape and accent in detail, with the exact allowed values.
  - **H3 Surfaces and variants** (`surfaces-and-variants`)
    Twenty-nine surface ids each list four host-built variants in Theater, Encore, GirliePop, Paper order; a theme names variants for any subset, an unnamed surface or unbuilt variant renders as the current core rendering, every variant keeps all controls and data, and one registry module holds the ids. app.room arranges the setup wizard, the Press, the Agent room and the CSS Workshop, and the newest, scene.reasoning, restyles the two thinking blocks above a reply.
  - **H3 Palette** (`palette`)
    The palette is lowercase six-digit hex in surface, line, text and role groups, one jewel hue per meaning with fill, text and deep tones. Inspection rejects any palette missing the contrast floors: text 7:1, other text 4.5:1, control boundary and focus 3:1, with on-tint and on-deep ink checks, and names the failing pairs.
  - **H3 Type, shape and accent** (`type-shape-and-accent`)
    Fonts come from the bundled ids, including Inter for body text, with a scale step that never drops body text below 16px. Shape sets frame style, width 1 to 3, density and motion; there is no radius field and reduced motion wins. Accent follows the user by mapping the Settings accent onto brand role and its neighbour hues onto action and select, or stays locked.
- **H2 Permissions** (`permissions`)
  Requested scopes must equal the derived set: palette, type, shape, surfaces and accent, plus assets, styles, fonts and slots when shipped. A package gets no library, credential, network or execution power. Approval is bound to the digest of every file, an edit makes the package inactive until reviewed, and an update that adds a scope waits for approval while the old one keeps serving. Disable and revoke return the core appearance, and the lock, recovery, Kit Gate and manager stay host-owned.
- **H2 Copy-paste starter** (`copy-paste-starter`)
  The guide embeds a complete manifest derived from the Encore example, minus its SVG marks, so it needs no asset files. A test parses the fenced JSON block from the page and inspects it as a package, which keeps the starter honest as the contract changes. Authors copy it, change the id, name and values, keep the requested scopes equal to the scopes the manifest derives, and import the result through Settings. It shows Encore's picks; the difference test prints all four themes' picks.
- **H2 Going further** (`going-further`)
  Four optional fields take a theme past picks, each through the same install, approval, update and revoke path and each adding one scope the installer shows. Stable data-part names let a stylesheet aim at surfaces and key parts, styles ships one checked stylesheet, fonts adds woff2 faces, images widen to PNG and WebP, and slots place static text and images in named places, now including each scene wing and its tabs. Encore uses all of them.
  - **H3 Parts: stable names to aim at** (`parts-stable-names-to-aim-at`)
    Every surface root and key part carries a data-part name of the form surface.part. The names are the contract while tags and host classes may change, and the full list is generated from component sources in docs/generated/theme-parts.md. A part name is a dotted lowercase literal; families such as props.tracker.<id> are keyed on a stable id, never a label, and the scene's wing panels share scene.wings.panel and carry the wing in data-wing. scan:parts fails an unnamed wing, tab or pop-out and any undotted or label-keyed name. A plain part selector has no specificity, so it loses to host class chains until raised.
  - **H3 Styles: one stylesheet, any selector** (`styles-one-stylesheet-any-selector`)
    A styles.css of at most 64 KB can hide, move, restyle and animate anything outside the protected layer. It is tokenized and checked whole: no @import or @layer, no url() except asset ids, no foreign font-face, no nested rules, attr(), image-set, host keyframe names, --host properties or protected-layer names. Rules load last and are scoped off the protected layer, the protected root keeps its tokens in a higher layer, and a watchdog drops styles and slots and revokes the theme as broken if a protected prompt is covered. The layer is hardened, not isolated. A font-face may not redefine any family Bunzai ships, including Lexend, OpenDyslexic, Crimson Pro, Playfair Display and JetBrains Mono.
  - **H3 Fonts** (`fonts`)
    Up to four woff2 files of 128 KB each, declared with @font-face using url(asset:id). A listed font the stylesheet never uses is refused, and the font license belongs in LICENSE.txt.
  - **H3 Images** (`images`)
    Assets accept SVG, PNG and WebP, referenced from CSS as url(asset:id), from slot items by id, or from marks. Bunzai serves each file from its own digest URL.
  - **H3 Slots: content in named places** (`slots-content-in-named-places`)
    Slots hold one to four static items each, 24 per package: an image of a package asset, or text of at most 280 characters drawn as text with an optional class that starts with mod- and is defined in the package styles as a sole-class rule. A table lists the slots, the eleven page ones and scene.wing.<wing> and scene.wing.<wing>.<tab> for each wing and tab, and where each is drawn; wrappers are named slot.<id> for styling.
  - **H3 What the installer shows** (`what-the-installer-shows`)
    A package using every power asks for styles, fonts, assets and slots scopes on top of the descriptor five, bound to the digest of every file. An update that adds a scope waits for approval, and revoke removes styles and slots at once.
- **H2 Checking your work** (`checking-your-work`)
  Authors verify a theme in three steps. First import the envelope in Settings, Appearance and read the review, where an appearance error names the failing field or the contrast pair that missed its floor, while a bad envelope, id or asset path reports only that the package is invalid. Second compare the surface picks against the matrix printed by the theme difference test, which also reports the share of surfaces where the theme differs from the others. Third open every screen in the theme, remembering that a variant the host has not built yet shows the current rendering until its component lands, so some screens will not change. The protected control list also includes the Dramaturg model and connection.

### Add a content kind

- id: `reference/extending/new-kind`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/new-kind.md`
- Add a content kind explains that a kind is one folder: src/entities/<kind>/kind.ts describes it once (id, labels, deck color and glyph, Library deck or not, the blank piece New saves, the inspect lines, and when they apply its links, name path, picture paths, heavy flag, libraryNew switch, scene field, editor, and for a family kind its deck and asPreset), and bun run kinds writes two checked seams, src/generated/kinds.ts for identity (KINDS, KIND_IDS, kindOf, isKindId, AnyKindEntity) and src/generated/kind-schemas.ts for every kind's whole-entity decoder and storage parts, both verified by kinds:check in verify:ci. A table names everything that reads the seam so no hand edits are needed. The steps make the folder's schema, runtime schema and kind.ts, optional parts.ts and capabilities, run bun run kinds, name its editor in kind.ts and add the component to workbench/editors.ts under that id (the completeness test fails on either side missing), write the reference page and scale fixture, and run the kind completeness test, which names every missing part or a stated exemption. Behavior that belongs to one kind stays with the code that owns it; scene fields keep their own shapes in the scene store, and the kind-lists guard test fails on a new hand-written kind list.

- **H2 What reads the seam (so you edit none of it)** (`what-reads-the-seam-so-you-edit-none-of-it`)
  What reads the seam: the studio folder set STUDIO_ENTITY_KINDS, the parse boundary, storage item parts, the generic KindAdapter contracts, deck labels, colors, glyphs and Library decks in decks.ts, Panel, editor head and Press deck colors via deckMeta, search kind words, import receipts, Kit deck counts and name lookup, createBlank and canCreateBlank for every New door, core/links.ts for every link follower (Press riders, export embedding, import renames, Kit delete warnings, node ports, archive caveats), core/piece-name.ts for names, media-reference.ts and ART_KINDS for pictures, the heavy flag in the Press and Library rename, bunzai inspect, first-run deck titles, and the format matrix and field docs scripts.
- **H2 Steps** (`steps`)
  Steps: make the folder with schema.ts and runtime-schema.ts exporting entitySchema built from entityShape; write kind.ts with defineKind, an existing deck token and glyph; optionally parts.ts and capabilities; run bun run kinds; name the kind's editor in kind.ts (id, label and load) and add the component to workbench/editors.ts under that id, with the completeness test failing on either side missing; write the reference page and the scale fixture; run the kind completeness test, which lists each missing part with the file to create, or a reason in its exemption lists. Behavior unique to one kind stays in its owning code; a scene's fields keep their shapes in the scene store, sceneField only declares that a scene can point at the kind, and promptingList names the prompting list that links it. The kind-lists guard test lists every file with a line naming two or more kinds, with its count and reason.

### Extension developer quickstart

- id: `reference/extending/quickstart`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/quickstart.md`
- This quickstart walks a developer from a checked-in extension example to a deterministic package artifact, a narrow permission review, and a host-rendered result. It uses a fresh temporary folder, validates the package for one host, and compares two pack outputs. The example asks for optional library metadata access and a required panel operation, showing how denial differs from install failure. The page explains the typed SDK bridge, preview/apply behavior, update and revoke semantics, host availability, and troubleshooting. Desktop and Browser command menus pass staged or active refs separately from guest input, with a 16-ref limit; Terminal has no current-piece selector, so commands must use explicit resource selectors supported by the operation. Three more examples cover draft review, scene events, and prompt contributions.

- **H2 1. Copy a working example into a clean folder** (`1-copy-a-working-example-into-a-clean-folder`)
  The exercise starts from the checked-in character-audit example and copies its manifest and entry into a uniquely named temporary directory. It runs the host-aware package check, packs the same source twice, and compares artifact hashes to confirm deterministic output. Both commands validate and bundle without evaluating the entry file. The entry has a bounded UTF-8 size, and import rules restrict dependencies to package-local paths or the generated SDK. The temporary directory is deliberately unique so the workflow does not overwrite an existing fixture or repository file.
- **H2 2. Review one optional permission and install** (`2-review-one-optional-permission-and-install`)
  The install walkthrough asks the developer to review the package identity and each operation request in Settings. `ui.panel.set` is needed to show the command result, while `content.list` is an optional library-wide metadata grant for character IDs, kinds, and names. The example does not request bodies, prompts, descriptions, or media. The first run denies the optional grant and demonstrates an actionable denial in the panel. A second permission review grants the scope, after which the same command lists bounded character names without changing library content.
- **H2 3. How the command and panel use the SDK** (`3-how-the-command-and-panel-use-the-sdk`)
  The SDK example imports `bunzai` from the generated package and defines a command handler with `bunzai.extension.define`. Its typed operation methods are wrappers over one broker bridge, rather than direct host callbacks. The command requests only the declared character metadata fields, then sends a value-only panel descriptor to the host. The manifest declares both operation permissions and the command and panel contribution IDs. The host validates the declared panel and draws it; extension code cannot return HTML, handler source, or permission controls. The broker checks grants at invocation time.
- **H2 4. Update, disable, and revoke** (`4-update-disable-and-revoke`)
  Updates require a new package review when the artifact bytes change. The walkthrough recommends writing the updated package to a new file, checking its identity and digest, and reapproving only operation requests that remain acceptable. Disabling stops activation while preserving the installed package. Revoking removes grants and stops package-owned panels and actions. Removing the extension and its data also deletes its private settings and activity records, but it does not delete library content. These distinctions help developers document expected recovery behavior without implying that an old approval transfers to new bytes.
- **H2 Preview and apply for content edits** (`preview-and-apply-for-content-edits`)
  Content mutation is staged as a preview or draft before persistence. The extension inspects the host's exact before-and-after review, then calls the corresponding apply operation with the expected revision and preview fingerprint. A preview alone does not save a change. Resource scope remains explicit: a grant for selected IDs covers those targets, while a library-wide grant is broader and needs deliberate review. The workflow separates proposed content from committed content and makes stale revisions visible instead of silently merging concurrent edits.
- **H2 Host availability** (`host-availability`)
  The generated catalog and host-aware check report which operations have concrete adapters on desktop, browser, and terminal. SDK types describe the source catalog, but an installed host determines whether a particular operation is available. Native file selection, network requests, UI gestures, and provider connections have host-specific boundaries. Desktop and Browser command menus attach staged or active refs as separate host context; Terminal has no current-piece selector or `--on` flag, so commands use explicit resource selectors supported by each operation. Naming an operation in a manifest does not make an unsupported host implementation appear. Developers should run `extensions:check` against the actual target host and keep example claims aligned with the adapters that have been exercised.
- **H2 Troubleshooting** (`troubleshooting`)
  The troubleshooting list maps common failures to specific checks: confirm operation IDs in the generated reference, validate the manifest and declared file paths, enable an installed package, and match contribution IDs to defined handlers. For access denial, inspect the exact operation, resources, and fields before granting anything. If an update stops working, review its new bytes and digest. Host-specific failures should be checked on that host rather than inferred from another composition. The guide links to generated API and SDK references for strict schemas and error codes.
- **H2 More checked workflows** (`more-checked-workflows`)
  Three checked examples extend the basic audit workflow. The draft-writer example stages a two-field character change and applies the reviewed fingerprint under an exact resource grant. The event-panel example subscribes to the safe `scene.opened` summary and displays it through the bounded panel API. The prompt-contributor example adds a manifest-declared candidate to one scene's system prompt stage during host preview. Each source can be copied to a temporary directory, checked for its target host, and packed. Manifest requirements describe requested scope but do not silently grant a library-wide resource selector.

### Generated guest SDK

- id: `reference/extending/sdk`
- path: `docs/reference/extending/sdk.md`
- This generated reference maps SDK convenience methods to canonical operation IDs and schema links. Each method uses the same `bunzai.call` bridge, and runtime schemas validate requests and responses at the broker. The SDK also exposes bounded host invocation refs through `bunzai.context.invocation.resources`; these refs are the union of host-selected resources covered by this package's approved grants, separate from guest input and not authority. The broker rechecks each operation against its exact grant before exposing narrowed access. Desktop and Browser menus attach staged or active pieces; Terminal has no current-piece selector and uses explicit operation selectors. Generated output should be refreshed from source rather than edited by hand.

- **H2 Host invocation context** (`host-invocation-context`)
  This section documents the typed `bunzai.context.invocation.resources` property. It is a read-only array of at most 16 host-selected resource refs covered by the package's approved grants, passed separately from command input. It is not filtered for one operation: the broker rechecks and narrows the resources against each exact operation grant before access. Desktop and Browser command menus attach Library staged refs or the Workbench active piece. Terminal has no current-piece or `--on` selector; handlers use explicit operation selectors where available. This distinction keeps examples honest about host support and authority.

## reference/formats

### Agnai format

- id: `reference/formats/agnai`
- path: `docs/reference/formats/agnai.md`
- Developer reference for the Agnai Agnaistic adapter family: native JSON character shape and memory-book lorebooks, not Tavern-lineage. Persona is structured kind plus attribute map, preserved in canonical persona.structured. Folder src/formats/agnai default-exports agnai character and agnai-lorebook codecs, both .json; schema facts come from Agnai source under AGPL as interop only with no code copied. Detection covers character score 1.0 when kind character persona object greeting string and schemaVersion body both absent to firewall vaud-json; lorebook scores 1.0 kind memory, 0.9 kind-less native entry keywords array, 0.7 standalone CCv3 character_book soft import, else 0. Field map covers character and lorebook canonical mappings. Escrow retains the parsed source card and unknown keys as a twin; export overlays canonical fields and regenerates JSON, so fidelity is semantic/field-level rather than byte-identical. The character persona is always rebuilt, and multi-element text attributes collapse to the first element. Lorebook entries overlay by id and preserve parsed fields and raw-only residue, while JSON formatting may normalize. Embedded characterBook uses Agnai MemoryBook mapping, not the CCv3 mapper. Quirks include overlay behavior, firewall, imageSettings per-key merge, single insert, embedded book handling, and selectiveLogic ST residue.

- **H2 Detection** (`detection`)
  Registry keeps the single highest detect score above 0.5 so specific formats outrank generic ones. Character detect parses JSON and returns 1.0 only when kind is character, persona is a non-null object, greeting is a string, schemaVersion is undefined, and body is undefined; otherwise 0. The last two clauses firewall vaud-json which carries schemaVersion and body so Agnai never fights the native wrapper on the same file. Parse failure or missing text returns 0. Agnai scores 1.0 not 0.9 as a self-identifying shape no other adapter supersets. Lorebook agnai-lorebook uses coerceMemoryBook: 1.0 for kind memory, 0.9 for kind-less books whose entries array first entry still has native entry plus keywords, 0.7 for standalone CCv3 Chub character_book with keys or content re-exported as MemoryBook, else 0 including SillyTavern keyed-object worldbooks and Risu envelopes without top-level entries array.
  - **H3 Character** (`character`)
    detect requires kind character, non-null persona object, string greeting, and both schemaVersion and body undefined. Those absences block vaud-json collision. Failure or missing text yields 0; success is 1.0 as a specific self-identifying shape.
  - **H3 Lorebook** (`lorebook`)
    coerceMemoryBook scoring: 1.0 with kind memory; 0.9 kind-less array entries whose first row has entry and keywords distinguishing native Agnai from CCv3 content keys; 0.7 standalone character_book soft import via shared mapper; 0 for ST object entries and Risu envelopes.
- **H2 Field map** (`field-map`)
  Decode uses cardToBody and bookToCanonical; encode uses applyBodyToCard and canonicalToMemoryBook. Only string values survive character decode via tolerant str and strList; only defined values write back. Character maps name description characterVersion culture persona scenario appearance voice imageSettings affixes system postHistory prefill insert greeting alternates sampleChat avatar sprite visualType creator tags json and whole card to original.agnai.raw. Persona kind text lands personality plus structured text; boostyle wpp sbf attributes keep structured attributes with personality unset; encode rebuilds from structured unless text. Lorebook maps MemoryEntry entry keywords weight priority distinct from CCv3 content keys, book-level scanDepth tokenBudget recursiveScanning, lorebookType hardcoded other, and documents weight to sortOrder priority to priority matching Agnai memoryEntryToNative interop comments.
  - **H3 Character** (`character-1`)
