  How Kit should apply it requires distinguishing three claim kinds. Upstream behavior describes what SillyTavern does with a field or artifact. Bunzai format behavior describes what the current adapter can preserve or emit. Authoring advice explains how fields interact but does not prove every target format can carry them. For interoperability decisions, read this collection with the SillyTavern format page; if they differ, the format page governs current Bunzai implementation and this collection governs upstream meaning. Kit must never invent adapter support from an upstream feature description alone.
- **H2 Refresh policy** (`refresh-policy`)
  Refresh policy states SillyTavern evolves quickly. A future refresh should compare the pinned upstream revision, update only the affected Bunzai pages, regenerate their semantic sidecars, and obtain a new independent review. New upstream features should join this collection only when they change a content type, authoring workflow, prompt contract, or interoperability decision. Routine UI rearrangements, installation changes, provider lists, and transient defaults stay outside the collection. When exact current UI labels matter, follow the linked official page rather than treating this corpus as a live UI screenshot.

### SillyTavern regex and automation

- id: `reference/platforms/sillytavern/regex-and-automation`
- path: `docs/reference/platforms/sillytavern/regex-and-automation.md`
- Defines SillyTavern regex scripts and STscript as authored content with executable effects inside SillyTavern while Bunzai only inspects, edits, and round-trips their text as data. Regex script records cover display name, find expression, replacement with complete match and capture groups, trim strings, enabled state, source scopes, macro-substitution mode, depth bounds, run-on-edit, ephemerality, JavaScript-style flags, global versus character scope, export JSON portability, Affects selectors for user input AI response slash values World Info and reasoning, and empty-selector plus depth constraints. Ephemerality and visible truth separates persistent chat JSONL rewrites from display-only and prompt-only modes, creating three truths of stored, rendered, and model-bound messages that both ephemerality flags must preserve, plus find-expression macro modes disabled raw or regex-escaped and the requirement for bounded terminable testing. STscript and Quick Replies describes pipe-batched slash commands, variables, conditions, generation, World Info mutation, QR presets and qrset, properties, a.b cross-preset calls, management commands, auto-execute triggers including World Info Automation ID, and installation-dependent extension commands. Bunzai safety and interoperability seals imported payloads, forbids execution during import or conversion, limits regex testing to the isolated user-invoked terminable worker, escrows same-format script fields, requires explicit semantic mapping for cross-format movement, and routes execution-condition edits through Gate review.

- **H2 Regex script records** (`regex-script-records`)
  Regex script records include a display name, find expression, replacement, optional trim strings, enabled state, source scopes, macro-substitution mode, depth bounds, run-on-edit behavior, and ephemerality settings. Replacement supports the complete match and capture groups. JavaScript-style flags control global, case-insensitive, dot-all, multiline, and Unicode behavior. Global scripts live in installation settings; scoped scripts live in the active character card. Exported JSON can move a script between installations, and scripts can move between global and character scope. Name is operational because slash commands and STscript may target it. Affects selectors include user input, AI response, slash-command values, World Info content, and reasoning. An empty selector set prevents normal chat activation but may still allow an explicit script call. Min and max depth constrain eligible chat messages and do not apply to utility or system prompts in the same way.
- **H2 Ephemerality and visible truth** (`ephemerality-and-visible-truth`)
  Ephemerality and visible truth states that with neither ephemerality option selected, a regex script can rewrite values stored in the chat JSONL, a persistent and possibly irreversible mutation. Display-only changes alter what the user sees without changing the outgoing prompt. Prompt-only changes alter what the model receives without changing display. Selecting both changes display and prompt while leaving stored chat text untouched. This creates three distinct truths: stored message, rendered message, and model-bound message. An editor must preserve both ephemerality flags rather than reducing them to one enabled switch. World Info scope also depends on outgoing-prompt behavior. Macro substitution in the find expression may be disabled, raw, or regex-escaped; raw values can change pattern meaning when names contain metacharacters, while escaped substitution treats the value literally. Testing must use bounded, terminable execution because user-authored patterns can be computationally expensive.
- **H2 STscript and Quick Replies** (`stscript-and-quick-replies`)
  STscript and Quick Replies describes STscript as pipe-separated slash command batches executed sequentially with pipe-passed values. The language includes named and unnamed arguments, macros, local and global variables, arrays and objects, conditions, closures, loops, math, generation calls, prompt injections, message access, character navigation, World Info mutation, text processing, and extension commands. Quick Replies store scripts behind buttons or hidden procedures; presets group replies and switch via qrset by name. From the pinned STscript reference: autocomplete on chat input and the large QR editor, slash-colon listing scoped variables and QRs, QR properties label message/script tool tip and debugger with breakpoint, cross-preset procedure calls as a.b with literal labels winning first, management commands including qr-create qr-delete qr-update qr-get and preset and context-menu helpers, and auto-execute at app startup user message AI message chat load group reply or matching World Info Automation ID. Extensions add installation-dependent commands. Scripts can call a model, modify messages and lore, change variables, navigate UI, or invoke extensions and are not static macro templates. Official docs warn users to inspect scripts before execution.
- **H2 Bunzai safety and interoperability** (`bunzai-safety-and-interoperability`)
  Bunzai safety and interoperability requires that imported regex, macro, Lua, and STscript payloads remain sealed text. Bunzai does not execute STscript, resolve Quick Reply procedures, follow Automation IDs, or apply a regex during import or conversion. Regex testing is available only through Bunzai's isolated, user-invoked test bench and its terminable worker. Same-format export can restore platform-native script fields from escrow. Cross-format movement is allowed only when a target adapter has an explicit semantic mapping; a target that merely has scripts does not prove compatible scope, lifecycle, persistence, or command semantics. Kit should describe executable consequences before proposing an edit. It must not imply that a disabled list switch, an internal disabled field, an empty Affects set, and ephemerality are interchangeable. Changes to any execution condition belong in the semantic Gate review even though the payload remains sealed.

### SillyTavern authoring source coverage

- id: `reference/platforms/sillytavern/source-coverage`
- path: `docs/reference/platforms/sillytavern/source-coverage.md`
- Auditable path-by-path coverage map for all 92 Markdown files at SillyTavern-Docs commit 70e5e4d3c239253fca4692fe82e3936cb9c4b1b1, retrieved 2026-07-26 under AGPL-3.0. Twenty-one included authoring sources route character cards, tags, chat interchange, groups, personas, Data Bank, World Info, prompt assembly, CFG, reasoning, tokenizers, macros, regex, STscript, Quick Replies, and expression images to owning Bunzai references. Seventy-one individually named exclusions cover provider setup, installation, administration, ordinary navigation and settings, runtime-only extensions, contributor workflows, and repository metadata with a reason per path. Dynamic installation authorities remain live macro help, field autocomplete, extension slash commands, and expression classifiers; Bunzai never executes imported automation to discover them. Refresh requires pin comparison, destination updates, macro coverage tests, fresh semantic summaries, and independent review.

- **H2 Pinned source** (`pinned-source`)
  Pinned source identifies the exact upstream documentation snapshot: SillyTavern/SillyTavern-Docs at commit 70e5e4d3c239253fca4692fe82e3936cb9c4b1b1, retrieved 2026-07-26, licensed AGPL-3.0. Paths in the coverage tables are repository-relative to that tree so audits can open the same files the Bunzai authoring corpus claimed to evaluate. The pin is the authority for inclusion and exclusion decisions on this page; later docs commits require an explicit refresh rather than silent drift. License and commit together make provenance reproducible for Kit and human maintainers.
- **H2 Included authoring pages** (`included-authoring-pages`)
  Included authoring pages table maps Characters index design Author Note tags chatfilemanagement groupchats personas data-bank worldinfo Prompts index advancedformatting context-template instructmode prompt-manager CFG reasoning tokenizer macros Regex STscript and Expression-Images to Characters Chats Personas World Info Prompts Macros Regex destinations. Tokenizer selection and padding routes to Prompts and presets. The claim that every markdown file is classified depends on tokenizer remaining in this include table or a defensible exclusion which this corpus does not use. Preserve these rules in Kit answers without inventing adapter support.
- **H2 Evaluated exclusions (path-level)** (`evaluated-exclusions-path-level`)
  Evaluated exclusions classifies 71 pinned files one row at a time with no directory wildcards. The table names chat navigation and branch UX, every provider-connection page, ordinary settings and onboarding, each installation and migration guide, every administration page, runtime-only audio image retrieval translation avatar and utility extensions, contributor workflows, and repository metadata. Each row states why it does not define a selected portable authoring contract. Together with the 21 inclusion rows, the manifest accounts for all 92 Markdown files. Macro names and operators remain governed by the stronger exhaustive catalogs rather than inferred from this coverage table.
- **H2 Dynamic boundaries** (`dynamic-boundaries`)
  Dynamic boundaries states that live slash macros queries, field autocomplete, extension slash commands, and installed expression classifiers remain installation authorities outside the pinned markdown inventory. Bunzai never executes imported automation, STscript, or sealed scripts merely to discover what an installation can do. Capability claims must not be reverse-engineered by running untrusted content; they come from static documentation, adapters, and user-invoked sandboxed benches only.
- **H2 Refresh** (`refresh`)
  Refresh requires comparing the next SillyTavern-Docs commit to the pinned hash, updating inclusion and exclusion destinations, re-running macro coverage tests, rewriting affected semantic summaries, and obtaining independent review after those summary rewrites. Do not advance the pin without reconciling every path-level verdict and regenerating author-valid sidecars for changed pages.

### SillyTavern World Info authoring

- id: `reference/platforms/sillytavern/world-info`
- path: `docs/reference/platforms/sillytavern/world-info.md`
- Models SillyTavern World Info as conditional knowledge and prompt injection rather than a passive dictionary. Books, links, and entry identity distinguishes global, character, chat, embedded, and named-link scopes, organizational memos, content, enabled state, character filters, generation filters, and extra scan sources. Activation and selection covers constants, primary and secondary keywords, selective logic, probability, inclusion groups, group scoring, and vector similarity with remaining eligibility checks. Placement, order, and prompt budget separates insertion position, chat depth, order, outlets, scan depth, minimum activations, and context budget. Recursion, timing, and automation covers recursive activation controls, sticky, cooldown, delay, branch state, and executable Automation IDs. Interoperability rules distinguishes portable character books from standalone World Info dialects, requires canonical mapping plus original escrow, and demands explicit loss reporting when another platform lacks recursion, grouping, timing, vectors, outlets, trigger filters, or scan-source controls.

- **H2 Books, links, and entry identity** (`books-links-and-entry-identity`)
  Books, links, and entry identity distinguishes availability from activation. Books may be global, character-linked, chat-linked, embedded in a card, or referenced by an installed name. Entries retain a memo for humans, model-bound content, enabled state, and ordering metadata; memo and keys are not stable identity substitutes. Character or tag filters narrow eligible speakers, generation filters distinguish Normal, Continue, Impersonate, Swipe, Regenerate, and Quiet, and optional scan sources extend matching into character, persona, and creator fields.
- **H2 Activation and selection** (`activation-and-selection`)
  Activation and selection covers constant entries, primary keys, secondary keys, selective any, all, not-any, or not-all logic, and global or per-entry case and whole-word behavior. Probability filters an otherwise qualified entry. Inclusion groups then resolve competing entries using weight, deterministic order priority, or matched-key group scoring. Vector similarity can replace keyword qualification but does not bypass probability, filters, groups, or token budget; retained keywords still permit ordinary activation, and embedding retrieval is less deterministic than explicit keys.
- **H2 Placement, order, and prompt budget** (`placement-order-and-prompt-budget`)
  Placement, order, and prompt budget keeps insertion position, chat depth, and sort order as independent axes. Entries may land around definitions, examples, Author's Note, or a role-tagged chat depth. Outlet entries wait for an exact case-sensitive `{{outlet::Name}}` in a compatible later-processed prompt field; outlets cannot nest or resolve in early card fields, World Info content, or Author's Note. Context percentage or token budget limits combined entries, scan depth defines recent key input, and Min Activations may search farther within Max Depth.
- **H2 Recursion, timing, and automation** (`recursion-timing-and-automation`)
  Recursion, timing, and automation explains entry chains activated by keywords inside other entry content. Per-entry controls may block recursive activation, stop further propagation, or delay eligibility to a recursion level, while Max Recursion Steps bounds passes and conflicts with Min Activations. Sticky, cooldown, and delay create message-counted chat-local state that branches inherit and edits clear. Automation IDs can execute matched Quick Reply or STscript behavior, so Bunzai treats those bindings as sealed executable metadata and never follows them during import.
- **H2 Interoperability rules** (`interoperability-rules`)
  Interoperability rules distinguishes the portable character-book schema from SillyTavern's richer standalone World Info wire dialect, including extension-carried settings. Bunzai maps represented behavior into canonical lorebook entries and retains the complete source for same-format restoration. A cross-format target may lack recursion, inclusion groups, timed effects, vectors, outlets, generation filters, or alternative scan sources. Conversion must report each unsupported behavior rather than flattening it silently, with the format adapter reference governing the exact supported field and enum map.

## reference/security

### Safe archive handling: .charx and .byaf

- id: `reference/security/archives`
- path: `docs/reference/security/archives.md`
- Safe archive handling covers how Bunzai bounds .charx and .byaf ZIP bytes as untrusted until parsed with two properties proven to different degrees. Decompression limits against a zip bomb are real shared and unit-tested via unzipBounded and CARD_ARCHIVE_BOUNDS used by every card container through unzipCard: reject before unzip pre-inflate per entry post-inflate backstop fail closed error wrap detection-only selection and module.risum RPack non-expansion. Path-traversal safety on entry names notes fflate does not sanitize names ingestion never turns a name into a filesystem write the one checked BYAF greeting id path via resolveGreetingPath and two unchecked re-export name carriers for BYAF images and Risu assets. What is not yet proven lists the untested lying-header backstop and the two unguarded export name paths so archive handling must not be called fully sandboxed against hostile archives.

- **H2 Decompression limits against a zip bomb** (`decompression-limits-against-a-zip-bomb`)
  Decompression limits against a zip bomb share one helper. The shared bound is CARD_ARCHIVE_BOUNDS 96 MiB archive 512 entries 64 MiB per entry 96 MiB aggregate used by every card container through unzipCard. Reject before any unzip call checks full compressed length first. Pre-inflate per entry uses fflate filter for total-entry compressed original and aggregate checks with missing originalSize falling back to compressed. Post-inflate as a backstop rechecks actual bytes but cannot undo allocation already done and lacks a lying-header test. Fail closed on any read error rewraps non-limit throws as ArchiveLimitError with Risu detect soft and toCanonical hard while BYAF open returns null. Detection is bounded too via only card.json or manifest.json. The module.risum aside is ordinary ZIP entry then fixed RPack substitution that cannot expand.
  - **H3 The shared bound** (`the-shared-bound`)
    CARD_ARCHIVE_BOUNDS is the budget both charx and byaf unzip under: 96 MiB max archive size 512 total entries 64 MiB max per selected entry compressed or inflated and 96 MiB max aggregate inflated across selected entries. The Risu, BYAF and Lumiverse containers all call unzipCard, which pins bounds CARD_ARCHIVE_BOUNDS and forwards to unzipBounded. Neither format reimplements the check; both call the one helper in core archive.ts.
  - **H3 Reject before any unzip call** (`reject-before-any-unzip-call`)
    The full compressed archive length is checked first against maxArchiveBytes before unzipSync is called at all. An archive over 96 MiB never reaches fflate so hostile bulk payloads die at the outer gate without decompression work.
  - **H3 Pre-inflate, per entry** (`pre-inflate-per-entry`)
    unzipBounded passes fflate an UnzipFileInfo filter that runs during extraction. Inside the callback before inflate: running total-entry count against maxEntries before selection so hostile directory counts still bound; compressed size and declared original size via entryOriginal fallback against maxEntryCompressed and maxEntryOriginal for selected entries; running sum of declared originals against maxAggregateOriginal. only-filter misses skip size budgets but already consumed total-entry budget and never inflate.
  - **H3 Post-inflate, as a backstop** (`post-inflate-as-a-backstop`)
    After unzipSync returns unzipBounded rechecks actual byteLength against maxEntryOriginal and actual aggregate against maxAggregateOriginal and maxEntries to catch understated originalSize. By then inflate already allocated; recheck discards and throws but cannot stop the spike. maxEntryCompressed plus practical DEFLATE ratio bounds the worst case. No streaming mid-decompress reject. No archive.test constructs a lying originalSize so this path is review-proven not test-proven.
  - **H3 Fail closed on any read error** (`fail-closed-on-any-read-error`)
    Anything unzipSync throws that is not already ArchiveLimitError is rewrapped as ArchiveLimitError archive could not be read safely. No partial-success path: fully bounded Record or throw. Risu detect catches everything as not this format while toCanonical lets ArchiveLimitError propagate. BYAF openByaf catches and returns null so bombs surface as generic not a valid byaf Error not ArchiveLimitError. Both fail closed under different error shapes.
  - **H3 Detection is bounded too** (`detection-is-bounded-too`)
    Format detection never inflates a whole archive to check shape. Risu detect calls unzipCard with only card.json so every other entry is skipped before inflation while directory entries still consume total-entry budget and the match passes size checks. BYAF detection reads only manifest.json the same way through readManifestBytes. A large hostile archive being probed cannot force full inflate or unbounded directory scan.
  - **H3 The module.risum aside** (`the-modulerisum-aside`)
    A charx can carry module.risum as one ordinary ZIP entry under the same CARD_ARCHIVE_BOUNDS with no separate inflate step. Extracted bytes run only through RPack byte substitution a fixed 256-entry table one byte at a time into a same-length output buffer not a compression codec. It cannot expand so it adds no zip-bomb surface beyond the entry cap it already sits under.
- **H2 Path-traversal safety on entry names** (`path-traversal-safety-on-entry-names`)
  Path-traversal safety on entry names is proven unevenly. fflate does not sanitize names so unzipBounded can return literal ../ keys within size budgets. Ingestion never turns a name into a filesystem write: entries become original twin base64 maps or basename-only sprite labels with no writeFile using zip names. The one place a name becomes a fresh write and it is checked is BYAF greeting ids through resolveGreetingPath byaf prefix no dots no leading slash no backslash plus knownPaths membership with hostile-id tests. Two places that are not checked the same way are BYAF image row paths returned verbatim into pack entry keys and Risu charx asset filenames re-emitted unmodified. Residual risk is downstream tools unzipping re-exports not same-process zip-slip.
  - **H3 fflate does not sanitize names** (`fflate-does-not-sanitize-names`)
    fflate unzipSync returns each entry name exactly as stored in the ZIP directory. There is no dot-dot backslash or absolute-path filtering in unzipBounded or fflate. A charx or byaf can legally contain ../../../evil.json and unzipBounded returns it under that literal key if size and count budgets pass.
  - **H3 Ingestion never turns a name into a filesystem write** (`ingestion-never-turns-a-name-into-a-filesystem-write`)
    Nothing in core archive Risu or backyard joins an entry name onto a real OS path and writes it. Names are keys into in-memory Records base64-encoded onto original twin assetFiles or BYAF files map or reduced to basename for sprite-pack image checks so a ../ prefix is inert. No Bun.write writeFileSync or fs.writeFile in src takes a zip entry name as target path. Classic zip-slip has no extract-to-disk code path today.
  - **H3 The one place a name becomes a fresh write, and it is checked** (`the-one-place-a-name-becomes-a-fresh-write-and-it-is-checked`)
    Export can write a new file into a re-packed byaf. Alternate greeting ids can name archive-internal scenario paths. resolveGreetingPath is the only function allowed to turn an id into a path: requires byaf prefix non-empty remainder no .. no leading slash no backslash and membership in knownPaths from the twin. Hostile ids cannot mint new paths; freshScenarioPath never derives from user input. Test hostile greeting id is never used as an archive path feeds ../../../evil.json cases and asserts clean scenarios/ outputs.
  - **H3 Two places that are not checked the same way** (`two-places-that-are-not-checked-the-same-way`)
    Two spots carry untrusted archive names into freshly-built output entry tables without resolveGreetingPath or hostile tests. BYAF pathForLabel returns existing image row path verbatim into arc.files then packByaf zips keys unmodified; only fallback filenames are sanitized. Risu charx assetFiles import path is written back unmodified then zipSync. Neither becomes a real filesystem write here; residual risk is a less careful downstream tool unzipping the re-export. Call it verified unguarded and untested not safe by design.
- **H2 What is not yet proven** (`what-is-not-yet-proven`)
  Proven and enforced: pre-inflate archive-size selected-entry size total-entry count and selected aggregate caps in unzipBounded exercised by archive.test.ts; fail-closed wrapping of unzip errors into ArchiveLimitError; only-mode selection plus total-entry cap for cheap detection on hostile archives; and resolveGreetingPath traversal-and-known-path gate on the one BYAF export write path with a hostile-id test. Not yet proven: post-inflate size backstop has no test fabricating understated originalSize; BYAF image-row path and Risu charx asset filename carry no traversal check before export entry names and no hostile tests. Do not describe archive handling as fully sandboxed against a hostile archive or as path traversal solved in general; it is solved and tested for one specific write path.

### Security threat model and defense layers

- id: `reference/security/README`
- path: `docs/reference/security/README.md`
- Bunzai protects the local library, provider credentials, API authority and machine resources across separate trust boundaries. Imported scripts remain data until explicitly invoked through supported execution gates. The overview links each detailed boundary to its authoritative reference rather than repeating changing numeric limits. Archives have selection and decompression checks; regex has conservative validation, construction bounds and isolated imported execution; markup and Lua have separate rendering and capability controls. Local API authority is distinct from opt-in remote access. Provider requests send selected conversation and observations to the configured host, with redirect checks on guarded HTTP paths; external CLIs and connected processes have separate network authority. Damaged vault reads reject instead of becoming empty state, and the keychain diagnostic uses a private invocation namespace. Source tests, native platform evidence and packaged-host evidence are distinguished. Packaged WebView2 isolation, native regex preemption, weaker local key-file protection, missing passphrase UI and unchanged vault cache/concurrency behavior remain explicit limits.

- **H2 Trust boundaries** (`trust-boundaries`)
  Untrusted files, archives, markup, Lua, regex, model responses and remote requests meet different gates. Import and conversion do not execute imported scripts; enabling an imported regex set is an invocation rather than an import effect. The primary Studio listener is loopback, while remote access is an explicit separate authority. Provider-backed turns send selected conversation and observations to the chosen provider. The boundary table directs readers to the owners for canonical conversion and semantic fidelity, archive checks and residuals, conservative regex validation and native-call limits, sanitized rendering, capability-restricted Lua, local Host/Origin/bearer checks, remote join/device controls, encrypted credential storage and per-hop provider egress. This is a navigation overview, not an independently maintained set of detailed limits or a claim that every boundary is proven in a packaged host.
- **H2 Evidence and remaining limits** (`evidence-and-remaining-limits`)
  Code-level tests do not establish real OS-keychain or packaged WebView behavior. The second sandbox origin has HTTP-layer tests but its packaged storage and credentialed API separation remain unmeasured; same-origin fallback is degraded. Native synchronous regex calls cannot be interrupted mid-match, so static refusal and construction bounds reduce risk without proving a deadline. The local identity-file fallback is weaker than OS key storage and there is no settings-driven passphrase unlock. Missing vaults read empty, while other read/envelope/plaintext failures refuse later saves; the narrow damaged-identity recovery preserves originals and reports the incident. Cache lifecycle and cross-process write-conflict safety are unchanged. The keychain probe uses a fresh service and cleans only its own entries, with isolated regressions for credential preservation and failed cleanup. Native-platform proof still requires recorded platform results.

### ReDoS defense in the regex engine

- id: `reference/security/regex-safety`
- path: `docs/reference/security/regex-safety.md`
- Bunzai treats imported regex patterns as untrusted data and combines conservative structural admission with bounded allocation. Native matching is synchronous, so a between-match clock cannot interrupt one RegExp call. The parser and shared AST analyzer refuse unsupported syntax and suspicious or dangerous repetition shapes, including sequential ambiguity, bounded nested loops and caseless alternatives. Source length and capture counts are bounded. Runtime validates the pattern after macro substitution, caps projected replacement and macro output during construction, bounds match enumeration and retained trace spans, and reports refusal without partial output from the failing rule. Studio execution adds a disposable worker with request, batch and output limits plus termination; static Pages refuses previews that cannot provide isolation. Tests cover demonstrated attack families and preserve safe controls. These checks are logical defenses with unit and source evidence, not a complete regex-runtime bound or proof of packaged WebView isolation, browser behavior or native-library security. Direct Kit and lore or Skill callers still lack preemption inside a native match.

- **H2 Structural admission** (`structural-admission`)
  The shared validateRule and vetPattern owners require structurally readable Unicode-compatible syntax, with no heuristic fallback for unsupported patterns. Source patterns and replacements cap at ten thousand characters and captures at 128. The AST screen covers nested variable repetitions, overlapping alternatives, repeated backreferences and sequential overlapping repetition, including bounded variants, single-branch groups, nullable seams and conservative case folding. Execution refuses suspicious and dangerous reports, while mandatory disjoint separators preserve accepted controls. Character-set reasoning remains approximate: acceptance is not proof of linear time, and conservative rejection can include harmless patterns. Complexity scores explain patterns rather than replacing structural admission.
- **H2 Runtime and allocation boundaries** (`runtime-and-allocation-boundaries`)
  Runtime validates the find pattern after macro substitution so variable values cannot bypass admission. Unresolved templates may fail static health checks until runtime context supplies them. Shared regex limits bound text construction and retained changed outputs to one million UTF-16 units, projected macro substitutions, replacement fragments, actual case expansion and prospective full results before concatenation. A limit leaves the failing rule unchanged and explicitly skips later rules, preserving earlier completed work. Enumeration stops at the first requested match or the thousand-match ceiling plus one, and retained capture spans cap at one hundred thousand across a set. Worker single requests cap at two hundred thousand characters, batches at one million input and output characters, five hundred rules and three hundred items. Worker termination supplements these logical allocation limits, which are not measured physical-memory guarantees.
- **H2 Evidence and remaining limits** (`evidence-and-remaining-limits`)
  Tests exercise the demonstrated polynomial pattern, grouped and bounded repetitions, caseless alternatives, unsupported-parser bypasses and macro-substituted patterns, alongside accepted safe controls and built-in templates. Allocation regressions use bounded probes rather than destructive host payloads. Source validators and worker boundaries have test evidence, but this audit does not prove packaged isolation, browser compatibility or complete regex safety. Direct core callers, including Kit draft tools and lore or Skill trigger matching, cannot preempt an individual native match. Rejections appear as traces and plain Health or inspector errors; culprit spans are returned without new highlighting UI.

### Remote access: Tailscale and LAN

- id: `reference/security/remote-access`
- path: `docs/reference/security/remote-access.md`
- Bunzai offers opt-in remote access through a private Tailscale sidecar or an approved LAN session, while its trusted primary listener remains loopback-only. Forwarded traffic crosses a separate untrusted handler with host-only operations refused. Tailscale admission requires the owner identity and the LAN listener requires a random Argon2-hashed connect code plus host approval. LAN session cookies require Secure and HttpOnly, login bodies and code length are bounded, and verification reserves per-IP and global admission before asynchronous work. Reset generations revoke stale credentials, comparisons and issuance. Listener startup is coalesced and invalidated by Off, and the sidecar manager terminates only its retained child rather than sweeping process names. Helper downloads are explicit host-only operations checked against repository-pinned hashes before publication. Existing tests cover authorization and download policy. The October 30 admission, transport and lifecycle changes have isolated handler and lifecycle evidence; older live journeys do not establish fresh browser, real-LAN or packaged security proof for these patches.

- **H2 Containers and SSH forwarding** (`containers-and-ssh-forwarding`)
  Container or headless use does not need a browser inside that environment. The bunzai ui startup receipt prints the local Studio URL, one copyable SSH command for the Studio port, and when the isolated Lua regex listener started a second forward for its random sandbox port. Run it on the browser machine, replace user@server, keep the tunnel open, browse localhost with the printed ui-port. Browsing the server or container IP is refused by the trusted Host gate. HTML may arrive before api/settings is refused, so the browser turns that 403 into localhost and SSH instructions with Studio, Settings Remote access deep link, and one-port recovery copy buttons; host-only API gates still decide whether controls may change anything. One-port is enough for ordinary Studio work; Lua and regex benches need both ports because ADR-009 uses a second ephemeral loopback origin. Tailscale and LAN serve the approved Studio surface only and do not make the sandbox listener a general remote endpoint. This does not broaden hostAllowed, expose a new listener, or weaken token and Origin checks.
- **H2 Two listeners, one trust boundary** (`two-listeners-one-trust-boundary`)
  The app binds two loopback listeners in server-remote and server. Trusted 127.0.0.1 port default 8321 is the local owner's browser, gated by loopback Host Origin check plus a per-launch session token on state-changing requests via checkApiRequest in server-security. Untrusted 127.0.0.1:8788 is the ONLY thing the Tailscale sidecar reverse-proxies already-owner-gated remote traffic to; gated by a per-boot shared secret the sidecar stamps on every forwarded request checkRemoteApiRequest plus the session token on writes. The shared secret is a CSPRNG value generated once per launch in setupRemoteAccess, passed to the sidecar via environment variable never argv because argv is world-readable in the process table. The untrusted listener REQUIRES the secret so a local process or DNS-rebinding page without it fails closed. The trusted listener REFUSES the secret on any path before routing: a request carrying the sidecar secret is by definition sidecar-proxied remote traffic and has no business at the local door in createHandler.
- **H2 The Tailscale owner gate** (`the-tailscale-owner-gate`)
  The sidecar embeds Tailscale via tsnet and for every remote request calls WhoIs on the caller tailnet address, admitting only the studio owner's own account. Identity is compared by numeric user id who.UserProfile.ID equals Self.UserID via resolveOwnerID and newProxy; login-name strings are not used because tsnet can return an empty login for the owner node while the numeric id is populated. Fail closed everywhere: unresolved owner id zero denies everyone; unidentifiable caller with no WhoIs is denied; a caller whose node has no stable id is denied because a device that cannot be tracked or kicked will not be served. Identity headers forwarded upstream X-Tailscale-User and X-Tailscale-Node are taken from WhoIs never from client-supplied headers so a forged header cannot survive. These properties are unit-tested against a fake resolver in sidecar main_test.go.
- **H2 The LAN gate: connect code + per-device approval** (`the-lan-gate-connect-code-per-device-approval`)
  LAN access requires a random Argon2-hashed connect code and separate host approval. Wrong guesses use per-IP exponential backoff; pending sessions expire after fifteen minutes and have per-IP limits. The listener uses self-signed TLS with LAN and loopback subject alternative names, and the owner verifies the fingerprint. Approval cookies require Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite Lax and Path slash: HTTPS-only listening does not stop a plaintext request to another port from exposing an insecure cookie. Login JSON caps at 1024 bytes and code input at 32 characters. Admission reserves one verification per IP and four globally before await, with bounded attempt and session stores. Reset immediately invalidates old credentials and generations; only current comparisons can issue sessions. Disabling invalidates pending startup, and expired sessions are swept before capacity checks. Browser certificate compatibility cannot justify removing Secure.
  - **H3 Session transport and bounded admission** (`session-transport-and-bounded-admission`)
    The session cookie requires Secure and HttpOnly with SameSite Lax and Path slash. A browser that cannot retain a Secure cookie must fail closed. The login route bounds JSON and code length; verification has synchronous per-IP and global admission without an unbounded queue. Attempt and session counts are capped, with expiry swept before admitting a session. Reset generations invalidate old hashes, comparisons and issuance immediately, and disabling LAN also invalidates pending listener startup. These are transport and admission protections rather than a measured browser compatibility guarantee.
- **H2 Host-only surface** (`host-only-surface`)
  Remote and LAN handlers refuse host management: remote-access controls, opening browsers, shutdown, restart and settings writes. Export-folder creation and file uploads are host-only because they write to the host disk; remote clients download locally instead. Bounded exclusive export writes preserve finished artifacts when a request is refused, remove only their own incomplete files and reject cancellation before opening. The same local token and origin checks still govern host requests.
- **H2 Kicking a device** (`kicking-a-device`)
  The owner can remove any connected device from the host. Over Tailscale the app writes a kick command to the sidecar stdin; the sidecar blocklists that node stable id and denies it on the next request even though it is the owner own account via deviceTracker in main.go. On LAN the session is dropped.
- **H2 Where the sidecar binary comes from** (`where-the-sidecar-binary-comes-from`)
  The sidecar is compiled Go and is not inside Bunzai.exe. sidecarCandidates in server-remote checks three locations in order: the repository sidecar folder that bun sidecar/build.ts writes, a sidecar folder beside the running executable, and the aux-package cache where a requested download installs it. None present reports unavailable, its own RemotePhase distinct from off and error, because off is a switch the owner can flip and error invites a retry while a build with no helper can do neither; the panel says so up front and offers no Enable control instead of spawning a path that is not there. LAN mode needs no helper. The beside-the-executable candidate is load-bearing: deriving the path from import.meta.url alone is correct from source and meaningless once compiled, resolving onto the Bun virtual filesystem, so every packaged studio failed to start remote access and surfaced a raw uv_spawn ENOENT. Pinned by server-remote.test.ts.
- **H2 The aux package: fetching the helper on request** (`the-aux-package-fetching-the-helper-on-request`)
  The helper is about 31 MB, so it ships as a release asset the owner fetches with an explicit click. A SHA-256 pin is the integrity control and it is committed in sidecar-pins.ts, pinned like a dependency: scripts/pin-sidecar.ts reads a release's published SHA256SUMS, re-downloads each asset to confirm it matches, and rewrites the module for review. Computing it during the build was the earlier design and it split the product in two, because only a CI-built binary carried a hash, so the download worked for packaged users and silently did not from source. Committing it works because the helper is not tied to an app version: the coupling is to the stdio protocol, not the version number, which is also why there is no staleness signal. Verification runs on bytes in memory before anything reaches the path the spawner reads, and the install renames from a sibling staging file, so no tampered or torn download leaves a runnable binary. HTTPS only with every hop inspected, the size cap applies while reading, and the routes are host-only.
- **H2 Sidecar lifecycle** (`sidecar-lifecycle`)
  The mesh helper uses stdio for status and commands, and its parent-watch exits when stdin closes. The manager terminates only the child handle it launched, never all processes sharing an executable name. It ignores late output from a stopped child. LAN startup is coalesced and checked against a lifecycle generation, so a listener returned after Off is stopped immediately. Windows GUI-subsystem builds and hidden spawn windows avoid console flashes while preserving pipe handles. This audit verifies those ownership rules in isolated tests and source, without rerunning native packaged or real LAN operation.
- **H2 What is proven** (`what-is-proven`)
  Unit tests cover the Tailscale owner gate, LAN code formatting, per-IP throttle, approval limits and expiry, trusted-port secret refusal, host-only routes and unavailable helper behavior. The helper resolver has earlier compiled-path evidence. Download tests cover checksum mismatch, truncation, redirect policy and body limits; pin-format tests reject duplicate digests and constrain assets. Release tests assert workflow shape and platform coverage, while successful cross-builds require a real release. Earlier LAN and Tailscale journeys had live evidence, but the October 30 Secure-cookie, capped login, hash-concurrency, reset-generation and startup-revocation changes have only isolated handler and lifecycle regressions. They have not been reverified with a browser, real LAN or packaged host. This distinction prevents older live evidence from being attributed to the changed authentication implementation.

### Safe rendering: sanitizing card HTML and CSS

- id: `reference/security/safe-rendering`
- path: `docs/reference/security/safe-rendering.md`
- Sanitizer spec of record for card-authored HTML and CSS: complete allow and forbid lists for both places the app turns untrusted markup into DOM. Concepts safe-rendering covers prose UX RenderBox and leaving-gate; this page covers the higher-risk Risu behavior.backgroundHTML and backgroundCSS surface and what is proven only at string level. Where card HTML and CSS enter the app: CharacterBehavior carries them verbatim, Risu maps unchanged, flagged hasExecutableContent until a behavior entity exists; regex replace HTML also reaches only the sealed preview; ordinary prose uses renderMarkup and dangerouslySetInnerHTML without iframe. Two rendering surfaces two boundaries: prose sanitizes with DOMPurify into the document relying on ambient CSP and a strict forbid list; backdrop preview uses narrower DOMPurify then a srcDoc iframe with scoped CSP as the load-bearing egress barrier; both run DOMPurify last and cap input. What DOMPurify allows and strips: ALLOW_DATA_ATTR false, script forbidden, prose forbids media and style form controls; backdrop keeps style and img and leans on CSP; afterSanitizeAttributes on prose only strips fetch attrs, isSafeHref, pins noopener, narrows styles. CSS sanitizeBackdropCss is defense in depth not a parser; Workshop CSS sanitizer is separate and more permissive. Iframe sandbox empty is most restrictive without allow-same-origin; scoped CSP default-src none; ambient app CSP covers prose. What is tested and what is not: jsdom string and HTTP header assertions only; no packaged WebView2 claim of full isolation.

- **H2 Where card HTML and CSS enter the app** (`where-card-html-and-css-enter-the-app`)
  CharacterBehavior carries optional verbatim backgroundHTML and backgroundCSS. The Risu field mapper reads and writes them unchanged with no transform either way. The module header calls this the executable behavior surface, not original-forever, carried on the raw twin and flagged at import via hasExecutableContent until a dedicated behavior entity models it. Nothing in the canonical model parses or interprets this HTML and CSS; it rides as data until a render call touches it. Regex replace effects can produce HTML strings; that output is never rendered inline and reaches the screen only through the same sealed preview. Ordinary prose fields description greeting creator notes are lower risk: markdown or HTML tagged by RenderFormat, rendered through renderMarkup with dangerouslySetInnerHTML, and no iframe between that output and app chrome.
- **H2 Two rendering surfaces, two boundaries** (`two-rendering-surfaces-two-boundaries`)
  Prose fields via render-markup and RenderBox sanitize with DOMPurify and inject into the current document with no iframe; isolation is only the app shell ambient CSP, so the forbid list must block auto-fetch elements outright. The backdrop preview sealed-html-preview wired from workshop code-pane for backgroundHTML and backgroundCSS sanitizes with a narrower DOMPurify config then loads into a srcDoc iframe carrying its own scoped CSP. The component header states sanitizing is not isolation; the iframe plus CSP are the load-bearing egress barrier. Both surfaces run DOMPurify last after any markdown-to-HTML conversion and both cap input before parsing: RENDER_INPUT_CAP 200000 characters, BACKDROP_HTML_CAP 200000, BACKDROP_CSS_CAP 100000.
- **H2 What DOMPurify allows and strips** (`what-dompurify-allows-and-strips`)
  Both configs set ALLOW_DATA_ATTR false and add back only target on anchors. DOMPurify empties script content and strips handlers and javascript or vbscript URIs; both also forbid script. Tested: script, onerror, SVG onload, javascript href and a mutation-XSS payload come out clean. Prose forbids style script iframe object embed form controls link meta base and all auto-fetch media including SVG image, having no CSP of its own. Backdrop forbid list drops the same tags plus frame and frameset but keeps style and img; its scoped CSP carries network egress instead. Permitting style was not enough to keep a design: sanitize returns the parsed body, so a document's head style went out with the head, and every style block is now hoisted first and passed through sanitizeBackdropCss. Prose afterSanitizeAttributes, registered lazily, strips FETCH_ATTRS, drops any href isSafeHref rejects, pins target blank rel noopener noreferrer, and narrows style to six props. buildBackdropSrcDoc does not install that hook; its guarantees come from its own FORBID_TAGS, the CSS sanitizer and the iframe CSP, never from hook order, which is untested.
- **H2 CSS: `sanitizeBackdropCss` is defense in depth, not a parser** (`css-sanitizebackdropcss-is-defense-in-depth-not-a-parser`)
  behavior.backgroundCSS as the css prop distinct from style tags inside backgroundHTML goes through sanitizeBackdropCss. Header states defense in depth only: sealed-preview CSP is the load-bearing egress barrier; this helper must not claim to be a complete CSS parser. Regex passes in order: neutralize close-style breakout, blank residual markup, strip import and font-face blocks, neutralize expression behavior and moz-binding, rewrite non-data url to about:blank, wipe bare http https substrings outside url. All verified at string-output level including four varied close-style breakout payloads. CSS Workshop uses separate sanitizeWorkshopCss not a reuse; it overlaps on import font-face expression behavior moz-binding and non-data url rewrite but omits CLOSE_STYLE neutralization and bare http https strip. Workshop CSP is more permissive img-src data blob https http versus backdrop data blob only. Workshop sanitizes CSS the user types against fixed mock HTML, a lower-stakes boundary not otherwise covered here.
- **H2 The iframe sandbox and the scoped CSP** (`the-iframe-sandbox-and-the-scoped-csp`)
  Backdrop preview uses iframe sandbox empty srcDoc with referrerPolicy no-referrer. Empty sandbox is the most restrictive HTML form: scripts forms popups pointer lock and top navigation disabled; without allow-same-origin the frame has a unique opaque origin not the app origin. srcDoc carries meta Content-Security-Policy SEALED_PREVIEW_CSP: default-src none, images and media data and blob only, script-src none, connect-src none, frame-src none, object-src none, base-uri none, form-action none, style-src unsafe-inline so inline style can apply. Policy is asserted as a literal string; tests confirm generated document has no http https after close-style breakout attempts. Tests do not run a real browser or WebView2; jsdom has no CSP implementation. Surviving claim is generated markup has no literal remote reference and the CSP string is present and well-formed, not cannot exfiltrate. ADR-009 records the same class of gap for Lua sandbox: pack measure BLOCKED; by the same posture this page does not claim full isolation or full sandbox in packaged Windows, only what code enforces and what tests exercise.
- **H2 The ambient app CSP covers the prose-field surface too** (`the-ambient-app-csp-covers-the-prose-field-surface-too`)
  Prose fields have no iframe but are not undefended past DOMPurify. Every HTML response including the shell carries a CSP from htmlSecurityHeaders: default-src self, script-src self plus exact sha256 hashes of served inline scripts computed from the HTML so hashes cannot rot, style-src self unsafe-inline, img-src self data blob, connect-src self, worker-src self blob plus sandbox origin when set, font-src self, frame-ancestors none, base-uri none, form-action none. server applies this to every served index HTML. No unsafe-inline on script-src means even a script past DOMPurify could not run from inline source; connect-src self means a surviving element could not fetch or beacon externally. Asserted against a real HTTP Response header in server.test proving the header is served correctly not that a browser enforces every directive. Prose DOMPurify media forbid is intentional defense in depth with img-src self data blob: either layer alone would stop a remote img; they fail differently by removing the node versus blocking the network request.
- **H2 What is tested, and what is not** (`what-is-tested-and-what-is-not`)
  Prose DOMPurify stripping scripts handlers active tags unsafe hrefs styles and fetch attrs is verified by render-markup.test on jsdom sanitized strings not a real browser or packaged WebView2. Ambient app CSP well-formed and served on every HTML response is verified by server.test real HTTP Response header not live browser enforcement. Backdrop DOMPurify plus CSS regex strip of active tags and known CSS breakout is verified by sealed-html-preview.test jsdom string assertions on srcDoc not CSP network enforcement. Backdrop iframe sandbox empty without allow-same-origin blocking scripts and granting opaque origin is standard HTML sandbox specification not packaged WebView2 measurement for this component; no measurement pass exists unlike the Lua-worker ADR-009 gap it mirrors. Do not read this page as absolute cards cannot smuggle active markup. Read it as two sanitizer passes, ambient CSP, and sandboxed iframe with second CSP each remove a tested payload class with intentional cross-layer cover, and no claim of confirmation against packaged Windows WebView2.

### The Lua script sandbox

- id: `reference/security/script-sandbox`
- path: `docs/reference/security/script-sandbox.md`
- How installed Bunzai runs untrusted card scripts only when the operator deliberately stages them on the Workshop Test Bench, never on import. Static GitHub Pages keeps script text editable but refuses execution before worker creation and omits worker and wasm artifacts because it cannot provide ADR-009's separate loopback origin. Installed execution layers a terminable worker, distinct loopback origin, value-only versioned protocol, and downward-only resource budgets. Worker termination and in-VM functionTimeout form the kill switch; stale runId responses are dropped. The sandbox origin serves only workers and wasm, never api or the session token; missing metadata is a degraded same-origin fallback outside Pages. The value-only wire uses key allowlists and forbidden credential/prototype keys. Lua opens SAFE_LIBS only, strips host-reaching globals, and exposes no DOM, fetch, filesystem, or network. lowLevelAccess is a warning, never a trigger. Packaged WebView2 origin measurements remain incomplete, so full isolation is not claimed.

- **H2 Scripts are data until you stage them** (`scripts-are-data-until-you-stage-them`)
  Card behavior rows are classified, never executed. The classifier only splits a condition or effect into known or advanced data, and import receipts state that scripts are saved as text rather than auto-run. Installed Bunzai can run them only after deliberate staging on the Workshop Test Bench. Static GitHub Pages preserves editing but refuses execution because it has no separate sandbox origin.
- **H2 Worker isolation (the kill switch)** (`worker-isolation-the-kill-switch`)
  Each run happens in its own Worker and the real stop mechanism is termination. runLuaSandboxed is documented never hang never throw: it spawns a module worker, arms a host wall-clock timer, and on completion or deadline calls worker.terminate before resolving. If the deadline fires first the worker is terminated mid-run and the result is timeout. Timeout is enforced twice: inside the VM wasmoon functionTimeout aborts a pure-Lua infinite loop with LuaTimeoutError; outside the host timer terminates the whole worker thread regardless of what Lua is doing. Per ADR-009 a same-origin worker is an availability boundary killable thread not an origin boundary; the termination path holds independently of the origin question. Each run carries a monotonic run id; a response whose runId does not match the run in flight is dropped as stale or spoofed generation.
- **H2 The distinct sandbox origin (ADR-009)** (`the-distinct-sandbox-origin-adr-009`)
  Installed Bunzai serves worker bundles and wasm from a second ephemeral loopback listener, distinct from the UI and api origin. That server allowlists only the Lua worker, regex worker, and glue wasm; it excludes api routes, studio paths, token-bearing HTML, and source maps. The UI receives the origin through vaude-sandbox-origin while the API token stays in vaude-session and trusted apiFetch. readSandboxOrigin accepts only http loopback values. Missing metadata retains a degraded same-origin fallback outside Pages. Static Pages is explicitly different: its runtime marker refuses before worker resolution, and its build omits worker and wasm artifacts. The distinct origin reduces future blast radius but packaged WebView2 measurements remain incomplete.
- **H2 The wire protocol (versioned, value only)** (`the-wire-protocol-versioned-value-only`)
  Only JSON-like values cross the worker boundary. Protocol is a versioned tagged union SANDBOX_PROTOCOL_VERSION 1; wrong version is rejected. Every message is runtime-validated both ways and fails closed on any schema violation via parseRunRequest and parseRunResponse. Three checks: value-only assertJsonLike allows null finite numbers strings booleans arrays and plain objects only and rejects functions symbols bigint undefined non-finite non-plain prototypes and nesting deeper than 32 so no functions DOM handles ports proxies object URLs or app objects ride across; key allowlists per message type via assertAllowlistKeys with state restricted to chatVars chat meta log; credential keys forbidden including token authorization cookie password secret vaude-session bearer and siblings plus assertNoForbiddenKeys against __proto__ constructor prototype. Before post the host runs messageContainsForbiddenKeys as a second pass and refuses to send. API token is never placed on a message and the protocol has no field that could carry it, enforced by construction and unit-tested per ADR-009.
- **H2 Resource budgets (clamped, never upward)** (`resource-budgets-clamped-never-upward`)
  RELEASE_LUA_LIMITS is the authoritative ceiling: wall-clock timeout 5000 ms, Lua heap 64 MiB via setMemoryMax, script source 2 MiB, request and response wire 4 MiB each, aggregate host state 2 MiB with per-field caps under it. Byte caps are UTF-8 via TextEncoder inclusive: exactly maxBytes accepted, maxBytes plus one rejected. Heap is VM-only so separate source wire and host-state caps exist for host JS. Budgets clamp downward only: resolveLuaRunLimits merges overrides under the release ceiling via clampInt so higher requests clamp to max and lower adversarial values pass; missing fields take release defaults. Clamp applies independently at three layers so a hostile postMessage cannot raise a ceiling: host before send, worker re-resolve documented re-clamped, engine again when built. Worker runs Lua on the same authoritative deadline as the host kill switch with no 120s leash above release max. Enforcement at edges: source host-state and request wire asserted before post, re-asserted in worker, response wire before parent accept. Over-budget yields bounded resource result with machine reason and never allocates attacker-sized state first.
- **H2 Lua engine hardening (deny by absence)** (`lua-engine-hardening-deny-by-absence`)
  Lua VM is wasmoon PUC-Lua 5.4 built fresh per run and hardened by opening nothing by default. Created with openStandardLibs false and injectObjects false, then only pure side-effect-free SAFE_LIBS load: base table string math coroutine utf8. io os debug and package are never opened. After safe libs load, base globals that reach the host or load bytecode are deleted: load loadfile dofile loadstring require collectgarbage as STRIPPED_GLOBALS. Memory ceiling uses setMemoryMax with traceAllocations true. Host capabilities are the only things a card can reach, injected as named Lua globals; anything not injected does not exist deny-by-absence. On the Test Bench the injected surface is JSON helpers plus the Risu host API bound to the run state and budgets. There is no DOM fetch filesystem or network global; those were explicitly out of scope for the sandbox per ADR-009.
- **H2 The privileged flag (a warning, not a trigger)** (`the-privileged-flag-a-warning-not-a-trigger`)
  A Risu card can request low-level script API lowLevelAccess. Bunzai surfaces that as a boolean and does nothing else with it. The canonical field is a warning marker for the UI and a sandbox gating input, never an execution trigger. The reader states scripts must never auto-run; the import receipt says deep access is not granted. Surfaced never honored: the flag grants no capability and starts no execution.
- **H2 What is not yet proven** (`what-is-not-yet-proven`)
  No full-isolation claim is made. Proven layers are worker termination plus in-VM timeout, three-layer resource clamps and byte caps, the value-only protocol, absence of token fields, and the sandbox-host allowlist with api and traversal refused. Not yet measured are the packaged WebView2 origin probe, crossOriginIsolated and storage visibility, and a credentialed API fetch from the packaged sandbox worker. The distinct origin is tested at Bun HTTP and handler boundaries only; missing-origin fallback is degraded, while static Pages refuses before execution. ADR-009 therefore keeps packaged-host proof BLOCKED. Token and resource controls remain load-bearing; origin separation is additive defense in depth.

### Secrets, provider credentials, and local API authority

- id: `reference/security/secrets-and-keys`
- path: `docs/reference/security/secrets-and-keys.md`
- Kit seals complete provider configurations and optional Spotify credentials, using the backend recorded in each envelope. Sealing prefers available OS storage, then the weaker local identity-file fallback; the passphrase backend has no automatic settings unlock. Writes verify the seal and atomically rename an owner-only temporary file. Only a missing vault reads empty: read errors, malformed envelopes and malformed decrypted data refuse reads and subsequent saves. Narrow damaged-identity recovery preserves original files and reports the incident; cache and concurrent-write behavior are unchanged. The native keychain diagnostic uses a fresh probe-only namespace and reports cleanup failure, with isolated fake-tool regressions distinct from native platform proof. Guarded HTTP provider paths enforce configured-host redirect checks; external CLIs and MCP processes have separate network authority; model requests transmit selected conversation and observations, while the privacy ledger records usage rather than request contents. Studio Host, Origin, bearer and body gates protect the local API, while ungated token-bearing HTML relies on loopback reachability. Sandbox HTTP evidence does not establish packaged WebView isolation.

- **H2 Kit provider vault (shipping source preview)** (`kit-provider-vault-shipping-source-preview`)
  Kit is a source preview with configured release-binary builds. The vault seals whole provider configurations, active selection and optional Spotify link. Backend-tagged opening prevents fallback to a different lock after decryption fails. Available Windows, macOS and Linux OS storage precedes the weaker identity-file fallback; passphrase setup is not exposed in settings. Saves verify a seal before private atomic replacement. Only ENOENT represents an absent vault; damaged envelopes, unreadable files and malformed decrypted rows refuse later saves instead of losing data. The narrowly defined damaged-identity recovery preserves both originals and reports the incident. Diagnostic keychain operations use their own invocation service and isolated fake-tool tests, which do not establish native-platform results. Provider egress, Spotify, recovery, cache and packaged-host limits are documented separately below.
  - **H3 What the vault seals** (`what-the-vault-seals`)
    The vault file stores a versioned backend-tagged ciphertext envelope under the configured Bunzai home. It encrypts the complete provider document, including keys, base URLs, extra headers, options, labels, active provider selection and optional Spotify link. Tests show provider names and keys are absent from the on-disk plaintext envelope. The decrypted document is cached in process memory after a successful open. Writes create an exclusive owner-only temporary sibling, verify that the chosen backend can reopen the new seal, sync file contents and rename it over the old vault. The containing directory is not fsynced. These operations protect replacement from a partial file write but do not establish cross-process revision-conflict handling for an already cached vault.
  - **H3 Backends and present platform boundary** (`backends-and-present-platform-boundary`)
    Opening follows the recorded backend. Sealing prefers DPAPI, macOS Keychain, Linux Secret Service, then the weaker identity-file fallback; passphrase selection is not automatic. Keychain creation uses a cross-process lock, marker and fresh reads to avoid replacement. Values travel over stdin. Fake-tool tests cover locking and first seals. The native diagnostic uses a fresh service for both instances, locks and cleanup, reports failures and removes its temporary home. Child regressions preserve planted application credentials; native success requires recorded platform results. Holding both local identity file and vault permits decryption. Provider environment variables remain unpersisted alternatives.
  - **H3 Provider egress boundary** (`provider-egress-boundary`)
    Guarded HTTP model discovery and sends enforce configured-host checks; external Claude CLI and MCP network traffic bypass this HTTP guard. Every redirect hop retains the same host, prohibits HTTPS downgrade and obeys the five-hop cap. Credentials authenticate requests but stay outside conversations, tool observations and exports; privacy records reported usage, not all network traffic. Native Ollama metadata has 2 MiB, 512-model, four-worker and eight-second discovery bounds. One uncached resolution owns capabilities and context. Scene body overrides cannot replace num_ctx; malformed replies and unoffered Kit tools fail closed. These boundaries do not prove model quality, memory needs or packaged behavior.
  - **H3 The Spotify link (one more outbound authority)** (`the-spotify-link-one-more-outbound-authority`)
    The Spotify tab adds two outbound hosts, accounts.spotify.com for tokens and api.spotify.com for the Web API, each behind its own guarded fetch that refuses a redirect elsewhere; the page cannot reach Spotify because its policy is connect-src self. The Client ID is the person's own app, there is no client secret, and sign-in is authorization code with PKCE. The redirect is the 127.0.0.1 loopback literal, on a route outside api gated only by a random single-use state. The refresh token is sealed in the key vault and never reaches preferences, logs, exports or model context; the access token is memory only. The routes are host-only. Proof is mocked network, not a live sign-in.
  - **H3 Recovery limitations** (`recovery-limitations`)
    Unknown backends, wrong credentials and authentication failures throw. The sole recovery exception is an identity key structurally damaged and replaced during this open: key and unopenable vault are preserved, with an explanatory notice. Other failures remain untouched. Only ENOENT means absence; other file, envelope and plaintext-shape errors refuse later saves instead of becoming empty data. Legacy notice strings are accepted, but the current notice is process-derived. There is no export, rotation, backend-migration or recovery-key UI. Decrypted caching and cross-process write-conflict behavior are unchanged; parsing protection is not a concurrency guarantee.
- **H2 The loopback server (shipping)** (`the-loopback-server-shipping`)
  Shipping code with automated Bun tests. The default visual app binds 127.0.0.1 only, and uploaded scripts remain data. A random per-launch bearer is injected into index HTML for trusted apiFetch and never handed to the sandbox. The api gate runs before every api handler, checks Host on every method, refuses cross-site GET and HEAD, and requires Origin plus token for POST and PATCH. JavaScript wasm CSS and icons are non-secret ungated assets. Index HTML is also ungated but contains the bearer, so its confidentiality relies on loopback reachability; any client that reaches the listener can read it. Reads remain weaker than mutations. Body caps are 64MB inspect and 32MB JSON.
  - **H3 The per-launch bearer** (`the-per-launch-bearer`)
    A fresh bearer is minted once per launch as randomBytes(32).toString base64url in createSecurityContext marked never log or persist. injectSessionMeta puts it into served index HTML as an escaped meta name vaude-session which trusted apiFetch reads for API authentication. The token is never handed to the sandbox origin. The index document itself is not bearer-gated, so the default listener's loopback bind limits which machines can fetch it; any local client that reaches the listener can read the bearer.
  - **H3 The /api/* gate** (`the-api-gate`)
    Every api route runs through checkApiRequest before its handler. JavaScript wasm CSS and icons are ungated. Index HTML is ungated too but carries the injected bearer, so it relies on the loopback bind. The api gate returns 503 before security context completion, checks Host on every method, and rejects attacker hostnames resolved to loopback. GET and HEAD carry no token and pass only for Sec-Fetch-Site same-origin or none; cross-site, same-site and unknown values are refused, and a Mozilla User-Agent with no header is refused on loopback. POST and PATCH require a loopback-equivalent non-null Origin and a timingSafeEqual token. Other methods return 405.
  - **H3 What the gate does not do** (`what-the-gate-does-not-do`)
    Stated plainly because overclaiming would be a defect. localhost and ::1 on the correct port are accepted not rejected; only wrong port or foreign hostname fails closed. All three loopback spellings resolve to the same bound interface so rejecting localhost would paper-cut bookmarks while protecting nothing; the gate stops attacker hostnames not loopback aliases. GET and HEAD are not token-gated: a header-less non-browser client (curl, CLI, test) with no Sec-Fetch-Site passes the read gate. The read gate is deliberately weaker than the mutation gate. Reads that expose entity data rely on the loopback bind and the foreign-page refusal not on the bearer.
  - **H3 Body caps** (`body-caps`)
    Request bodies stream with a hard byte cap and 413 on overflow: INSPECT_BODY_MAX 64MB JSON_BODY_MAX_BYTES 32MB in core media limits. readBodyCapped checks Content-Length early when present and enforces a running total as it reads so a lying or absent length header still cannot exceed the cap. Same fail-closed posture the upload adapters use against zip bombs.
- **H2 The sandbox origin (shipping code, packaged proof BLOCKED)** (`the-sandbox-origin-shipping-code-packaged-proof-blocked`)
  Untrusted Lua from the Risu Test Bench runs in a Worker from a second loopback listener on an ephemeral port, a distinct origin from UI and api. Defense-in-depth: same-origin Worker is a killable thread not an origin boundary so a future WebView or bundler bug would widen blast radius if same-origin. Fails closed by construction: serves only sandbox worker.js regex-worker.js and glue.wasm; every other path 404 with locked CSP; no api no index HTML no session token no traversal. CORS limited to exact UI origin plus loopback aliases so only trusted UI constructs the cross-origin module Worker. API token is never injected into anything this host serves. What is proven and what is not: allowlist CORS value-only wire API denial and token absence proven at Bun HTTP layer; packaged WebView2 isolation not measured; Plan 017 BLOCKED; do not claim fully isolated; same-origin fallback degraded not claimed isolated.
  - **H3 What is proven, and what is not** (`what-is-proven-and-what-is-not`)
    Scaffolding additive to token Plan 005 and resource quotas Plan 016 not a replacement. Proven: allowlist CORS value-only wire API denial and token absence by Bun HTTP and handler tests. Not measured: packaged WebView2 origin isolation, whether WebView collapses two loopback authorities, crossOriginIsolated and storage separation, and credentialed API fetch from sandbox origin; Plan 017 BLOCKED until a WebView2 smoke matrix records those. Do not call the sandbox fully isolated sandboxed or exploit-proof. Until the matrix is green it is defense-in-depth with tested HTTP behavior and unmeasured in-WebView separation. Sandbox bind failure falls back to same-origin Worker explicitly degraded and not claimed isolated.

### Cards and files as untrusted input

- id: `reference/security/untrusted-content`
- path: `docs/reference/security/untrusted-content.md`
- Layer 1 of the threat model: cards lorebooks regex sets and zip containers charx byaf risum are author-controlled content the user opens never something Bunzai fetches. Guarantee: each file is read by exactly one tolerant parser into the canonical model; nothing on that path evaluates a script; cross-format conversion carries only the portable body so one app scripts never reach another app file. Boundary: this page covers getting content past the door import inspect convert export; deliberate run isolation lives on script-sandbox and running is never automatic. Parsed once into a known type: JSON PNG and zip readers are total functions returning null or empty on bad input never throw or eval; unzipBounded caps archives; risum decode returns null without executing. Two script surfaces neither runs on import: declarative triggers classified as data pure TS runDataTriggers only on Test Bench Run; real Lua opaque text only via runLuaSandboxed on explicit click; privileged lowLevelAccess is warning never trigger with deny-by-absence host. Escrow never blind-copies scripts across formats: only Risu adapter emits body.behavior and export honesty names drops; original twin never crosses. What this page does not claim: regex hang safety sandbox isolation completeness decompression tuning details. Source of truth maps concerns to files.

- **H2 Parsed once, into a known type** (`parsed-once-into-a-known-type`)
  Every import reader is a total function over untrusted bytes: parse into a known shape or fail to a known empty value. None throw on bad input and none evaluate what they read. JSON readers readCardJson readJsonAny readJsonObject wrap JSON.parse in try catch and return null on non-parse or wrong shape; no eval or lenient parser. PNG extractCharacterJson only reads tEXt iTXt after the 8-byte signature; safeExtract catches corrupt chunks and returns empty array; ccv3 preferred over chara so V3 is never silently downgraded. Zip containers charx byaf risum go through unzipBounded checking compressed size per-entry size entry count and aggregate inflated size before and after inflation; card caps 96 MiB archive 512 entries 64 MiB per entry 96 MiB aggregate with ArchiveLimitError on breach not silent truncate. risum openRisumModule decodes into typed records with script bodies as plain strings and returns null on any decode failure never throwing; nothing executes script bodies; raw module bytes kept for lossless export so failed structural decode never loses the source file.
- **H2 Two script surfaces, neither runs on import** (`two-script-surfaces-neither-runs-on-import`)
  Two authored script kinds treated the same at the door: classified as data never evaluated until an operator explicitly stages one. Declarative trigger rows become TriggerScript arrays of event conditions effects; classifyCondition and classifyEffect only sort shapes; nothing executes. The only interpreter runDataTriggers is pure TypeScript comparisons calc arithmetic and setvar with no eval Function or wasmoon; impersonate and command effects are only console text lines. It runs only when the operator clicks Run on the Workshop Test Bench; import convert and export never call it. Real Lua virtualscript triggerlua and risum script bodies ride as opaque text or bytes; CharacterBehavior states converter editor NEVER executes any of this no eval require or DOM injection. Only explicit onRunPackage hands code to runLuaSandboxed; execution never on import never during conversion never without opening Test Bench and clicking Run. lowLevelAccess becomes privileged boolean warning marker never an execution trigger; receipt says deep access not granted; no sandbox file reads privileged and host bridge never implements network filesystem or app access deny-by-absence.
- **H2 Escrow never blind-copies scripts across formats** (`escrow-never-blind-copies-scripts-across-formats`)
  Every canonical entity keeps a lossless twin keyed by source format; cross-format conversion hands the target only the canonical body never that twin. Scripts use two mechanisms. Body carries scripts but only the RisuAI adapter emits them: applyRisuToBody lifts triggerscript virtualscript backgroundHTML CSS defaultVariables and lowLevelAccess onto body.behavior and convertFile hands that body to targets so scripts exist in memory; only risu-fields reads body.behavior while SillyTavern RoleCall Agnai Backyard BYAF Lumiverse Pygmalion never look so triggers virtual script backdrop and privileged flag are never written to their wire. Declared not accidental: only Risu coverage lists behavior with scripts ride as DATA never executed here. Export honesty buildExportHonesty names what will not travel for targets that omit behavior and drops packaged risum module contents; import receipt says scripts saved as text never run unless staged. The raw twin never crosses: original is keyed per source format and a target only reads its own key; on fresh cross-format that key is empty so source raw payload and risum bytes under original.risu never reach the output even if a future adapter read body.behavior.
- **H2 What this page does not claim** (`what-this-page-does-not-claim`)
  Regex execution safety: card-embedded regex is a plain find replace data row never compiled or applied on import; applying is a regex replace never an eval; whether a pattern can hang at runtime is a separate question covered by regex-safety. Sandbox isolation completeness: this page states execution requires explicit operator action and makes no claim about isolation once staged; origin separation is tested at Bun HTTP layer only; packaged WebView2 is not measured and ADR-009 acceptance is BLOCKED Plan 017; do not read this page as fully isolated or cannot be exploited; see script-sandbox. Decompression-bomb tuning: exact archive size caps and rationale live in archive.ts and security README; this page only relies on enforcement before card JSON is read out of a zip.
- **H2 Source of truth** (`source-of-truth`)
  Maps each concern to its authority file. Tolerant JSON is card-io.ts; PNG chunks png.ts; bounded zip archive.ts; risum structural decode open-module and rpack module; trigger classification behavior.ts; TriggerScript CharacterBehavior privileged schema.ts; pure TypeScript trigger interpreter run-data-trigger.ts; Risu import hasExecutableContent isPrivileged module decode index.ts; scripts onto body risu-fields.ts; Risu behavior coverage coverage.ts; only canonical body crosses convert.ts; escrow envelope canonical.ts; export honesty honesty.ts; import receipt receipt.ts; Test Bench run wiring workshop view.tsx; deny-by-absence host bridge risu-api.ts.
